# Metric sign-off matrix

Scope: the **51 paper-core tasks** (`tasks.yaml` tier1 + tier2 + tier3).

Generated from `verification_campaign/verdicts/*.json` by
`verification_campaign/refresh.py`. Do not hand-edit.

## Rules

A metric is signed off only when **both** reviewers return GREEN on the **same**
code state, and its provenance columns are settled. YELLOW blocks: its caveat
must be fixed, not merely documented. A fix made after a sign-off voids that
sign-off.

`n/a` in a provenance column means the reviewer established that no such
artifact exists — no code was released, or the paper publishes no reproducible
number. That is a settled answer, not a gap.

## Findings

### `dynamics_coe`

**codex — YELLOW** · On randomized non-degenerate chains, BLME matched the freshly fetched released formulas with maximum absolute errors 2.13e-14 for CoE-R and 2.05e-14 for CoE-C; on a three-block end-to-end fixture its headline matched the released raw L+1 hidden-state chain with an exact 0.000e+00 difference. It remains YELLOW because a dimensionless anchor whose exact CoE-R/CoE-C is 0.0398345638/0.5398345638 returned NaN/NaN at 5 of 7 finite positive rescalings, while a finite near-parallel chain returned 0.500000000/0.500000000 instead of 3.14e-14/0.493846236.

- GitHub (YES): Alsace08/Chain-of-Embedding — Fetched the current raw score.py and inference.py from Alsace08/Chain-of-Embedding on 2026-09-07 and locally executed a line-for-line transcription on the same randomized chains: maximum absolute errors were 6.66e-16 for normalized magnitude, 2.13e-14 for normalized angle, 2.13e-14 for CoE-R, and 2.05e-14 for CoE-C. inference.py consumes all L+1 raw hidden-state entries; current 100-passage headline/block-output CoE-R pairs are +0.0132015/-0.1830236 (gpt2), +0.1209245/-0.2143480 (distilgpt2), -0.3656390/+0.1361894 (pythia-70m), and -0.1503001/+0.5688235 (pythia-160m), a 4/4 sign flip, while the corrected provenance flags read false/true for headline-is-block-output/hook-fired on all 4 models.
- Paper (NONE): The paper contains 0 absolute raw CoE-R or CoE-C scalar anchors: its reported numbers are downstream AUROC/FPR95/AUPR, for example 64.23/84.47/20.94 for the cited Llama-2 Math row, while BLME emits 0 of those three downstream statistics. Therefore no like-for-like paper number exists even though the released scoring equations can be cross-verified.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Depth is a causal architecture confound: inserting two identity states changes L=2 to L=4 and multiplies both headlines by exactly 0.500000 without changing the represented map, while a d=2 to d=128 isometric zero-padding changes them by exactly 0.000000. The fresh n=4 panel gives rho(R,n_layers)=0.0000, rho(C,n_layers)=-0.4472, and rho(mean_normalized_angle,n_layers)=-0.8944; the older 66-result hash-33743e8 panel gives +0.21999/+0.13650/-0.35632 respectively, so observational signs vary but the exact depth intervention does not.
- DEFECT: CODE/NUMERICS, coe.py:49-93 and :237/:250: the finite scale-free anchor returns NaN/NaN at 1e-30, 1e-25, 1e-13, 1e25, and 2.406159650500376e38 instead of CoE-R/CoE-C=0.0398345638/0.5398345638; only scales 1 and 1e13 survive. The public path's z_mag is 0, 0, 1.118e-13, inf, and inf at the five failures because tensors are cast before ordinary norms and because a dimensionful <=1e-12 guard is applied to a dimensionless ratio estimator.
- DEFECT: CODE/NUMERICS, coe.py:49-62: on unit vectors at angles 0, 0.5e-6, and 1.5e-6 radians, the absolute cosine snap makes both adjacent angles 0 and returns CoE-R/CoE-C=0.500000000/0.500000000; stable evaluation gives 3.1419e-14/0.493846236, and even released acos arithmetic gives -2.4671e-05/0.493845631. Translation is not a theoretical CoE invariance, but as a conditioning probe +1e3 agrees with stable evaluation to 1.9e-10 in CoE-R and 8e-12 in CoE-C, whereas +1e6 returns 0.539834564/0.539834564 instead of 0.039834564/0.532575913.
- DEFECT: CODE/DOCUMENTATION, coe.py:3-5, :170-175, and :243-248: the header and hook prose still say the headline ends at the final transformer block or falls back to a trim, but both live branches use the untouched raw HF tuple and hence the post-final-norm endpoint. This is material wording: raw-tuple versus hooked-block CoE-R is +0.0132015/-0.1830236, +0.1209245/-0.2143480, -0.3656390/+0.1361894, and -0.1503001/+0.5688235 on the 4 fresh models; the emitted flags themselves are now correctly false/true on 4/4.
- DEFECT: CODE/ROBUSTNESS, coe.py:190: tokenization has no model-context cap; 17 tokens sent to a 16-position GPT-2 fixture raise IndexError: index out of range in self. The new try/finally is effective on that path, with registered BLME hook counts 0 before and 0 after the exception.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY, not implementation: inserting two identity states halves CoE-R 0.03983456377 -> 0.01991728188 and CoE-C 0.53983456377 -> 0.26991728188 exactly. No feature-eligible normalized variant exists: _meta_normalized_coe_c is a literal alias of coe_c with maximum absolute difference 0.000e+00, and NORMALIZERS contains 0 dynamics_coe entries.
- DEFECT: PROTOCOL, not implementation: the effective study configuration is num_samples=100 and token_position=last, so each passage contributes 1 prompt position and 0 generated positions; the released inference.py averages each layer over T generated-output positions. The returned note discloses this departure, but the two score distributions are not interchangeable.
- DEFECT: CONFIG/UNCERTAINTY, not implementation: defaults.yaml supplies num_samples=5 and is merged before evaluate, so the code-level get(...,5) fallback never fires; the study override is 100. From the fresh 100-passage runs, naive CoE-R/CoE-C standard errors are 0.00595/0.00568 on gpt2 and 0.02024/0.00698 on pythia-160m; at the package default 5 those same per-passage dispersions imply 0.02663/0.02540 and 0.09051/0.03121.
- DEFECT: FEATURE REDUNDANCY: current outputs satisfy coe_r = _meta_mean_normalized_magnitude - mean_normalized_angle with maximum residual 7.11e-17 on the fresh four models, and pearson(coe_c, coe_r + mean_normalized_angle)=0.99964864. The older 66-result panel gives 0.99996843 (p=4.05e-136) and identity residual 1.42e-14, so retaining coe_r, coe_c, and mean_normalized_angle still supplies nearly two rather than three independent directions despite correctly demoting the exact magnitude and normalized_coe_c aliases.
- DEFECT: PROVENANCE/ARCHIVE: all 66 blme_verified_a envelopes are from hash 33743e8 and retain the old unprefixed alias/schema; current versus archived CoE-R/CoE-C moves by +3.8248%/+0.1161% on gpt2, +4.5152%/+2.1833% on pythia-70m, and -11.4463%/-4.8821% on pythia-160m. The archived signs resemble the raw-tuple values rather than the hooked alternatives, so there is no measured basis for calling all 66 block-output results, but they still require a current-schema rerun before pooling.
- DEFECT: TEST GAP: 26 focused CoE/reference/hook tests and all 20 tests in test_scale_invariance.py pass, but that scale file contains 0 CoE cases; neither the 5/7 finite-scale failures nor the 0.500000000 near-parallel failure is covered. The existing suite therefore gives 46 passes without a check that would fail on either live numerical defect.
- FIX: For GREEN, factor a finite global maximum before every norm, use exact-zero rather than <=1e-12 degeneracy tests, and replace F.normalize plus the absolute cosine snap with scale-safe unit vectors and a stable near-parallel angle such as 2*atan2(||u-v||,||u+v||); fail closed on any non-finite intermediate. Add regressions at 1e-30, 1e-25, 1e-13, 1, 1e13, 1e25, and 2.406159650500376e38, at translations 1e3/1e6, and at 0.5e-6-radian steps while preserving released-code parity on ordinary inputs. Keep the released raw L+1 HF tuple and the now-correct false/true provenance flags, but correct the stale block-output/fallback prose, cap inputs to the resolved model context, raise or qualify the effective n=5 package default, and rerun the 66 old-schema archives. Keep normalized_variant absent until a separately named candidate survives the exact L=2 -> L=4 identity-refinement test; do not silently redefine the faithful raw score.

**opus5 — GREEN** · Both flags now match reality and I checked each against the released reference and against the running code. I have the reference locally (Alsace08/Chain-of-Embedding, eef2d8f) and read inference.py:177-189: `hidden_states = self.sample_info['output']['all_token_hidden_states']`, `layer_num = len(hidden_states[1])`, `for j in range(layer_num)` -- every entry of the raw HF tuple including the final ln_f(b_{L-1}), with no hook and no trim. BLME's headline chain follows that convention, so `_meta_last_entry_is_block_output: False` (coe.py:345) is TRUTHFUL rather than the previous True-because-the-hook-fired. Separately `_meta_blockout_hook_fired` is derived from `_used_hook[0]` and measured True on gpt2 via both the self-collect and shared-cache routes, which is also correct -- the hook does fire, it just does not feed the headline. Running the real evaluate() on gpt2 (defaults num_samples=5, token_position='last'): coe_r = -0.008823179873817488, coe_c = 0.26645554010225947, with the disclosed alternative `_meta_coe_r_blockout` = -0.1951677863858973 -- a sign-consistent reproduction of the file's recorded gpt2 pair and confirmation that the two chains genuinely differ, which is why the flag had to be split.

- GitHub (YES): Alsace08/Chain-of-Embedding — Read the released inference.py directly (lines 177-189) to adjudicate the chain convention the flag describes. It confirms the raw-tuple read with all L+1 entries, which is what BLME's headline implements and what the new `_meta_last_entry_is_block_output: False` declares. I did not execute the reference end-to-end: it is built around a generation loop over its own prompt pools, whereas BLME deliberately uses the prompt's last-token state to stay output-free for base models (disclosed in the task's own `note` field).
- Paper (NONE): The CoE paper reports correlations between CoE and answer correctness on QA benchmarks, not a per-checkpoint CoE scalar for a base model on WikiText, so there is no published number for this configuration. The task's `note` field states the protocol difference (prompt last token rather than a mean over generated tokens) explicitly.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): The chain length equals n_layers exactly (measured 12 on gpt2), so any statistic aggregated over the chain inherits a depth term; the file demotes `_meta_chain_length_per_sample` for precisely this reason. coe_r's random-walk null falls as 1/sqrt(L), so the promoted quantity is depth-sensitive even though it is dimensionless. I did not measure the correlation over a model zoo this round.
- DEFECT: SENSITIVITY DISCLOSED BUT LARGE: the headline flips sign under the alternative chain -- measured coe_r = -0.008823179873817488 (reference chain, shipped) vs `_meta_coe_r_blockout` = -0.1951677863858973 on gpt2, a 22x magnitude difference. The file records sign flips on 3/3 models. Following the released code is the right call and the alternative is disclosed, but a reader must not compare a BLME coe_r against any number computed on a block-output chain.
- DEFECT: PROTOCOL DIFFERENCE FROM THE PAPER: the paper's CoE is defined over the hidden-state trajectory across GENERATED output tokens; BLME uses the prompt's last-token state. This is deliberate and stated in the emitted `note`, but it means the quantity is not the paper's estimand and no published CoE value can validate it.
- DEFECT: CONFIG: num_samples = 5 is small for a per-sample statistic -- measured coe_c std 0.02684409262369027 over 5 samples against a mean of 0.26645554010225947, i.e. a standard error near 0.012 on the headline, about 4.5% of the value. defect_location CONFIG, not CODE.
- DEFECT: `_meta_chain_length_per_sample` = 12 = n_layers exactly: an emitted column that is a pure architecture readout. Correctly `_meta_`-prefixed, noted so it is never promoted.

### `dynamics_fractal`

**codex — GREEN** · Independent equation-level recomputations of pooled R/S H, increment-standard-deviation J, and hit-probability S matched the current outputs with maximum absolute errors 1.11e-16, 1.11e-16, and 5.55e-17; iid truth 0.5 gave H/J/S = 0.527297/0.483121/0.492633 and exact-covariance fGn with H = 0.7 gave H = 0.707072 (+1.010%). Across the required scale ladder 1e-30 through 2.406159650500376e38, maximum H/J/S drift was 0/3.33e-16/0, and translations by 1e3 and 1e6 moved them by at most 1.71e-11/1.99e-10/0; the code also uses the paper-correct next-token targets, whereas the released same-position scoring bug shifted H/J/S by 0.0423823/0.0439460/0.0306024, leaving no implementation-fidelity counterexample.

- GitHub (YES): google-research/google-research — I loaded the locally fetched official notebook at commit 42775283862022b2c6a89f9f0701d75bf4318de5 and executed its estimator cells verbatim. On the same 24 GPT-2 next-token streams, BLME H = 0.579315 versus the notebook's 20-run mean 0.579445 (-0.0225%), BLME J = 0.495167 versus the corrected notebook formula 0.483661 (+2.379%), and BLME S = 0.529814 versus 0.474257 (+11.715%, notebook SD 0.174027); the released accumulating/global-X J routine instead gave 0.318216, and its same-position S routine failed on 1/20 seeds after sampling a zero probability and taking log(0).
- Paper (PARTIAL): The paper's Wikipedia row reports H/J/S = 0.74 +/- 0.01, 0.52 +/- 0.00, and 0.62 +/- 0.02. The current GPT-2 study path gives 0.613959/0.486000/0.569557, respectively 17.033%/6.538%/8.136% low; because that run uses GPT-2, 5 pseudo-documents, 1,024 input tokens, a 128-token burn-in, and epsilon 0.05 instead of PaLM2-L, more than 1,000 real Pile documents, 2,048 tokens, a 100-token trim, and epsilon 0.005, this is only a partial numerical reproduction.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): Across the 4 permitted local checkpoints at 24-25 documents, Spearman rho(H/J/S, unique parameter count) was +0.20/+0.40/+0.40 (p = 0.80/0.60/0.60), rho with d_model was -0.258 for all three (p = 0.742), and rho with n_layers was 0.000/+0.447/+0.447 (p = 1.000/0.553/0.553); vocabulary/tokenizer family gave +0.894 for all three (p = 0.106). Within Pythia alone, 70M to 160M raises d_model/layers from 512/6 to 768/12 and H/J/S from 0.613259/0.561962/0.651627 to 0.626538/0.572626/0.695706, but n = 4 and entangled model quality do not distinguish an architectural proxy from a learned effect, so the confound is SUSPECTED rather than confirmed.
- DEFECT: CODE/PROTOCOL, src/blme/core.py:200-210 and src/blme/tasks/dynamics/fractal.py:276-291: core sizes the corpus in rows while this task interprets num_samples as >=4,096-token documents. The exact current 200-row path returns 5/100 requested documents and 4,475 usable tokens; even all 1,747 usable WikiText validation rows can form only 57 GPT-2 or 58 Pythia pseudo-documents, so num_samples=100 is unreachable on the default corpus. Expanding 200 to 800 rows yields only 24-25 documents and moves H/J/S by up to 0.0346441/0.0989115/0.216435.
- DEFECT: CODE/PROTOCOL, src/blme/tasks/dynamics/fractal.py:276-287: pseudo-document boundaries depend on the model tokenizer. The first 200 rows group as 35/22/40/24/58 under GPT-2 but 35/20/40/24/54 under Pythia; scoring both boundary sets with the unchanged GPT-2 model moves H/J/S from 0.613959/0.486000/0.569557 to 0.630240/0.537393/0.623450, absolute shifts 0.0162805/0.0513925/0.0538929.
- DEFECT: CODE/PROTOCOL, src/blme/tasks/dynamics/fractal.py:288-310: the 5 completed GPT-2 groups consume 20,687 individually tokenized tokens (20,861 after joining) but forward only 5,120, or 24.750%, and retain 4,475 after shifting and burn-in; 21 trailing rows containing another 2,585 tokens are silently discarded. The result is neither the paper's real-document population nor an auditable fixed token sample.
- DEFECT: CODE/PROVENANCE plus CONFIG/PROTOCOL, src/blme/tasks/dynamics/fractal.py:34-37,241-252,345 and src/blme/task_metadata.py:140: the output says protocol='per_document_paper_protocol' and the metadata says the paper protocol is followed, but the measured path has 5 manufactured documents, segment_len=1,024, burn_in=128, and holder_eps=0.05; the paper uses >1,000 real documents, 2,048 tokens, a 100-token trim, and epsilon=0.005. The prior shadowing bug is closed--the merged study config now resolves holder_eps=0.05 and emits _meta_holder_eps_at_or_above_reference=true--but the protocol label remains the opposite of the full truth.
- DEFECT: CODE/PROTOCOL, src/blme/tasks/dynamics/fractal.py:401-404,429-432: at the realized length 895, BLME fits H over [25,32,42,54,71,92,120,156,203,264,343,447] and J/S over [25,31,40,51,66,84,107,137,174,223], not the released notebook's [25,50,75,100,200,300]. On the same 24 GPT-2 streams, substituting the released grid changes H/J/S from 0.579315/0.495167/0.529814 to 0.587498/0.483938/0.503816 (-1.393%/+2.320%/+5.160% for current versus released-grid values); this is a window/protocol defect, not an implementation-verdict defect under the campaign rules.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY: S remains unidentified at this budget despite the corrected effective config. On the expanded four-model panel, epsilon 0.05 to 0.005 changes rank at rho=-0.20 and moves one model by 0.493994; on the 5-document path it moves GPT-2 0.569557 to 0.0437725 (-92.3146%) and Pythia-160M 0.479272 to -0.190975 (shift 0.670246). The machine flag is correct, but downstream feature construction must enforce it.
- DEFECT: TEST/ENVIRONMENT: the focused fractal selection ran 5 checks: 4 passed and the nolds check failed before its assertion because installed nolds 0.6.3 raises TypeError ('nolds.datasets' is not a package) during import. Loading nolds/measures.py directly gives BLME 0.530379 versus nolds 0.497732, absolute difference 0.0326473, which satisfies the test's 0.1 band; however, 0 existing tests exercise _pooled_fractal_params, the 200-row underfill, the false protocol label, or the requested 1e-30-to-2.406e38 ladder.

**opus5 — GREEN** · I fetched the released notebook (google-research/fractals_language/fractal_patterns_in_language.ipynb), transcribed get_hurst_exponent / get_joseph_exponent / get_self_sim_exponent and power_law_fit verbatim, and ran them against BLME's _pooled_fractal_params on the SAME NLL streams (57 documents x 895 tokens, gpt2 and distilgpt2, eps = 5e-2, the notebook's own scales [25,50,75,100,200,300], samples_per_doc = 100, np.random.seed(2024)). Relative agreement: Hurst 0.71% (gpt2: ref 0.604517 vs BLME 0.600229) and 0.54% (distilgpt2: 0.615461 vs 0.612134); Joseph 2.73% and 3.14% (ref 0.498148 / 0.490789 vs BLME 0.511753 / 0.506222); self-similarity 18.0% and 4.37%. The Joseph comparison uses the reference formula with its own bug repaired -- the released get_joseph_exponent never resets its accumulator `v` between scales and reads the global `X` instead of its argument, which as-released gives 0.3267 on distilgpt2 against 0.4908 for the corrected form. BLME implements the correct sigma_tau ~ tau^J and is right to. I also confirmed the harness default: the notebook's DEFAULT_EPS is literally 5e-2, which is what src/blme/tasks/configs/defaults.yaml now carries.

- GitHub (YES): google-research/google-research @ fractals_language (fractal_patterns_in_language.ipynb) — Fetched the notebook (498 KB) from the google-research master branch, extracted its code cells, and ran its three estimators against BLME on identical NLL streams -- numbers above. DEFAULT_EPS = 5e-2 verified in the released source, and its docstring's warning ('If eps is too small, probability estimates will be unreliable') is verbatim. Deliberate BLME deviations, all defensible: BLME uses ALL offsets at each scale rather than 100 random ones per document; BLME's scale grid is 10-12 log-spaced values from 25 to n/4 or n/2 rather than the notebook's fixed six; and BLME fixes the reference's Joseph accumulator bug. Each of those makes BLME's estimator lower-variance, not different in kind, and the measured agreement is <=3.2% on H and J.
- Paper (PARTIAL): Alabdulmohsin, Tran & Dehghani 2024 Table 1 gives, for the Pile's Wikipedia subdomain, S = 0.62 +/- 0.02, H = 0.74 +/- 0.01, J = 0.52 +/- 0.00. BLME on gpt2 over WikiText-103 validation (57 documents, 895 scored tokens each after a 128-token burn-in) gives J = 0.5118 (rel 1.6%, inside nothing but very close), S = 0.5626 (rel 9.3%) and H = 0.6002 (rel 18.9%). The H gap is a PROTOCOL gap, not an implementation gap: the released reference run on my identical streams gives H = 0.6045, i.e. it lands in the same place BLME does, and the paper's documents are >2K tokens (they filter to >2048 and score up to MAX_LENGTH) while gpt2's 1024-token context leaves BLME only 895 points -- R/S is biased low on short series. PARTIAL: J reproduces, S is close, H is systematically short and the reference reproduces the shortfall.
- Confound (NONE): Measured over 13 models (distilgpt2, gpt2, gpt2-medium, gpt2-large, pythia-70m/160m/410m/1b, opt-125m, SmolLM2-135M/360M, Qwen2.5-0.5B, Llama-3.2-1B) at the harness default eps: no |spearman| against d_model, n_layers or vocab_size reaches significance for any of the three pooled exponents (largest is joseph vs n_layers at +0.451, p ~ 0.12). The real instability is the FREE PARAMETER, not architecture -- see defects.
- DEFECT: THE CONFIG OVERRIDE WAS A REAL DEFECT AND ITS REMOVAL IS RIGHT, but the effect is WORSE than the ledger describes: at eps 5e-2 vs 5e-3 the pooled self_similarity_exponent does not merely shrink, it REORDERS models. Over the full validation split (57-58 documents) I measure gpt2 0.562563 -> 0.506790 (-9.9%), distilgpt2 0.428483 -> 0.282207 (-34.1%) and pythia-70m 0.573240 -> 0.750697 (+31.0%). A parameter that moves one model down 34% and another up 31% is not a scale factor.
- DEFECT: At eps = 5e-3 the per-document estimate goes NEGATIVE, which is impossible for a Hoelder exponent (p_eps(tau) is non-increasing in tau, so S >= 0): distilgpt2 self_similarity_exponent_perdoc = -0.015737 at 57 documents and gpt2 = -0.134327 at 15. That is the estimator failing outright, exactly as the notebook's docstring warns, and it is a cleaner proof that 5e-3 is unusable than any percentage.
- DEFECT: THE SOURCE COMMENT'S NUMBERS DO NOT REPRODUCE. The block above `_eps_ok` states, for gpt2, pooled 0.569557 -> 0.043773 (-92.3%), perdoc 0.667857 -> 0.470090 and std 0.668682 -> 0.057779. Reproducing the run those came from (num_samples=100 against a 100-row corpus, i.e. 3 documents), I get pooled 0.670405 -> 0.137456, perdoc 0.560543 -> 0.470090, std 0.179416 -> 0.057779. Two of the six quoted values match EXACTLY (perdoc and std at 5e-3), which pins the corpus, so the other four are from a different code state or a different column. The headline '-92.3% on the pooled exponent' is not what that config produces; -79.5% is.
- DEFECT: CONFIG (the shipped study): dynamics_fractal: {num_samples: 100} against a WikiText prefix builds only THREE 4K-token documents on a solo run, because each document consumes ~30 corpus rows. I measure _meta_n_documents = 3 at pool 100, 15 at pool 500 and 57 at the full 1747-row split, and the pooled S moves 0.6704 / 0.5650 / 0.5626 across those three. The value therefore depends on the shared corpus size that other co-running tasks set -- the same coupling dynamics_interpolation was just fixed for, still live here. num_samples caps DOCUMENTS, not corpus rows, and nothing pins the pool.
- DEFECT: The paper reports the MEDIAN fractal parameter over documents (Table 2 caption); BLME's headline is a single POOLED power-law fit and its secondary is a per-document MEAN. Neither is the paper's estimator, and the pooled/perdoc difference is not small (gpt2 S 0.5626 vs 0.5553 at 57 docs, but 0.6704 vs 0.5605 at 3 docs).
- DEFECT: hurst_exponent_perdoc carries spearman -0.665 with d_model over 13 models while the pooled hurst_exponent carries -0.207. Both are feature-eligible and unprefixed, so the analysis can pick the depth-linked one.
- FIX: Nothing is required for GREEN: the estimators agree with the released reference to 0.54-3.1% on H and J. Config/reporting: (1) replace the unreproducible number block above `_eps_ok` with values a rerun produces (gpt2 at pool 100: pooled 0.670405 -> 0.137456), or state the corpus each was measured on; (2) pin the document pool the way dynamics_interpolation now pins its sample pool -- either request a fixed corpus length or truncate the incoming dataset -- since _meta_n_documents (3 / 15 / 57) moves the headline; (3) raise the effective document count well above 3 for the study run; (4) treat self_similarity_exponent as eps-conditional in the paper text, since 5e-3 vs 5e-2 reorders models rather than rescaling them.

### `dynamics_generation_diversity`

**codex — GREEN** · Li et al.'s collection-level union-of-n-gram-types / total-generated-token statistic matches an independent derivation with maximum absolute error 0.000e+00 over 9,000 randomized checks; ten duplicate [1,2] completions give Distinct-1 = 0.100000000000, not the separately named per-completion mean 1.000000000000. Texygen's leave-one-out method-1 Self-BLEU matches the released expression with maximum absolute error 0.000e+00 over 5,000 randomized hypothesis/reference cases and 1,000 randomized collections, so neither of the manuscript's exactly 2 attributed source metrics has a value-changing counterexample; the measured 1.609437942505 error is confined to the uncited BLME entropy add-on.

- GitHub (YES): https://github.com/geek-ai/Texygen — Fetched the current 81-line master utils/metrics/SelfBleu.py and executed its exact nltk sentence_bleu + SmoothingFunction.method1 expression locally: BLME's primary path differs by at most 0.000e+00 over 5,000 randomized cases and its whole-collection leave-one-out aggregation by 0.000e+00 over 1,000 collections; forcing ImportError through the public BLME entry point exercises the fallback and differs by at most 1.110e-16. The released code defaults to gram=3 and sample_size=500, whereas BLME computes Self-BLEU-4 over the actual 48-completion study collection; those are explicit protocol choices, not kernel mismatches.
- Paper (NONE): Li et al. publish Twitter baseline Distinct-1/2 values 0.023/0.107 and OpenSubtitles values 0.0056/0.0136, while Texygen publishes Self-BLEU-4 values 0.583-0.848; the fresh 4-model actual-study-protocol panel instead spans 0.378146-0.425130 for Distinct-1, 0.731126-0.822917 for Distinct-2, and 0.062176-0.170690 for Self-BLEU-4. There are 0 matched checkpoints and 0 matched generated corpora, so no published value admits a meaningful relative-error reproduction, although the definition-level anchors reproduce at exactly 0.000e+00 error.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): Across 4 fresh local models under the actually executed 8-prompt x 6-completion study protocol, Distinct-1 has Spearman rho = +0.894427 with n_layers (p=0.105573) but only +0.258199 with d_model (p=0.741801), while Self-BLEU has rho = -0.894427 with vocab_size (p=0.105573), +0.774597 with d_model (p=0.225403), and 0.000000 with n_layers. With n=4, p>=0.105573, and the same-text GPT-2/Pythia BPE intervention moving Self-BLEU only -0.587%, these associations do not establish domination by width, depth, or vocabulary, so the confound remains suspected rather than confirmed.

**opus5 — GREEN** · Both paper-defined estimands are exact. Li et al. 2016's COLLECTION-level Distinct-n is what `distinct_1/2/3` compute: ten duplicate [1,2] completions give exactly 0.100000000000 (2 types / 20 tokens) where the per-completion mean -- kept separately under `mean_distinct_n` -- gives 1.000000000000. Zhu et al. 2018's Self-BLEU calls Texygen's own kernel, and I compared it to a LITERAL transcription of Texygen's `calc_bleu` (nltk sentence_bleu, weights (1/max_n,)*max_n, SmoothingFunction().method1) over 300 randomised hypothesis/reference sets: worst absolute difference 0.000e+00. Its aggregation matches Texygen's `get_bleu_parallel` (`other = reference[:index] + reference[index+1:]`) by construction, the NLTK-free fallback matches NLTK to 4.163e-17 over 300 pairs (296 nonzero), and the named anchors are exact -- 0.000000000 on the disjoint [7,8,9,10] vs [[1,2,3,4]] and 0.367879441 on the brevity [1,2,3,4] vs [[1..8]]. I RETRACT the round-4 Opus YELLOW, which rested on an interior-pad misalignment in the BLME-only entropy add-on: I MEASURED its reachability at the shipped configuration and it is zero. gpt2 and distilgpt2 have tokenizer.pad_token_id = None, so `_trim_completion`'s `continue` branch is dead by construction; pythia-70m/160m have pad = 1 != eos = 0, so it is reachable in principle, but over 36 fresh completions x 32 steps at the pinned top_k = 0, T = 0.8 the mean per-step P(token 1) is 0.000e+00, the implied interior-pad rate per completion is 0.000e+00, and 0/36 completions contained one. ADJUDICATION: where the two round-4 reviewers disagreed, my measurement supports gpt-5.6-sol's GREEN -- but for a stronger reason than it gave: not only is the defect outside the paper-defined estimands, its firing probability at the shipped protocol measures 0.000e+00.

- GitHub (YES): geek-ai/Texygen (utils/metrics/SelfBleu.py) — Fetched utils/metrics/SelfBleu.py from geek-ai/Texygen master and read `calc_bleu` and `get_bleu_parallel` directly. `calc_bleu` is `nltk.translate.bleu_score.sentence_bleu(reference, hypothesis, weight, smoothing_function=SmoothingFunction().method1)` with `weight = tuple(1./ngram for _ in range(ngram))`; `get_bleu_parallel` scores each sentence against `reference[:index] + reference[index+1:]`. BLME's `_self_bleu_single` makes the identical NLTK call and its evaluate() uses the identical leave-one-out aggregation, and running Texygen's exact expression against BLME's over 300 randomised cases gives worst absolute difference 0.000e+00. Two disclosed divergences, both properties of the protocol rather than the kernel: Texygen's SelfBleu defaults to gram=3 and sample_size=500 where BLME uses max_n=4 over the whole collection, and Texygen tokenises with nltk.word_tokenize where BLME uses subword ids. Li et al. 2016 released no code for Distinct-n.
- Paper (NONE): Neither source publishes a number this task could reproduce. Li et al. 2016 report distinct-1/2 for their own seq2seq/MMI systems on Twitter and OpenSubtitles dialogue (0.023/0.107 and 0.0056/0.0136); Zhu et al. 2018 report Self-BLEU-2..5 for GAN text generators on COCO Captions and EMNLP News (0.58-0.85). Both are model-, corpus- and decoding-specific, and neither involves a pretrained LM on eight fixed prompts. The DEFINITIONAL anchors do reproduce exactly: collection Distinct-1 = 0.100000000000 on the ten-duplicate collection, Self-BLEU = 0.000000000 on the disjoint anchor and 0.367879441 on the brevity anchor, and 0.000e+00 against the released Texygen expression.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): The tokenizer channel is the plausible one and it is a PROTOCOL dependence rather than a size proxy: the same generated text scored under a different BPE moves Self-BLEU and Distinct-n, which is why the docstring restricts comparison to models sharing a tokenizer. Vocabulary saturation is not a mechanism here -- Distinct-1's denominator is ~1,150 generated tokens against a 50,257-entry vocabulary, so it is nowhere near a V/N ceiling -- and with top_k pinned to 0 the entropy family is not capped by a filter width (the processed scores are finite on the full vocabulary). I could not rule out a parameter-count association: generation at this cost is only affordable on three or four small models locally, and n = 4 cannot separate a capability effect from a size effect. Test on the full zoo before treating these columns as size-free.
- DEFECT: CODE (LATENT, measured unreachable at the shipped config) generation_diversity.py:336-342: the entropy quarters are cut as `per_token_H[row_idx, :len(comp)]`, but `_trim_completion` DROPS pad tokens from the middle of a row, so when an interior pad exists the retained score rows are the first k RAW steps rather than the k VALID ones. Constructed row [5,1,7,8,9] with pad=1, eos=0 and per-step entropies [1,2,3,4,100]: BLME's last-quarter mean is 4.0 where the correct value at the surviving raw positions is 100.0 (abs err 96.0). Reachability measured: pad_token_id is None on gpt2/distilgpt2 (branch dead) and on pythia-70m the mean per-step P(pad id 1) is 0.000e+00 with 0/36 completions affected, so no shipped number is wrong today. The fix is to return the retained RAW POSITIONS from _trim_completion and index per_token_H with them.
- DEFECT: DOCUMENTATION generation_diversity.py:~379-390: the stale 'top-50, T = 0.8 / 99.90% of the vocabulary is -inf / support is exactly 50 / 2.21 nats / capped at log(50) = 3.91' paragraph is still physically present below its own STALE-NUMBER CORRECTION. The correction is right and is read first, but leaving the superseded figures in place invites a reader to quote them.
- DEFECT: DOCUMENTATION task_metadata.py:118 still says 'distinct-n == Li formula (per-completion scope flagged in output)'. The headline `distinct_n` keys are now the COLLECTION-level ratio, with the per-completion form under `mean_distinct_n`; the metadata describes the older convention.
- DEFECT: HYGIENE generation_diversity.py:~404-406: `completion_filter`, `distinct_n_scope` and `self_bleu_scope` are STRING values emitted without a `_meta_` prefix, and `seed` can be None, so non-numeric entries sit in the result dict alongside the features.

### `dynamics_gradient_flow`

**codex — GREEN** · Pascanu et al. Equations 3-7 define a loss-state error signal transported through a Jacobian chain, and BLME's disclosed depth-for-time analogue computes ||dL_CE/dh_l||_F faithfully: 144/144 current live-model layer gradients were nonzero, the public hook norms agreed with an independent autograd route to at most 1.236034e-06 relative, and the five profile summaries agreed to at most 6.229161e-07 absolute. A six-block linear anchor returned slopes +3.465735902800/-3.465735902800 and endpoint log-ratios -3.465735902800/+3.465735902800 for gains 0.5/2.0 (maximum analytic error 1.332268e-15); a nonuniform 8:4:2:1 profile stayed finite from 1e-30 through 2.406159650500376e38 with maximum summary drift 6.705747e-14, and function-preserving translations of 1e3/1e6 changed every output by 0.0.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): https://github.com/pascanur/trainingRNNs @ 3f5df3ffddccc42059cf9599dc75389f116aff85 — I re-inspected the clean locally fetched 507-line RNN.py at commit 3f5df3ffddccc42059cf9599dc75389f116aff85: it computes gH/g_on_H and one whole-parameter norm_theta but emits 0 of BLME's 5 profile-summary families, so no same-input scalar exists for head-to-head parity. The released program could not be run in either available interpreter (2/2): Python 2 raises ImportError for Theano and Python 3 reports no Theano module, making the repository evidence PARTIAL rather than YES.
- Paper (NONE): The paper provides 0 numerical values for BLME's per-depth norms, entropy, normalized-depth slope, below-10%-of-max fraction, or endpoint log-ratio; its Table 2 value 1.50 bits/character is a different observable. Equation 7's contraction law is reproducible and BLME matched the +/-3.465735902800 anchor to 1.332268e-15 absolute, but an equation-derived anchor is not a published figure/table value for this metric, so paper_number remains NONE.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): On the current path, corr(log gradient norm, -log hidden norm) is +0.902038/+0.949147/+0.952957/+0.941640 and layer 0 carries 0.545305/0.653999/0.747285/0.642752 of gradient mass for gpt2/distilgpt2/pythia-70m/pythia-160m. On the current 20-passage four-model panel, entropy has r=+0.909052 with log depth, slope has r=+0.943883 with log width, gradient_norm_mean has r=-0.954694 with log width, and its range is 22.710062x; together with the exact-logit c=16 intervention, hidden-coordinate, depth, and width confounding are confirmed.

Eq. (7)'s exponential-contraction closed form exactly: a matched six-block chain gives slope +3.465735903 at a=0.5 and -3.465735903 at a=2 against the analytic -(L-1)ln a = +/-3.4657359027997265 (rel 8.9e-16), with the corrected sign convention (contractive => POSITIVE slope) now matching the docstring. Round-3's RED (underflow) is FIXED and re-measured: a 4-block end-to-end model whose stored gradients sit at 2.6e-24 now reports 2.613178888e-24 per layer against an independent float64 anchor of 2.613178846e-24 (rel 1.62e-08) with entropy exactly ln4 = 1.386294361120, where the naive torch .norm() returns 0.0; at the high end a 6-block model reports 2.614203530e+15 against 2.614203439e+15 where naive .norm() returns inf.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): pascanur/trainingRNNs — Cloned the authors' released repo (RNN.py, 507 lines, Theano). It computes ONE whole-parameter clipping norm (norm_theta = sqrt(sum gW_hh^2 + ...), RNN.py:219) and the Eq. (9) regulariser as a ratio of successive squared activation gradients (tmp_x/tmp_y, RNN.py:205-207) -- it emits ZERO of BLME's five profile summaries (entropy, normalized-depth slope, below-10%-of-max fraction, endpoint log ratio, norm mean). - Paper (PARTIAL): Pascanu et al. 2013 publishes no numeric value for a per-depth gradient-norm profile summary; its reported results (e.g. PTB 1.50 bits/char) are a different observable. What IS reproducible is Eq. (7)'s eta^(t-k) contraction bound, and BLME matches its closed form to 8.9e-16: a = 0.5 gives slope +3.465735903 and log-ratio -3.465735903, a = 2 gives -3.465735903 and +3.465735903, against the analytic +/-(L-1)ln a = +/-3.4657359027997265.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): gradient_flow_entropy is bounded above by ln(n_layers) by construction, and on the four locally-run models r(H, log n_layers) = +0.710865 and r(H, log d_model) = +0.658304, r(slope, log d_model) = +0.782070, r(gradient_norm_mean, log d_model) = -0.881396 (n = 4, so these are indicative, not decisive). The mechanistic confound is stronger than the correlations: corr(log gradient norm, -log hidden norm) = +0.902 to +0.969 across the four models, i.e. the profile is measuring residual-stream norm growth -- an architecture/normalisation property -- rather than gradient health, and layer 0 supplies 54.5%-73.5% of the mass on every model.

(1) Correct the two wrong comments at gradient_flow.py:157-158 and ~172 (exp(H)/L is not H/log L: 0.631548569 vs 0.581671866 for p = (0.8, 0.1, 0.1); it is an exponential-entropy effective count, not the inverse-Simpson participation ratio 0.505050505). (2) Expose the hard-coded max_length = 64 in defaults.yaml. (3) Raise num_samples from 4 or emit a bootstrap sd -- gradient_norm_max varied 1.965x across 8 disjoint four-passage groups. (4) Add regression assertions for the a = 0.5/a = 2 sign convention (+/-3.465735903), the endpoint log ratio, and the 2.6e-24 / 2.6e+15 extreme-scale norms so the round-4 safe_norm fix is pinned. (5) Disclose in the write-up that the profile tracks -log||h_l|| at r = +0.90 to +0.97 and is not invariant to a function-preserving rescaling.

### `dynamics_interpolation`

**codex — RED** · White/Shoemake SLERP, and the task's subsequent radial projection, are positively homogeneous; on ordinary inputs the current `_slerp` agreed with the released formula over 2,500 fresh points to maximum relative-sup error 4.392e-15. But a same-function transform h->c*h, W->W/c preserved endpoint logits within 1.073e-7 while the required gap 0.131697065 became 0.0 at c=1e-25, 5.960e-8 at c=1e-13, and NaN at c=1e25; separately, the omitted native Gemma-2 softcap changed the current/native gap from 0.262955323 to 0.478187433 on a valid finite fixture.

The match is not global: BLME differed from the released function by relative L2 0.510903 at scale 1e-13 and 0.204936 at 1e25, and the repository contains 0 next-token-entropy or convexity-gap outputs, so only the sourced kernel has a released counterpart.

For radius-10 orthogonal endpoints BLME's radial projection changes the paper's raw midpoint norm 7.071067812 to 10.000000000 (+41.421%), and the paper publishes 0 entropy-gap values, hence only partial component reproduction.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): A function-preserving affine coordinate change confirms an origin/head-parameterization confound: translations 1e3 and 1e6, with compensating LM-head biases and endpoint-logit errors only 1.819e-13 and 1.863e-10, both changed convexity_gap by +33.78% (0.131696999->0.176182449). In contrast, direct width 4->128 and tuple-depth 2->25 controls changed 0.0, the orthogonal control changed at most 6.311e-8, and the four-model size correlations were rho=+0.7746/+0.4472/-0.8944 for width/depth/vocabulary with all p>=0.1056, so the confirmed mechanism is coordinate origin rather than a demonstrated raw width or depth multiplier.

### `dynamics_perturbation_growth`

**codex — RED** · Li et al. Eq. 9 is homogeneous of degree zero: for the finite one-coordinate trajectory delta_l = s * 0.5^l over 5 transitions, the exact rate is -0.693147180560 for every positive s. The current _growth_profile returns that value at 1e-13, 1, and 1e13 but returns no metric at 1e-30, 1e-25, 1e25, and 2.406159650500376e38; independently, a common +1e6 coordinate translation moves an exact 0.139761942549 rate to 0.110812307057 (-20.7135%), so a concrete value-changing implementation counterexample remains.

- GitHub (NONE):  — Fresh inspection of the 17-page arXiv v1 paper found 0 matches for github, http, code, or repository, and 4 targeted GitHub searches by exact title, arXiv ID, authors, and QLE terminology found 0 author implementations; the only repository result was a 2026 third-party prior-art citation. Thus 0 released reference implementations were available to fetch or cross-run.
- Paper (NONE): Figure 6 identifies 4 perturbed layers (10, 20, 30, and 39), 2 coordinates (4074 and 3500), and perturbations labelled 0.01 and 1%, but publishes 0 scalar or tabulated QLE values that define a reproducible numeric target. A fresh 15-case Eq. 9 gain/depth sweep matched ln|a| to maximum absolute error 1.4872e-6 at ordinary scale, but that is a formula check rather than reproduction of a published number.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Fresh fp32 rates were 0.436656628 (distilgpt2, 6L), 0.525536054 (pythia-70m, 6L), 0.233391509 (gpt2, 12L), and 0.275772470 (pythia-160m, 12L), giving rho -0.894427 with depth and -0.774597 with width. More decisively, the c=2 residual/epsilon gauge preserved logits with maximum absolute difference 0.0 and argmax agreement 1.0 while changing the normalized rate +24.7491%; initial_divergence also spanned 0.006582052-0.033026908 (5.0177x) with vocab_size rho -0.894427 and Pearson r -0.998737.
- DEFECT: CODE/numerics, perturbation_growth.py:48-52: the ordinary row norm underflows or overflows although every supplied trajectory entry is finite. For delta_l = s * 0.5^l, all 6 layers contain 8 nonzero finite rows and the exact rate is -0.693147180560 at all 7 requested scales; BLME returns no profile at s = 1e-30, 1e-25, 1e25, and 2.406159650500376e38, versus the correct rate at 1e-13, 1, and 1e13.
- DEFECT: CODE/translation precision, perturbation_growth.py:48: each operand is forced through .float() before subtraction. On a fixed gain-1.15 trajectory with delta_0 = 0.01, the independent rate is 0.139761942375 and BLME gives 0.139761878023 unshifted; adding the same 1e3 vector to both trajectories gives 0.139796043342 (+0.0244%), and adding 1e6 gives 0.110812307057 instead of 0.139761942549 (-20.7135%).
- DEFECT: CODE/perturbation construction, perturbation_growth.py:155-160: rmax * norm is evaluated before multiplying by rel_eps. For a finite 16-coordinate row at 2.406159650500376e38, the requested 1% perturbation norm is the representable 9.62463845e36, but the intermediate true norm 9.62463845e38 overflows, noise scale becomes inf, and an identity-stack evaluation returns 'Degenerate divergence profile' instead of growth rate 0.0.
- DEFECT: PORTABILITY/COMPARABILITY: identical GPT-2 weights give growth_log_ratio 2.800698110 / 4.584853453 / 3.078507595 in fp32 / bf16 / fp16, a 63.7040% maximum shift, while _meta_realized_rel_eps is 0.0099999994 / 0.0101200352 / 0.0100024908 and passes the 5% warning in all 3 cases; the output records no effective parameter dtype.
- DEFECT: NORMALIZATION/ARCHITECTURE: growth_rate_per_block fails a same-function gauge test. Scaling GPT-2's final raw residual by c=2 and multiplying ln_f epsilon by 4 leaves logits exactly unchanged (max absolute difference 0.0; exact-element and argmax agreement 1.0) but moves the rate 0.233391509 -> 0.291153774 (+24.7491%); its fresh 4-model rho remains -0.894427 with n_layers.
- DEFECT: FEATURE CONFOUND/CODE: is_feature returns True for initial_divergence, rel_eps, and n_samples_used. Across the 4 fresh models initial_divergence spans 0.006582052-0.033026908 (5.0177x) with vocab_size rho -0.894427 and Pearson r -0.998737, while rel_eps = 0.01 and n_samples_used = 10 are exact constants in 4/4 runs.
- DEFECT: UNCERTAINTY/PROTOCOL: the resolved package and study configurations both use exactly 10 passages, while the result contains 0 std, SEM, confidence, or per-sample dispersion fields; the observed 4-model differences therefore cannot be separated from corpus-sampling variation from the result envelope alone.
- DEFECT: PROTOCOL (correctly disclosed, not used for verdict): Li et al. perturb one selected coordinate at intermediate layers 10/20/30/39, whereas BLME uses a seeded isotropic perturbation of every input-token embedding. For A = diag(1,100) and X = (1,0), Eq. 10's proportional direction gives rate 0.0 while BLME's seed-42 Gaussian direction gives 3.576384256, so the proxy-only provenance label must remain.
- DEFECT: TEST GAP: the 2 selected PerturbationGrowth unit tests pass, but neither sweeps scale nor translation; the current kernel still fails 4/7 mandated scales and shifts -20.7135% at translation 1e6. The hook itself is now correct: GPT-2 and Pythia raw final-block outputs reconstruct hidden_states[-1] through final norm with max absolute error 0.0, and hook counts remain 0 before and after early-return, injected-exception, and successful paths.
- FIX: For implementation fidelity, preserve source precision until after subtraction and compute every row norm with a max-factored wide accumulator, e.g. m * ||delta/m||, so the ratio remains finite at all 7 scales; likewise evaluate rel_eps inside the normalized factor (rmax * (unit_norm * rel_eps)) or in a wide accumulator, and compute _meta_realized_rel_eps with the same stable norm. Add exact regressions for the 7-scale gain-0.5 trajectory, +1e3/+1e6 common translations, and the 2.406159650500376e38 identity stack. Separately, either run the two forwards in fp32 or emit _meta_param_dtype and reject cross-dtype pooling; demote initial_divergence, rel_eps, and n_samples_used to _meta_ keys, emit per-sample dispersion, and retain the explicit proxy-only protocol label.

**opus5 — GREEN** · THE HOOK LEAK IS FIXED AND I VERIFIED IT THE WAY I FOUND IT. The collection loop is now wrapped in try/finally with `_hook.remove()` in the finally block. Patching model.forward to raise on the third call and counting BLME-registered hooks on get_layers(model): gpt2 goes [0]*12 -> [0]*12 and layers[-1]._forward_hooks is {} after the raise; pythia-70m goes [0]*6 -> [0]*6. Previously this was 0 -> 1 and stayed. The metric itself is unchanged and self-consistent: `_meta_last_entry_is_block_output` = True on all 10 models I ran and `_meta_realized_rel_eps` = 0.01 exactly at the study's rel_eps=0.01.

- GitHub (NONE):  — No code was released with Li et al. 2025 (arXiv:2503.13530) or with the Fernando & Guitchounts residual-stream line; the task is BLME's own clean-room formulation of a quasi-Lyapunov exponent and is documented as such. Nothing to fetch.
- Paper (NONE): Li et al. publish a quasi-Lyapunov analysis of reasoning traces, not a per-layer divergence-growth number for a base LM at a stated perturbation size, so there is no published value this can reproduce. The module's own analytic anchor does hold (a linear stack h_{l+1} = a h_l gives growth_log_ratio = (L-1) ln|a| exactly).
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Over 10 models at the study config: rho(growth_log_ratio, n_layers) = +0.673 -- it accumulates one term per block traversed, so a deeper model scores higher for being deeper. The depth-normalized twin OVER-corrects and flips the sign: rho(growth_rate_per_block, n_layers) = -0.648, and rho(., d_model) = -0.470. `initial_divergence` is a vocab/embedding-scale proxy at rho = -0.826 with vocab_size (range 0.00443-0.03303) and is emitted unprefixed.
- DEFECT: dtype: growth_log_ratio 2.800723 / 4.642212 / 3.102867 on the same gpt2 weights in fp32 / bf16 / fp16 (65.7% spread), with the realized perturbation identical to within 1.2%. `_meta_realized_rel_eps` passes on all three, so the guard that exists does not detect the larger of the two dtype problems, and nothing in the output records the storage dtype.
- DEFECT: growth_rate_per_block is a WORSE depth proxy than the column it normalizes, with the sign flipped: rho -0.648 vs +0.673 with n_layers over 10 models. Dividing by n_transitions assumes the log-divergence is proportional to depth; measured, the two are anti-correlated after division.
- DEFECT: initial_divergence carries rho -0.826 with vocab_size over 10 models and is feature-eligible and unprefixed. It is rel_eps times the mean embedding row norm, i.e. an embedding-scale readout, not a dynamical property.
- DEFECT: NO UNCERTAINTY IS EMITTED AT ALL at num_samples=10 -- no std, no SEM, no per-sample spread -- so the reader cannot tell whether a between-model difference exceeds sampling noise. The task emits n_samples_used and nothing else about dispersion.
- DEFECT: The default fallback dataset ('Perturbation growth probe sentence {i}.') is a set of near-identical short sentences; a run that reaches it produces a number on a degenerate corpus with nothing in the output to say so.
- DEFECT: SCOPING CHECK ON THE NEW TEST (asked, and it holds): `tests/tasks/test_forward_hook_leaks.py::_is_blme_hook` excludes only hooks whose __module__ or __code__.co_filename is outside blme. I confirmed the upstream leak it is scoped around is real and NOT Llama-specific -- on pythia-70m (GPTNeoX) a raised forward leaks `transformers.utils.output_capturing.install_output_capuring_hook.<locals>.output_capturing_hook` on all 6 blocks -- and that the scoping does NOT hide a BLME leak: the same helper correctly CAUGHT geometry_matrix_entropy's own lambda leaking on layers[-1] in the same run. The scoping is honest. What is not honest is the test's docstring claim that it 'covers every task that hooks a module': it is parametrized over 2 of the ~25 hook sites in src/blme.
- FIX: Compute the clean and perturbed forwards in fp32 (promote-and-restore, as repe_steering_effectiveness already does), or emit a `_meta_param_dtype` flag and refuse cross-dtype comparison -- the same gpt2 weights currently read 2.800723 / 4.642212 / 3.102867. Prefix `initial_divergence` with _meta_ (rho -0.826 with vocab_size). Emit a per-sample std/SEM for growth_log_ratio. Either report the profile as a shape (log d_l against normalized depth) rather than as a level, or state that growth_rate_per_block is anti-correlated with depth (rho -0.648) so it is not a depth-neutral twin. Extend the parametrization of test_forward_hook_leaks.py to every task that registers a hook, or narrow its docstring.

### `dynamics_sharpness`

**codex — GREEN** · All four live kernels agree with independent definitions or released code: on a 51-parameter float64 MLP the signed power iteration returned 2.0562113169633247 versus exact eigendecomposition 2.056211316963325 (2.16e-16 relative), and a same-vector HVP matched PyHessian with maximum absolute delta 0.0 and vHv 0.22298727374960844 in both. Exhaustive Hutchinson over all 16 Rademacher probes gave 4.75 versus exact trace 4.75, p=2 SAM gave 0.504999999999999 versus 0.505 (1.98e-15 relative), and 40-bisection critical sharpness had worst relative error 4.55e-13 over lambdas 0.25, 4, 37.5, 100, 1e4, and 4e6.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): amirgholami/PyHessian; google-research/sam; facebookresearch/scalable-curvature — I found all 3 official repositories and executed the locally installed official PyHessian 0.1 (hessian.py SHA256 f465fa75a657fd7d5553a16e6712190f64d64e4e7a44c318e7f0c41561acf3e9): the same-vector HVP had maximum absolute delta 0.0 and vHv 0.22298727374960844 in both, while BLME/PyHessian eigenvalues were 2.0562113169633247/2.056207196430183 (2.004e-6 relative). Fresh local clones of PyHessian, google-research/sam, and scalable-curvature each failed with exit 128 because outbound git was blocked, so the latter 2 were source-inspected and checked only through local analytic anchors (SAM 0.504999999999999 versus 0.505; critical 0.4999999999997726 versus 0.5), making PARTIAL rather than YES the evidence-complete grade.
- Paper (NONE): The 3 defining sources publish 0 like-for-like scalar for BLME's language-model checkpoint, final-block scope, corpus batch, and loss. The measured 2.0562113169633247 eigenvalue, 4.75 exhaustive trace, 0.504999999999999 SAM delta, and 0.4999999999997726 critical sharpness are analytic or released-code anchors, not reproductions of a paper table or figure.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): With the represented function and loss fixed exactly, the (a,b) scale sweep 1->100 moved trace/eigenvalue/SAM/critical sharpness by 5000.00005x/3333.33347x/234.742286x/9999.9999x and gave Spearman rho=+1.0 for each. Independently, adding 4095 inactive coordinates kept trace=1.0 but divided the per-parameter variant by exactly 4096 (rho=-1.0), directly confirming parameterization and parameter-count confounding rather than merely observing a fitted correlation.

- DEFECT: CODE/search range, src/blme/tasks/dynamics/sharpness.py:228-313: on scalar quadratics at the requested scales 1e-30/1e-25/1e-13/1/1e13/1e25/2.406159650500376e38, safe_norm equaled the stored curvature and power iteration had ratio 1.0 at all 7 scales, while SAM stayed finite with worst relative error 1.96e-6. Critical-sharpness ratios were 0.992248/0.992248/0.992248/0.996492/0.998830/NaN/NaN: the hard 30-step downward search cannot reach the finite eta*=2/lambda boundaries at the last 2 scales.
- DEFECT: CODE/state restoration, src/blme/tasks/dynamics/sharpness.py:401-402,525-529: only the root training flag is saved. The 1e6 case reflects quantization inside the supplied loss, which a generic task-side Hessian wrapper cannot undo, but the translation sensitivity must be disclosed.

**opus5 — GREEN** · All four probes are faithful on their paper-defined domain, and the ONE defect that held my round-4 verdict at YELLOW is verified FIXED on this code state. Power iteration returns the exact largest-MAGNITUDE eigenvalue with its sign on six analytic quadratics (2.0 / -5.0 / 0.25 / 4.0 / 37.5 / 100.0, relative error 0.00e+00 at every one, including the negative-dominant case) and escapes both adversarial starts (diag(2,1) from [1e-6,1] returns 2.0; diag(-5,1) returns -5.0); against an EXACT autograd Hessian eigendecomposition of a tanh MLP it gives 0.6207057980635492 against 0.6207057980637266, relative 2.86e-13, where the released PyHessian 0.1 on the same model returns 0.6201990329421846 (8.16e-4) -- BLME is roughly 3e9 times closer to the truth than its own reference. Hutchinson is unbiased (600 probes give 3.343485770 against an exact trace 3.491580409, 4.2% at a reported relative standard error of 3.07%). SAM Eq. 2 with p=2 returns 0.05125000000000002 against the closed form 0.05125 (2.71e-16). The critical step returns eta* within 3.2e-3 to 5.0e-3 relative of the exact 2/lambda at lambda = 0.25 / 4 / 37.5 / 100 / 10000 -- inside the 2^-6 = 1.6% resolution of its own 6 bisections. The round-4 branch asymmetry is closed: on the loss that is EXACTLY flat for eta <= 1e-9 and rising above it, the downward branch now returns eta* = 9.9945068359375e-10 against an exact 1e-9 (5.5e-4) and lambda_c = 2001099236.6 against 2e9, where the round-4 build returned (nan, nan). The round-3 underflow anchor is also exact: a float32 Hessian of 1.0000000787060493e-23 returns 1.0000000787060493e-23, bit-identical.

- GitHub (YES): amirgholami/PyHessian; google-research/sam — I ran the installed PyHessian 0.1 `hessian(net, criterion, data).eigenvalues(top_n=1)` against BLME's `_power_iteration_top_eigenvalue` on the identical float64 tanh MLP and cross-entropy batch, with an exact 92x92 autograd Hessian eigendecomposition as the arbiter: BLME 0.6207057980635492, exact 0.6207057980637266, PyHessian 0.6201990329421846. BLME is 2.86e-13 from exact and PyHessian is 8.16e-4, so BLME is strictly the more accurate of the two under PyHessian's own signed-Rayleigh convention (PyHessian's default maxIter/tol stop early). Foret et al.'s p=2 normalized-rho perturbation gives the analytic 0.05125 and BLME 0.05125000000000002.
- Paper (NONE): None of the four sources publishes a like-for-like scalar for a named pretrained language model at this parameter scope, batch and loss: Foret et al. tabulate generalization error rather than the loss delta L(theta + rho g/||g||) - L(theta), Yao et al. plot ResNet/CIFAR Hessian spectra, Hutchinson 1990 is a variance result, and Kalra & Barkeshli plot OLMo-2 edge-of-stability trajectories rather than a per-batch eta*. The reproducible anchors are therefore analytic (exact eigh, exact diagonal trace, the closed-form SAM delta rho + rho^2/2, and eta* = 2/lambda) plus the released reference code, all quoted above.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Coordinate count and parameterization write directly into every key. (1) Zero-padding a scalar quadratic with inactive zero-curvature coordinates from n = 1 to 64, 1024 and 4096 holds the raw Hutchinson trace at EXACTLY 1.000000000 while hutchinson_trace_per_param goes 1.000000000000 -> 0.015625000000 -> 0.000976562500 -> 0.000244140625, i.e. exactly 1/n, so that key measures the scope's size and nothing else. (2) The function-preserving two-factor reparameterization above changes the top eigenvalue 3333x and the trace 4794x with the loss fixed at exactly 0.5. (3) The default scope is the last transformer block, whose parameter count is 12*d_model^2 + 13*d_model for a GPT-style block, so n_params enters quadratically in width by construction (measured 7,087,872 for gpt2, distilgpt2 and pythia-160m; 3,152,384 for pythia-70m). Over the four-model panel rho(top_eigenvalue_estimate, d_model) = +0.775 (p = 0.225) and rho(critical_sharpness, d_model) = -0.775 (p = 0.225), but n = 4 makes the causal tests, not these, the evidence.
- DEFECT: CODE (dead), src/blme/tasks/dynamics/sharpness.py:271: `hi0 = eta` is assigned in the downward branch of `_critical_step_size` and never read -- left over from the pre-midpoint version. Same dead-assignment pattern reviewers have caught three times in this campaign.
- DEFECT: DIAGNOSTIC, sharpness.py:_power_iteration_top_eigenvalue: `converged` compares the last relative Rayleigh change against a fixed 1e-7 while the loop runs in the model's float32 arithmetic, so the tolerance is below what that arithmetic can deliver and the flag reads False on 3 of 4 real models -- gpt2 _meta_power_iter_rel_change 2.850460e-06, distilgpt2 1.445922e-06, pythia-160m 4.225201e-07 (all False) against pythia-70m 0.0 (True). It is not measuring convergence: the estimate is stable to ~1e-6 across the whole sweep while the flag flickers. Set the tolerance from the arithmetic dtype (sqrt(eps) ~ 3.5e-4 in float32) or drop the key.
- DEFECT: CONFIG, defaults.yaml:257 n_hutchinson = 24: nowhere near enough on real models. The task's own emitted _meta_hutchinson_trace_rel_se is 1.822277 on pythia-160m (182% relative standard error, so the sign of hutchinson_trace_estimate = -16305.2 is undetermined) and 0.344795 on pythia-70m, against 0.035011 (gpt2) and 0.028727 (distilgpt2). On the analytic MLP whose exact trace is 3.491580409, the seeded 24-probe estimate is 3.150198309 (9.8% off) while 600 probes give 3.343485770 (4.2%). The uncertainty IS emitted, which is the right fix; the number must not be read as a point estimate, and on half this panel it should not be read at all.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY/HYGIENE: `hutchinson_trace_per_param` is emitted UNPREFIXED although it fails the mechanism test outright -- zero-padding inactive coordinates divides it by exactly n (1.0 -> 0.000244140625 from n = 1 to 4096) while the raw trace is unchanged at 1.0. NORMALIZERS is correctly empty, but an unprefixed key that behaves like 1/n can still enter the feature matrix through is_feature().
- DEFECT: COST, defaults.yaml:258 n_power_iter = 64: the loop issues two HVPs per iteration plus one at the end (129 double-backward passes) and never stops early by design (documented, because a start vector near a non-dominant eigenvector makes the Rayleigh quotient look stationary -- which my diag(2,1) test confirms is a real hazard). The full budget is therefore justified; recording it as a cost, not a defect.
- FIX: Delete the unused `hi0` at sharpness.py:271. Set `_meta_power_iter_converged`'s tolerance from the arithmetic dtype (e.g. sqrt(finfo.eps)) or drop the key, since at 1e-7 in float32 it reads False on values stable to 1e-6. Rename `hutchinson_trace_per_param` to `_meta_hutchinson_trace_per_coordinate` so an exactly-1/n quantity cannot enter the feature matrix. Raise n_hutchinson, or gate the trace on its own emitted relative standard error, so a value whose sign is undetermined (pythia-160m, relSE 1.82) is not reported as a point estimate.

### `dynamics_stability`

**codex — RED** · The embedding-noise branch violates its defined row-relative perturbation: a fixed non-tied finite embedding fixture has scale-factored mean 0.966494845361, but the live evaluator returns 0.031756902891, 0.042763078846, and 0.044048734770 at scales 1e-30, 1e-13, and 1e25, while matching at scale 1. Decisively, noise_std=0 must give self-stability 1.0, yet it gives 0.048611111111 at 1e25; the reference branch also gives 0.039290606301 instead of 1.0 after a pure token-ID permutation of identical word vectors.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): laura-burdick/embeddingStability — The authors' public 31-commit repository identifies stability.py as the paper's word-stability implementation, but a fresh local git fetch failed with exit 128, so executable parity was not claimed. The released statistic is top-10 overlap o/10, while BLME reports J=o/(2k-o): at k=10 and o=4 these are 0.4 and 0.25, respectively, confirming an explicitly disclosed proxy rather than a like-for-like port.
- Paper (NONE): Wendlandt et al. Table 1 has pair overlaps 4/10, 3/10, and 5/10, whose paper-defined mean is 0.400000; applying BLME's per-item Jaccard transform gives 0.25, 0.176470588235, and 0.333333333333, mean 0.253267973856 (-36.6830%). The paper publishes no target number for BLME's k=50 seeded-noise/checkpoint proxy, and the proxy-only provenance label is therefore accurate.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): A fresh 11-model panel gave pooled Spearman rho +0.22884 with d_model, -0.05991 with n_layers, and -0.37081 with vocab_size (Pearson vocab r=-0.82367), while within-family rho(score,d_model)=+1.0 for GPT-2, Pythia, and Qwen and within-family Pearson r was +0.98369/+0.97544/+0.93271. The exact-geometry zero-padding intervention independently raises the score by 0.0165028, causally confirming ambient width as a confound despite modest pooled rank association.
- DEFECT: CODE/numerics, stability.py:61-86: row_scale uses an ordinary norm with an absolute 1e-10 floor before the new safe cosine normalization. On one non-tied fixture the correct scale-invariant mean is 0.966494845361, but live means across scales 1e-30/1e-25/1e-13/1/1e13/1e25/2.406159650500376e38 are 0.031756902891/0.031756902891/0.042763078846/0.966494845361/0.966494845361/0.044048734770/0.044048734770; realized relative noise is 4.276e17-8.640e17, 4.276e12-8.640e12, 4.276-8.640, 0.01, 0.01, non-finite, and non-finite instead of 0.01 throughout.
- DEFECT: CODE/fail-open, stability.py:84-86,102-104,142-146: at scales 1e25 and 2.406159650500376e38 the finite input rows produce infinite norms and entirely non-finite E2, after which argsort silently turns NaN similarities into a finite mean 0.044048734770. The exact noise_std=0 anchor is even stronger: expected 1.0, live 0.048611111111 at both scales because inf*0 becomes NaN.
- DEFECT: CODE/reference semantics, stability.py:41-46,92-94: reference mode loads no reference tokenizer and checks only vocabulary length. Merely permuting IDs of the same 97 semantic token vectors returns 0.039290606301 +/- 0.054353670484 instead of the word-aligned 1.0 +/- 0.0.
- DEFECT: CODE/config boundary, stability.py:25-26,130-146: 1 <= k < V and num_samples >= 1 are not validated. At V=97, k=0, 97, and 98 all return the misleading perfect 1.0, while num_samples=0 returns NaN summaries and -1 raises a raw ValueError.
- DEFECT: CODE/precision, stability.py:89-104,137-144: similarities are unconditionally accumulated after casting to float32. On a 64-row near-tie angular fixture, a higher-precision cosine calculation on the same stored rows gives mean 0.046006944444 with 255 distinct Gram values, while the live path collapses them to ties and returns 1.0.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY/design: zero-padding unchanged GPT-2 geometry from D=768 to 2048 moves the 10-seed mean 0.950654510372 -> 0.967157292586 (+0.016502782214; Spearman over four widths 1.0), so stability_mean must not be pooled across widths.
- DEFECT: PROTOCOL: the study's num_samples=2000 fix resolves the prior two-model row-sampling example (GPT-2/DistilGPT-2 gap 0.00582438 versus seed ranges 0.0027098/0.0025504 and 0/10 flips), but one perturbation draw remains the whole measurement; stability_std and _meta_stability_sem describe rows conditional on that draw, not across-draw uncertainty.
- DEFECT: TEST GAP: 23 focused tests pass, but test_comprehensive_parity.py:2705-2719 copies the same norm-and-floor perturbation construction before invoking its independent neighbor search. It therefore cannot catch the measured 0.966494845361 -> 0.031756902891 scale failure or the noise_std=0 failure.
- FIX: For implementation fidelity, form the perturbation entirely in per-row factored coordinates: scale-safe normalize each nonzero E1 row and each random direction, compute E2_unitish = E1_unit + noise_std*raw_unit without materializing an absolute row norm, keep zero rows zero or reject them explicitly, and fail closed before ranking if any value is non-finite. Align reference rows by shared token string, validate 1 <= k < V and num_samples >= 1, and accumulate cosine similarities at sufficient precision for stored near-ties; add regressions for all seven requested scales, noise_std=0, zero rows, token-ID permutations, and invalid k. Separately, retain the raw score but add a distinctly named cross-width variant only after it survives the D=768 -> 2048 zero-padding test at max cosine change 0.0 and removes the current +0.0165028 shift; average multiple perturbation draws and report across-draw uncertainty.

**opus5 — GREEN** · YES, GREEN with confounds is the right axis call, and I checked both halves before saying so. The estimator computes exactly its documented definition -- Jaccard overlap of the top-k cosine neighbourhoods of each sampled vocabulary row between the embedding matrix and a seeded perturbation of it whose realized relative size is exactly noise_std. The `norm + 1e-10` -> `safe_unit_rows` change is VALUE-PRESERVING on real embeddings, measured directly: over gpt2, distilgpt2, pythia-70m and Qwen2.5-0.5B (V up to 151936) the maximum absolute difference between the two unit-row matrices is 5.96e-08 / 5.96e-08 / 7.45e-08 / 4.47e-08 and the maximum RELATIVE difference is 3.28e-07 / 3.35e-07 / 4.63e-07 / 3.50e-07 -- i.e. float32 rounding only. The reason is visible in the same measurement: the smallest embedding row norm on any of the four is 3.5457e-04 (pythia-70m), seven orders of magnitude above the 1e-10 the old code added, and NO row on any model has a norm below 1e-6. So the change is correct in principle (it removes an absolute constant from a scale-free operation) and inert in practice on these checkpoints, which is exactly what a correctness fix should look like. Nothing about the DEFINITION is at fault, so the sample-size problem belongs on `confounds`, not here.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): laura-burdick/embeddingStability — The repository released with the cited inspiration (Wendlandt, Kummerfeld & Mihalcea 2018, arXiv:1804.09692) computes stability as the percentage overlap of the top-10 nearest neighbours between two INDEPENDENTLY TRAINED embedding spaces. It has no noise-perturbation mode, so there is no head-to-head number to run against BLME's self-vs-noised-self construction. I did verify the transform relation algebraically: for two k-NN sets of equal size k with overlap o, |A and B| = o and |A or B| = 2k - o, so Jaccard = o/(2k - o) exactly -- a correct monotone reparameterisation of the paper's o/k. PARTIAL: found and read, not runnable on this estimand.
- Paper (NONE): Wendlandt et al. report stability percentages for word2vec/GloVe/PPMI on PTB/NYT across independently trained spaces, a different experiment. Moreover the per-item monotone transform does not preserve AGGREGATE ranking: overlap profiles [0, 1] and [0.6, 0.6] rank 0.5 < 0.6 under mean-overlap but 0.5 > 0.4286 under mean-Jaccard at k=10, so their published means cannot be recovered from BLME's mean even in principle. BLME's own metadata marks the task proxy-only rather than claiming their numbers.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): This is where the finding belongs. The estimator is sound and the realized perturbation is exactly noise_std at every width, but the shipped protocol at num_samples=100 could not resolve the models it was asked to rank: within-model seed range 0.0093 (gpt2) and 0.0106 (distilgpt2) against a between-model difference of 0.0066. My seeds 1-10 give NO ordering flips at either sample size (the ledger reports 1 flip in 10 with a different seed set), so I do not reproduce the flip -- but the range-exceeds-effect fact reproduces cleanly, and that alone means a 100-row run cannot support a ranking. At 2000 rows the SEM is 0.000855 against a 0.005393 effect, so the study config now clears the bar. The task's new _meta_stability_sem is the right instrument and its warning threshold (0.002) fires at 100 rows (SEM 0.0039) and not at 2000 (0.00086), which is the correct behaviour.
- DEFECT: CONFIG (fixed in the study config, still wrong in the package): defaults.yaml ships num_samples: 5000, the study now uses 2000, but any caller who runs the task with num_samples ~ 100 gets SEM 0.0039 on an effect of 0.0054 with nothing but a log line to say so. The _meta_stability_sem emission is the right fix and it is present.
- DEFECT: CODE: the absolute-epsilon pattern the source comment criticises is still present TWICE in the same function, in the noise construction rather than the normalisation: `row_scale = E1_float.norm(dim=1, keepdim=True).clamp_min(1e-10)` and `raw.norm(dim=1, keepdim=True).clamp_min(1e-12)`. Inert on the four models I measured (minimum row norm 3.5457e-04) but it is the identical hazard, and safe_unit_rows_torch already exists in the same package.
- DEFECT: CODE (latent, reference mode): reference_model_path alignment checks only vocabulary LENGTH (`if len(E2_np) != n_vocab`). Two tokenizers with the same vocab size but different id ordering would be compared row-for-row and score near zero rather than near one. Alignment must be by token STRING.
- DEFECT: CODE (latent): k is not validated against 1 <= k < V. k=0 makes np.argsort(sims)[-0:] return the WHOLE vocabulary (Python's [-0:] == [0:]) and k >= V re-includes the query despite the -inf self-mask; both produce finite, meaningless values.
- DEFECT: CODE: one perturbation draw IS the measurement. stability_std is dispersion across sampled token ids, not across perturbation replicates, and _meta_stability_sem inherits that -- it is the standard error of the row-average at a FIXED noise draw, not of the statistic across draws. The seed sweep above shows the across-draw sd at 2000 rows is 0.00105, slightly larger than the within-run SEM of 0.00086, so the emitted SEM mildly understates the true uncertainty.
- DEFECT: CODE (cosmetic): `stability_mode` = 'embedding_noise' and `diagnostic_semantics` = 'embedding_neighborhood_jaccard_stability' are constant strings emitted unprefixed; the same class of column was demoted to _meta_ in sibling tasks.
- DEFECT: CONFIG: the shipped reference_model_path=null means the production path never exercises the reference-model branch, so the only mode the study measures is the synthetic-noise proxy. Worth stating in the paper text rather than leaving to metadata.
- FIX: Keep the current estimator; it is correct and the normalisation change is measured value-preserving to 4.6e-07 relative. Then: (1) average over >= 5 perturbation draws and report the across-draw sd, since one draw is currently the whole measurement and the across-draw sd (0.00105 at n=2000) exceeds the emitted within-run SEM (0.00086); (2) replace the two clamp_min absolute floors with safe_unit_rows_torch; (3) align reference-mode rows by token string and error out when tokenizers differ; (4) validate 1 <= k < V; (5) prefix the two constant strings with _meta_.

### `geometry_categories`

**opus5 — GREEN** · BOTH ROUND-7 FIXES LAND, AND MY OWN ROUND-7 MEASUREMENT WAS THE ONE THAT WAS WRONG. The four word-analogy accuracy columns are now emitted as `_meta_present_past_analogy_acc_top{1,5}` and `_meta_singular_plural_analogy_acc_top{1,5}`, and I re-ran the study pipeline's `is_excluded_predictor` on those exact live names: all four return EXCLUDED, both by the `_meta_` prefix and independently by the widened `[._]acc(?:[._]|$)` pattern (feature_selection.py:149), so the belt-and-braces fix works. All 14 `<cat>_purity_matched` keys are likewise `_meta_`-prefixed and excluded. The remaining 87 kept numeric columns are the purity / intra / inter / separation / hub_max / hub_skew families plus `n_category_tokens_mapped` and `present_past_consistency`. The kernels themselves are unchanged and there is no released reference to diverge from -- this is a BLME-original construction, correctly declared 'proxy-only' in task_metadata.py.

- GitHub (NONE):  — There is no source paper and no released code: task_metadata.py records the provenance as 'BLME category geometry', 'proxy-only', with an empty repo field. That is the honest classification and I confirmed it -- the category token lists, the k-purity kernel and the hub statistics are all BLME's own. Nothing to fetch or cross-verify.
- Paper (NONE): No source paper, therefore no published number. Stating this plainly rather than manufacturing an anchor: the task's own reference tests (exact constant-relation anchors for the relation/analogy kernels; the hub kernel against an independent k-occurrence recomputation) are the correct standard here and they are in the repo.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Mean |rho| with vocab_size 0.376 over the 14 raw purity columns on 18 models, with four KEPT columns above 0.70 (multi_digit_inter +0.806, currencies_hub_max +0.767, punctuation_separation +0.731, singular_plural_purity -0.713). d_model is much weaker (mean |rho| 0.156). The demoted matched twins are worse on BOTH axes (see defects), which is why they are demoted.
- DEFECT: I MUST RETRACT MY OWN ROUND-7 NUMBER. Last round I reported that the maintainer's d_model claim did not reproduce -- he wrote mean |rho| 0.151 -> 0.241 (worse) and I measured 0.353 -> 0.199 (better) over 11 models. On an 18-model panel with a much wider vocabulary spread (32,000-262,144) the measurement is raw 0.156 -> matched 0.193, i.e. WORSE, reproducing the maintainer's original direction and magnitude almost exactly. My 0.353 -> 0.199 was a small-panel artifact. So the answer to 'does the demotion rest on the vocab_size half alone?' is NO: it rests on both. vocab_size 0.376 -> 0.418 (worse), d_model 0.156 -> 0.193 (worse). Per category the matched twin is worse on vocab_size for 6 of 14 (units -0.142 -> -0.730, multi_digit +0.348 -> +0.878, colors -0.375 -> -0.627, currencies -0.487 -> -0.792, prepositions -0.413 -> -0.598, pronouns -0.454 -> -0.581), better for 7 (singular_plural -0.713 -> -0.069, determiners -0.409 -> -0.210, digits +0.391 -> +0.136, emotions -0.216 -> +0.185, present_past -0.540 -> -0.403, punctuation +0.164 -> -0.093, seasons -0.370 -> -0.300) and unchanged for months (-0.247). The MEAN rises because the degradations are much larger than the improvements -- which is the honest way to state it. Fixing m and k removes the ceiling artifact, which was real; the fixed-size subsample is still drawn from a tokenizer-dependent pool, so the association relocates rather than reduces. The demotion is correct and both halves of the evidence stand.
- DEFECT: THE RAW `<cat>_purity` FAMILY AND ITS SIBLINGS CARRY THE SAME CONFOUND THEY WERE DEMOTED FOR, AND ARE KEPT. Four kept columns exceed |rho| 0.70 with vocab_size at p < 0.003 (multi_digit_inter +0.806, currencies_hub_max +0.767, punctuation_separation +0.731, singular_plural_purity -0.713). Demoting the twin while keeping 87 columns whose strongest members are stronger vocabulary proxies than the twin is not a resolution.

- DEFECT: task_metadata.py's entry is a single sentence ('Tokenizer/category dependent. B7: relation/analogy kernels now covered...') with no record of the 2026-09-05 demotions, the widened regex, or the measured confound sizes. Classification 'proxy-only' is right; the note has not kept up.
