Correct the campaign record: my round-7 d_model figure (0.353 -> 0.199, better) does not survive an 18-model panel; the measurement is 0.156 -> 0.193 (worse), so the maintainer's original claim was right in direction and the demotion rests on both axes, not on vocab_size alone. If the study wants this task's structural half, demote `n_category_tokens_mapped` and the four KEPT columns above |rho| 0.70 with vocab_size (multi_digit_inter, currencies_hub_max, punctuation_separation, singular_plural_purity), or state in the paper that this family is a tokenizer-geometry probe rather than a scale-free structural one.

### `geometry_cka`

**codex — RED** · For column-centered X and Y, Kornblith et al. Eq. 14 gives linear CKA = ||Y^T X||_F^2 / (||X^T X||_F ||Y^T Y||_F), so nonzero isotropic rescaling cancels exactly. On one fixed finite, nonconstant input pair the defining formula stayed in [0.317322557802, 0.317322558265] over the seven requested scales, but CKATask.evaluate returned NaN at 1e-30, 1e-25, 1e-13, 1e13, 1e25, and 2.406159650500376e38 (6/7 cases), versus 0.317322581658 at scale 1.

- GitHub (YES): google-research/google-research (representation_similarity/Demo.ipynb) — Fetched the official 19,029-byte Demo.ipynb and executed feature_space_linear_cka and its debiased path locally. On the notebook's seed-1337 input, official biased/debiased values 0.557613256612/0.513456489506 matched CKATask's 0.557613251381/0.513456492801 at relative errors 9.38e-09/6.42e-09, but on the scale-1e-13 finite input the official formula returned 0.317322558001 while CKATask returned NaN.
- Paper (NONE): The paper publishes 0 raw linear-CKA scalar values with corresponding released activations for this BLME estimand. Table 2's 99.3% is layer-identification accuracy over 10 trained 10-layer CNNs, not a CKA value, so the locally reproduced 0.557613256612 notebook value is GitHub evidence rather than a paper-number reproduction.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): The exact identity-layer sweep L={2,4,8,16,32,64} gave rho=+1.000 for biased avg_adjacent_cka and mean_offdiag_cka and also rho=+1.000 for both debiased counterparts; debiased avg_adjacent_cka moved 0.012983882329 -> 0.984333077454 despite endpoint delta 0.000e+00. The independent-cloud width sweep also gave rho=+1.000 and a 29.789x biased-floor increase, while debiased values stayed between -0.008064576901 and 0.003104398839, confirming distinct depth-aggregation and finite-sample width mechanisms.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY: evaluate() computes unscaled X^T X and torch.norm at cka.py:340-341, then passes those already-destroyed cached norms at cka.py:376-377; the helper's later max-factorization cannot recover them. At scales 1e-30/1e-25 the cached norm was 0, at 1e-13 all 576 Gram entries were nonzero but torch.norm was still 0, and at 1e13/1e25/2.406159650500376e38 it was inf, producing the six NaNs above although all 9,600 input entries were finite and nonzero at every scale.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY (AUXILIARY): _linear_cka_debiased re-centers the already-centered tensor in unscaled arithmetic at cka.py:187-188; at 2.406159650500376e38 it returned NaN against the official debiased value 0.236224080361. Separately, _linear_cka's default centering path returned 0.377405083803 after an exactly representable +1e6 translation versus an unchanged reference 0.377439581624 (relative error 9.14e-05), although the full task's conditional centering path remained accurate to 1.27e-07 on that fixture.
- DEFECT: TEST COVERAGE: all 16 selected CKA/reference/scale/task tests passed, but test_scale_invariance.py:130-143 imports geometry.mutual_info._normalized_linear_hsic rather than geometry.cka._linear_cka, and the CKA-specific test at lines 398-448 supplies no cached norms and never drives CKATask across the scale ladder. Thus the suite passes while both cache and fresh CKATask routes return NaN at 1e13.
- DEFECT: DOCUMENTATION/PROVENANCE: task_metadata.py:49 still labels the task CONFIRMED_CORRECT and quotes <=1.8e-8, yet the pinned 0.450953881200 official fixture now gives helper output 0.450953945634 (relative 1.43e-07) and production fails 6/7 extreme scales. The _linear_cka_debiased docstring at cka.py:179 still calls it cross-model-comparable despite rho=+1.000 and a 0.012983882329 -> 0.984333077454 identity-layer move.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY, NOT VERDICT: identity-layer insertion raised avg_adjacent_cka by 895.33% and mean_offdiag_cka by 881.34% with zero endpoint change, while d=8 -> 512 raised the unrelated-cloud biased floor by 29.789x; these results determine comparable/confounds only and are not the basis of the RED implementation verdict.
- FIX: Rescale each centered layer with geometry.utils.pow2_rescale before cka.py:340 forms X^T X, store that rescaled tensor in centered_acts, and compute both cached norms and debiased self-HSIC from it; do not attempt to divide a cached 0/inf norm after the fact. That locally tested candidate was finite on 7/7 requested scales with worst biased relative error 2.44e-07, repaired the top-scale debiased value to 0.236224080594 versus 0.236224080361 (relative 9.86e-10), and changed 0/100 ordinary outputs. For _linear_cka's uncentered helper path, center the representable input in float64 (or reuse the task's conditional centering) before rescaling; add direct plus cache/fresh end-to-end regressions over all seven scales and +1e3/+1e6 translations, then correct the stale metadata and comparable-form docstring.

**opus5 — RED** · The 2026-09-06 fix to `_linear_cka` is real but UNREACHABLE on the shipped path, so the counterexample it was written to close is still live end-to-end. Driving the REAL `CKATask.evaluate()` on gpt2 (defaults.yaml num_samples=100) with the collected hidden states multiplied by a constant -- an operation Kornblith et al. Eq. 4 leaves EXACTLY invariant -- gives min_offdiag_cka 0.12497064739937529 at scale 1, 0.12497059294951675 at 1e-13 (rel 4.4e-07, fixed), and **NaN at 1e-25, 1e13 and 1e25**, where every one of the 14 numeric keys is NaN. Root cause is the CALLER, not the kernel: cka.py:335-336 computes `norms[idx] = torch.norm(X.t() @ X, p='fro').item()` in unrescaled float32, which underflows to 0.0 at 1e-25 and overflows to inf at 1e13; `_linear_cka`'s new conversion `norm_x / (_gxf*_gxf)` then divides an already-dead 0.0 or inf and returns NaN. Measured directly on a seeded (200,24) centered pair with max exactly 1 so every scale is representable: the cached-norm path returns NaN at 1e-30/1e-25/1e-13/1e13/1e25/2.406e38 and only 0.12361074014749511 at scale 1, while the SAME function called without cached norms is finite at all seven (rel 7.0e-09..1.7e-07 vs a float64 reference of 0.12361074836266336). The claim 'invariant 1e-30..2.406e38' is therefore false for the configuration the task actually runs.

- GitHub (YES): google-research/google-research (representation_similarity/Demo.ipynb) — Compared against the released `feature_space_linear_cka` formulation transcribed to float64. On ordinary inputs BLME agrees to rel 2.65e-08..1.97e-07 across scales that survive (float32 cast, not a formula difference). The disagreement is confined to the magnitudes where the shipped cached-norm path dies: at 1e-25 the released formula stays finite at 0.12361074836266336 while `CKATask.evaluate` returns NaN for all 14 keys.
- Paper (PARTIAL): Kornblith et al. publish CKA heatmaps for CNNs, not a scalar table entry for a language model, so there is no published number to reproduce. The estimator-level anchors do hold: identical layers score exactly 1.0 and independent Gaussians sit at the published d/N floor.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): The null-floor mechanism is a direct d/N dependence (0.033 -> 0.668 as d goes 8 -> 512 at n=256, the file's own measured table). At the shipped N the floor contributes a small fraction of the reported value, so the confound is real in mechanism but weak in magnitude at the study's sample size.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY, UNCHANGED BY THE FIX: cka.py:335-336 computes the cached `||X^T X||_F` in unrescaled float32. Measured on the real gpt2 evaluate(): every headline key is NaN at input scale 1e-25, 1e13 and 1e25 against min_offdiag_cka = 0.12497064739937529 at scale 1. The max-factoring added inside `_linear_cka` cannot recover a norm that was already 0.0 or inf when it arrived. The fix moved the failure boundary from 1e-13 to 1e-25; it did not remove it.
- DEFECT: DEAD FIX: the new `_center` helper in `_linear_cka` (which factors the max out before `mean` so 200 rows at 3e38 do not sum to inf) is on the `centered=False` branch, but the ONLY production call site, cka.py:376-377, passes `centered=True`. grep over src/ shows `_linear_cka(` is called exactly once outside tests. The centering that actually runs is cka.py:309-331 in `evaluate`, which calls `X.mean(dim=0)` on unrescaled float32 -- precisely the overflow the new helper was written to prevent. The fix protects a branch the shipped path never takes.
- DEFECT: TEST GAP: tests/test_scale_invariance.py:434-438 exercises `_linear_cka(a, b, centered=True)` WITHOUT cached norms, so it takes the healthy path and passes while the real `CKATask.evaluate` returns NaN on the same ladder. No test drives evaluate() at a non-unit input scale.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY, NOT VERDICT: the biased null floor rises from 0.033 to 0.668 as width goes 8 -> 512 at n=256 (the file's own table), so the headline aggregates carry a width term; the debiased twins that remove it are emitted only as `_meta_`.
- FIX: Rescale in the CALLER, not only in the kernel. At cka.py:333-336 factor each centered layer's max magnitude out before forming `X.t() @ X` and store the norm together with its factor (or simply store the rescaled matrix and let `_linear_cka` recompute), so the value reaching `_linear_cka` is finite at every representable input scale. Either delete the now-dead `_center` branch in `_linear_cka` or apply the same max-factoring to the real centering at cka.py:309-331. Add a regression that drives `CKATask.evaluate` end-to-end at 1e-25/1e13/1e25 and asserts finiteness -- the existing scale test cannot see this because it bypasses the cached-norm path.

### `geometry_cknna`

**codex — YELLOW** · The paper-defined mutual-kNN normalized kernel alignment and the released implementation are faithfully ported on ordinary inputs: BLME matched the clean dcd76ba checkout in 240/240 ordered kernel cases, 40/40 preprocessing tensors, and 20/20 full-pipeline scores bit-for-bit (maximum error 0.000e+00), and both extraction routes now consume the released reference's raw HF hidden-state tuple. YELLOW remains for one value-changing implementation caveat: rescaling the same all-finite input from 1 to 2.406159650500376e38 changes 0.129549999 to 0.159972884 (+23.484%), whereas an independent stable evaluation gives 0.129550017.

- GitHub (YES): minyoungg/platonic-rep @ dcd76ba3c950c1b197a2ae8b1c6713535c94ecf9 — The remote HEAD resolved to dcd76ba3c950c1b197a2ae8b1c6713535c94ecf9, and I directly imported and executed metrics.py from a clean local checkout at that exact commit. AlignmentMetrics.cknna matched BLME in 240/240 ordered cases over 5 seeds, N={40,64,80,200}, k={5,10,39}, both HSIC estimators, and both argument orders; official remove_outliers(q=0.95) then F.normalize matched 40/40 tensors, and 20/20 full-pipeline scores were exact (maximum error 0.000e+00). The reference extract_features.py reads the raw hidden_states tuple at both relevant branches, and fresh cache/uncached probes observed replace_final_norm=false in 2/2 BLME routes.
- Paper (NONE): Huh et al. publish 0 raw within-model layer-profile CKNNA scalars that this task can reproduce; Figure 10 reports standardized trends over 1,024 paired samples. The paper's analytic k=N limit does reproduce on a fresh N=k=1024 fixture: BLME and official CKNNA both give 0.015542514293 versus independent linear CKA 0.015542516108 (relative error 1.167e-07), but that is not a published empirical scalar.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Causal interventions confirm two architecture channels: isometric width padding leaves raw CKNNA unchanged to 0.000e+00 but makes the released full pipeline collapse from mean 0.139841145 at d=8 to 0.000000000 at d=161/256 (20/20 seeds; mean rho=-0.7769), while fixed-endpoint layer refinement L=4 to L=48 raises adjacent/all-pair means by 68.837%/49.714% with rho=+1.000. The effective 2,000-token study cap equalizes N after the first 100 WikiText passages contain 12,895 GPT-2/DistilGPT-2 tokens versus 13,186 Pythia-70M tokens, but it does not remove the measured width or layer-grid effects.
- DEFECT: CODE/NUMERICS src/blme/tasks/geometry/cknna.py:262-286 and :447-448: on a deterministic N=80,d=16,k=10 seed-70 fixture whose maximum is below 1, finite scales 1e-30 through 1e25 retain 0.129549999 within relative 2.02e-07, but multiplying by 2.406159650500376e38 leaves every entry finite while the mean of the row quantiles overflows to inf. The non-finite guard therefore clips 0 coordinates instead of the scale-1 route's 99/89 in X/Y and returns 0.159972884 rather than 0.129549999 (+23.484%); stable evaluation of the same rounded inputs returns 0.129550017.
- DEFECT: CODE/NUMERICS src/blme/tasks/geometry/cknna.py:155-256 and :289-326: translations are not a theoretical invariance of the released row-normalized kernel, but the required conditioning probes expose arithmetic error on the same rounded finite inputs. At +1e3 BLME returns 0.100709393 versus stable evaluation 0.094152517 (+6.964%); at +1e6 it returns 0.085430545 versus 0.101045449 (-15.453%). The official ordinary-precision route is itself unstable (0.100709393 and 0.031557111), so this does not overturn the exact ordinary-domain reference parity.
- DEFECT: CODE/PROTOCOL src/blme/tasks/geometry/cknna.py:405-439 and :515-516: n_points/topk validation occurs before max_cknna_tokens is applied. With 25 rows, cap=15, and requested topk=20, evaluate silently uses k=14 and emits avg_adjacent_cknna=0.043437061 while metadata reports topk=20 and n_points=15; requested topk=15 produces the identical 0.043437061 while reporting 15, whereas topk=10 produces 0.189006268. topk=1 also passes the wrapper guard and raises ValueError instead of returning the task's normal error payload.
- DEFECT: CODE/DISCLOSURE src/blme/tasks/geometry/cknna.py:451-466 and :517-519: _meta_cknna_order_asymmetry_max checks adjacent pairs only although every off-diagonal output copies one argument order. On a deterministic 5-layer, N=40,d=8,k=5 seed-3 task fixture it reports 0.000271184, while the true all-pair maximum is 0.001795403 (6.621x larger); reversing all pairs moves mean-off-diagonal from 0.088420727 to 0.088380432 (absolute 0.000040295).

A fresh valid-domain search across N={4,5,6,8,12,20}, every 2<=k<N, and 300 seeds found 12,888 finite values, of which 2,199 were outside [0,1], plus 12 non-finite values; the finite range was -5.776147119 to 15.093293766.

At d=161/256 the q=0.95 bound is zero and all 20/20 scores collapse, so no cross-width claim is supportable for the faithful raw output.

No separately named normalized output exists: the runtime registry has 0 CKNNA normalizers.
- DEFECT: CONFIG/PROTOCOL, not implementation, src/blme/tasks/configs/defaults.yaml:400-404 and beyond_benchmarks/configs/tasks.yaml:204-205: the effective study setting is num_samples=100, topk=10, unbiased=true, max_cknna_tokens=2000, and seed=42. The first 100 cached WikiText validation passages produce 12,895 GPT-2/DistilGPT-2 tokens and 13,186 Pythia-70M tokens before all are capped to N=2,000; the first 8 produce N=630 versus 631. The released language experiment instead uses 1,024 paired document-average features, so BLME is a disclosed within-model token-level adaptation rather than a like-for-like protocol.
- DEFECT: PROVENANCE/DOCUMENTATION src/blme/task_metadata.py:131: metadata still says replace_final_norm was added, the opposite of current and released extraction. Runtime probes observed false in 2/2 BLME routes and the clean reference stacks the raw tuple; raw versus hypothetical hooked extraction changes mean-off-diagonal by +0.1874%, +1.3043%, and -0.5929% on GPT-2, DistilGPT-2, and Pythia-70M, with the largest tested endpoint shift 8.026%.
- DEFECT: TEST GAP: 12/12 CKNNA parity tests, 20/20 post-final-norm declaration tests, and 20/20 generic scale-invariance tests pass (52/52 total), but test_scale_invariance.py contains 0 CKNNA cases. Consequently none fails on the live 0.129549999 to 0.159972884 boundary-scale error, the cap/topk metadata mismatch, or the 6.621x understated all-pair order asymmetry.
- FIX: For GREEN, preserve bit-exact ordinary-domain behavior but conditionally prevent the row-quantile mean from overflowing, either by a power-of-two rescale around the reduction or by accumulating that mean in float64, and add the full remove_outliers -> normalize -> CKNNA pipeline at 1e-30, 1e-25, 1e-13, 1, 1e13, 1e25, and 2.406159650500376e38. Promote normalization, Gram, and HSIC arithmetic conditionally for offset-dominated clouds and add +1e3/+1e6 conditioning regressions. Separately revalidate topk after capping and emit the realized k, compute order asymmetry across every emitted pair, correct the helper range and provenance text, and keep cross-width/depth normalization as a separately named output rather than silently redefining the faithful score.

**opus5 — GREEN** · The revert is correct and the kernel is BIT-EXACT with the released reference. I cloned minyoungg/platonic-rep (dcd76ba) and read extract_features.py:81-84: the `avg` pooling path is `feats = torch.stack(llm_output['hidden_states']).permute(1,0,2,3)` -- the RAW HuggingFace tuple, whose last entry is ln_f(block L-1) and whose first is the embedding, with no hook and no substitution. cknna.py:389/394 now passes `replace_final_norm=False` on both routes, matching it, and the task emits `_meta_last_entry_is_block_output = False` (measured on gpt2 via both the self-collect and shared-cache routes), which is truthful. I then ran platonic-rep's own `AlignmentMetrics.cknna` head-to-head against BLME's `_cknna` on six configurations -- (n,d) in {(64,32),(128,64),(100,768)} x topk in {5,10} -- and every pair was IDENTICAL to the last bit (rel 0.000e+00, `a == b` True): e.g. 0.06270747384949794, 0.10419933663139197, 0.0418546593174689, 0.07627008269988227. The 2026-09-06 revert is a fidelity improvement, not a regression.

- GitHub (YES): minyoungg/platonic-rep — Cloned the repo and executed metrics.py's `AlignmentMetrics.cknna` directly against BLME's `_cknna` on 6 (n, d, topk) configurations: bit-identical in all 6 (rel 0.000e+00, equality True). Separately read extract_features.py:82 to confirm the hidden-state convention the revert restores -- `torch.stack(llm_output['hidden_states'])`, the raw tuple including the post-final-norm entry.
- Paper (NONE): Huh et al. (The Platonic Representation Hypothesis) report CKNNA alignment as scatter plots and relative trends across model pairs, not a tabulated scalar for a named LM pair that this task should reproduce. Bit-exactness against the authors' released metric is the stronger available check and it passes.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): Like all masked-HSIC similarity indices CKNNA carries a topk/n term and a representation-magnitude term through the reference's absolute epsilon (see defects); on gpt2 all 89 layer-pair calls sat at max|X| in [0.121938, 0.238567], where the epsilon shifts the value by up to 8.675e-04 relative. That is small but systematic and would differ between models whose activation magnitudes differ.
- DEFECT: INHERITED SCALE-INVARIANCE VIOLATION, LIVE ON REAL DATA BUT SMALL: `denom = sqrt(sim_kk*sim_ll) + 1e-6` (cknna.py:257) is an ABSOLUTE epsilon, so the score is not invariant to rescaling the representations even though the HSIC ratio is. The rescale guard at cknna.py:206-223 deliberately does NOT fire inside the band [7.25834e-05, 16384] to preserve bit-exactness with the reference. Measured on a seeded (64,32) pair: correct at 1e-30/1e-25/1e-13/1e-8..1e-5 (rel <= 3.3e-05, where the guard DOES fire) and at 1/1e4/1e13/1e25/2.406e38, but WRONG throughout the no-rescale band's lower reach -- rel 1.000e+00 at max|X| = 8e-5, 1e-4, 1e-3, 1e-2 (returning 1.85e-13, 4.52e-13, 4.52e-09, 4.52e-05 against 0.12132517229678574) and rel 2.115e-01 at max|X| = 0.1. The failure boundary coincides exactly with the guard's lower edge: rel 1.32e-05 at max 7.23e-5 versus rel 1.000e+00 at max 8e-5. On the shipped path this is mild, not catastrophic: instrumenting the real gpt2 evaluate() shows all 89 `_cknna` calls at max|X| in [0.121938, 0.238567], where shipped-vs-exactly-rescaled differs by 5.2e-04 to 8.675e-04 relative (worst case 0.9991290980801834 vs 0.9999965498983133). This is the reference's behaviour, so it does not make the implementation unfaithful -- but the source comment's claim that an unconditional rescale would only 'move the score in the 5th decimal' is measured wrong by four orders of magnitude at the bottom of the band.
- DEFECT: POST-FINAL-NORM, DISCLOSED NOT HIDDEN: the deepest entry is ln_f(block L-1) and the first is the embedding, so the CKNNA matrix mixes one LayerNorm output and one embedding with L-1 block outputs. This is the reference's convention and the task now says so via `_meta_last_entry_is_block_output = False`, but a reader building a cross-layer feature from this matrix should know one whole row and column is a different kind of object.
- DEFECT: STALE PROVENANCE RECORD: src/blme/task_metadata.py:131 still describes the REVERTED state as current -- "2026-09-05: replace_final_norm added for consistency with the CKA sibling, but measured impact here is only -1.0% to +2.0%" -- while cknna.py:389 and :394 both now pass `replace_final_norm=False`. The metadata is the campaign's provenance index; it currently claims this task hooks the last block when it deliberately does not, which is the opposite of the fidelity property the revert established.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY, NOT VERDICT: the shipped topk=10 is an absolute neighbour count, so the estimand changes meaning if num_samples is ever varied between models. defaults.yaml pins num_samples=100 and topk=10 together, so this is inert as shipped -- defect_location CONFIG at worst.

### `geometry_collapse`

**codex — RED** · Roy-Vetterli Property 2 requires erank(cA)=erank(A): on a finite three-layer fixture the current evaluator agreed with float64 references within 3.64e-9 relative from scales 1e-30 through 1e25, but at 2.406159650500376e38 it returned NaN / 5.213422550 / NaN instead of 15.276807901 / 12.719444962 / 7.806865804 and reported collapse_ratio=1.0 instead of 0.511027294. The defining scale-invariant implementation therefore has a concrete value-changing failure even though its ordinary-domain formula is correct.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): facebookresearch/directclr — I inspected the locally fetched official checkout at commit a86f64c9b0815ff19cfea9929cb7dc6bc293b5d4: its 147-line spectrum.py contains one singular() routine, row-normalises latents at line 131, forms np.cov at line 136, and calls np.linalg.svd at line 137, with 0 effective-rank, entropy, collapse-ratio, or max-drop routines. Thus the motivating release exposes a spectrum but provides 0 matched scalar implementations to execute head-to-head; Roy-Vetterli 2007 supplies the actual formula and has no released code in the cited materials.
- Paper (YES): Roy-Vetterli Sec. 2.3 gives erank=exp(2H((1+|rho|)/2,(1-|rho|)/2)) for its 4x4 correlation matrix. The current effective_rank kernel returned 4.000000000000000 / 3.755143410318479 / 3.079201435678004 / 2.124508162217515 / 1.487401275304271 at rho=0/0.25/0.5/0.75/0.9 versus the closed form 4.000000000000000 / 3.755143410318479 / 3.079201435678004 / 2.124508162217516 / 1.487401275304272, worst relative error 4.48e-16; the BLME collapse_ratio and max_drop extensions have 0 published target numbers.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Width and realised point count are direct causal proxies: the fixed-N Gaussian sweep had rho(erank,d_model)=1.0 and changed 7.994411 -> 480.438700, while the fixed-d sweep had rho(erank,N)=1.0 and changed 30.007313 -> 126.207689. An exact isometric zero-padding control preserved raw erank bit-for-bit at 7.995169971 for d=8/32/128/512, proving the raw kernel tracks occupied dimension rather than array width alone; however the study fixes 200 documents, not token rows, so both architecture width and tokenizer-dependent N remain in the reported raw profile.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY, collapse.py:111-181: the task never calls the newly added utils.safe_svdvals and instead runs np.linalg.svd on a float32 centred matrix. Reproducer: rng=default_rng(8675309); make H=normal(60,16).astype(float32)/maxabs, interleave H and -H, then use layers X, X*linspace(0.05,1,16), and X with columns 8: zero. At scale 2.406159650500376e38 every stored coordinate is finite, but NumPy casts 16/16, 10/16, and 8/16 singular values to inf; current eranks are NaN / 5.213422550 / NaN versus float64 15.276807901 / 12.719444962 / 7.806865804. safe_svdvals on those same represented matrices gives 15.276807905 / 12.719444961 / 7.806865802, at most 3.00e-10 relative from reference.
- DEFECT: CODE/FAIL-OPEN, utils.py:42-55 and collapse.py:184-194: effective_rank silently discards non-finite singular values and nanmin/nanmax silently discard failed layers. On the extreme fixture, the middle layer keeps only its 6 finite smallest singular values and becomes 5.213422550 (-59.01%), the other two become NaN, and the one surviving value produces the false finite collapse_ratio=1.0 instead of the defined 0.511027294; headline summaries must fail closed when any required layer fails.
- DEFECT: CODE/TRANSLATION NUMERICS, collapse.py:111-126: the unconditional .float() followed by float32 mean subtraction violates the translation invariance introduced by centring. On exact float64 translations of the same fixture, +1e3 moved the worst layer erank by 3.670e-6 relative and +1e6 moved 12.719444945 -> 12.801761794 (+0.6472%), collapse_ratio 0.511027293 -> 0.511132689 (+0.02062%), and |max_drop| by +1.5481%; the float64 definition is unchanged.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY/DIMENSION: at N=1200, otherwise structureless Gaussian representations gave max_erank 7.994411 / 31.897372 / 126.231250 / 480.438700 over d=8/32/128/512, a 60.0968x move with rho=1.0. Raw erank_per_layer, max_erank, min_erank, and max_drop must not be pooled across widths; collapse_ratio is the scale-free exception.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY/NORMALIZER: zero-padding one fixed 1200x8 cloud to d=32/128/512 preserved raw erank exactly at 7.995169971, while _meta_max_erank_frac moved 0.999396246 -> 0.249849062 -> 0.062462265 -> 0.015615566, exactly 64x end-to-end. The /d_model metadata twins therefore do not preserve fixed representation geometry and remain RED as cross-width normalizers.

**opus5 — GREEN** · The erank kernel is verbatim Roy & Vetterli 2007 Def. 1 / Eq. (1) (p_k = sigma_k/||sigma||_1, erank = exp H(p) on RAW singular values). Feeding the paper's own Sec. 2.3 4x4 correlated matrix through numpy SVD and BLME's effective_rank reproduces the published closed form exp{2H((1+|rho|)/2,(1-|rho|)/2)} at rho = 0/0.25/0.5/0.75/0.9 with worst relative error 2.99e-16 (rho = 0.5: 3.079201435678004 against 3.079201435678004), and Property 1 (k equal singular values -> k) holds to 5.68e-16 for k = 1,2,3,5,10,50. Both CODE defects that held this task at YELLOW from BOTH reviewers in round 4 are FIXED at the state I reviewed: collapse.py:193 now computes min_erank/max_erank with NO +1e-12 (two identical rank-one layers return exactly 1.0 instead of 0.9999999999989999, abs err 1.000089e-12 removed), and utils.effective_rank:41-55 returns NaN, not 0.0, for a spectrum with no positive singular value -- restoring Property 1's 1 <= erank(A) range and routing a degenerate layer into the existing nanmin/nanmax/_meta_n_failed_layers accounting instead of silently driving collapse_ratio to 0.0. I constructed no value-changing counterexample on the paper-defined domain. ADJUDICATION: both round-4 reviewers returned YELLOW for the same reason -- the +1e-12 in collapse_ratio -- and both were right about the code AS IT STOOD. That epsilon is gone at this state (collapse.py:193), together with the effective_rank 0.0-on-degenerate return, so the shared objection no longer has an object.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): facebookresearch/directclr — Fetched the released spectrum.py from facebookresearch/directclr (147 lines, HTTP 200). Its singular() L2-row-normalizes the latents, transposes, forms np.cov and returns np.linalg.svd's singular values -- grep for 'effective_rank' or 'exp(' returns 0 hits, so the release contains NO effective-rank, exponential-entropy or collapse-ratio routine and there is no matched scalar to run head to head; executing it at all needs an ImageNet val loader, a ResNet-50 checkpoint and CUDA. Roy & Vetterli 2007 (EUSIPCO), which is the actual source of the formula BLME computes, is a 2007 conference paper with no released code. Cross-verification was therefore done against that paper's own closed form and against an independent exp(-sum p log p) implementation (see paper_reason).
- Paper (YES): Roy & Vetterli 2007 Sec. 2.3 gives erank = exp{2 H((1+|rho|)/2, (1-|rho|)/2)} for their correlated 4x4 example. BLME's effective_rank on the actual matrix returns 4.000000000000000 / 3.755143410318479 / 3.079201435678004 / 2.124508162217515 / 1.487401275304271 against the closed form 4.000000000000000 / 3.755143410318479 / 3.079201435678004 / 2.124508162217516 / 1.487401275304271 at rho = 0 / 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75 / 0.9 -- worst relative error 2.99e-16. Property 1 gives exactly k for k = 1,2,3,5,10,50 (worst rel 5.68e-16). collapse_ratio and max_drop are BLME extensions on top of that profile and have no published counterpart.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): The raw profile keys ARE effective-dimension counts and therefore track d_model directly: on a no-structure iid null the erank rises 7.9892 -> 480.5317 over d = 8 -> 512 (a 60x change from width alone) and the participation ratio 7.9165 -> 358.5887. They also track the realized point count. This is a property of the estimand, not a defect: the same sweep leaves the value bit-identical under an exact zero-padding isometry (7.992182755 at d = 8, 64 and 512), so what moves is genuine ambient dimension use, not an arithmetic artifact. collapse_ratio, being a within-model min/max, is the key that should carry a cross-model claim.

The KEY NAME is right; the prose is not, and the two differ substantially (on a measured layer 3.62 vs 5.78). Both round-4 reviewers raised this and it is still at HEAD.
- DEFECT: ROBUSTNESS collapse.py:~104 and ~217: an empty activation mapping reaches `layers[0]` and raises IndexError instead of returning a structured error. Not reachable through collect_hidden_states or a populated cache, so it affects no shipped number.
- DEFECT: CONFIG defaults.yaml:31-32: num_samples: 100 counts DOCUMENTS, so the realized activation-row count -- which max_erank/min_erank depend on strongly -- is not matched across models. Raw erank fields should only be pooled at matched N.

### `geometry_correlation_dimension`

**codex — RED** · The cited Grassberger-Procaccia estimand is D2 = lim_{r->0} log C(r) / log r with all distinct-index pairs retained; for mu = 0.04 delta_0 + 0.96 U([3,4]^3), C(r) has the positive floor 0.04^2 and therefore D2 = 0. A fresh end-to-end run instead returned correlation_dimension = 2.8835033699291728 with zero_pair_frac = 0.0015615615615615615 and fallback 'atoms_removed', while direct counts below the minimum positive separation 0.009222527157012184 held C(r) exactly at 0.0015615615615615615 with fitted slope -3.9796003351734796e-16; pure one-atom and two-atom measures both returned an error rather than 0.

- GitHub (NONE):  — The 1983 Grassberger-Procaccia sources have 0 released author-code repositories, so there is no original implementation to fetch and github is NONE. As secondary evidence only, the active Conda nolds 0.6.3 parity suite passed 5/5: on common logistic-map radii BLME's demoted helper and the intended nolds kernel both returned 0.9675619766124346 (absolute difference 0), while the raw nolds API returned 0.9227377086316992 because of its documented self-match artifact (gap 0.044824267980735355); this later third-party agreement does not exercise the promoted plateau selector or its deletion of zero distances.

One of 3 anchors is inside its published interval and 2 of 3 are outside, so reproduction is PARTIAL; continuous-attractor reproduction does not resolve the exact atomic counterexample.

The direct width mechanism test was exactly null across 5 widths (range 0), and n = 4 leaves every association non-significant, so architectural confounding is SUSPECTED rather than CONFIRMED. normalization.NORMALIZERS contains 0 correlation-dimension entries, hence there is no normalized twin to test.

**opus5 — YELLOW** · The Grassberger-Procaccia machinery itself is right and the numerics are EXACT where this campaign has repeatedly found them broken: on a 1000-point uniform 3-cube the headline is 2.9253622414828975 and reads 2.9253622414829357 / ...9694 / ...9903 / ...8975 / ...8710 / ...9220 / ...8010 at scales 1e-30, 1e-25, 1e-13, 1, 1e13, 1e25 and 2.406159650500376e38 (rel +/-0.000000% at every rung, status 'ok' throughout), and 2.925362241488438 / 2.9253622377411332 under translations +1e3 and +1e6 -- D2 is scale- and translation-invariant by definition and BLME honours both. Calibration is the documented compressive bias: uniform d-cubes at N=2000 give 1.006607989606215 (d=1, +0.66%), 1.9378089586690737 (-3.11%), 2.821568296302525 (-5.95%), 3.7639178077310764 (-5.90%), 5.548795673384107 (-7.52%), 6.9302034509996755 (d=8, -13.37%). BUT I CONFIRM the still-open gpt-5.6-sol RED on both of its legs. (1) For mu = 0.04*delta_0 + 0.96*U([3,4]^3) the exact correlation integral has a constant floor c0 > 0 for every r > 0, so GP's D2 = lim log C(r)/log r is EXACTLY 0; driving the real evaluate() through a controlled stub returns `correlation_dimension` = 2.781014593223445 with `_meta_correlation_dimension_scaling_status` 'ok' and `_meta_correlation_dimension_fallback` 'atoms_removed'. A GP-FAITHFUL fit on the same distances with the atoms kept gives 2.306266648394283 at f=0.04 and 0.01913873847372414 at f=0.40, i.e. the reference behaviour does head to 0, while BLME's headline holds 2.795365687778883 and 1.5733537683119805. The deviation is deliberate and documented in the source, but the key is named `correlation_dimension` and `correlation_dimension_method` says 'hidden_state_grassberger_procaccia_scaling_region', so the emitted name asserts an estimand the branch is not computing. (2) A PURE atomic cloud returns an ERROR rather than 0: one atom x100 and two atoms x50 both give {'error': 'Failed to compute correlation integral across scales.'}. I found the exact mechanism -- the 0.0 atomic branch (correlation_dimension.py:314) requires `_sr[2] == 'unidentified'`, and on those clouds `_gp_correlation_dimension_plateau` returns 'insufficient' (1 atom, 0 positive distances) or 'degenerate' (2 and 3 atoms), so the branch written to answer exactly this case is unreachable for it.

- GitHub (YES): CSchoel/nolds (corr_dim); j-bac/scikit-dimension (CorrInt) — Ran the installed `nolds.corr_dim` live on an RK4 Lorenz attractor (3000 points from a 20k trajectory) against both BLME kernels on the same data. nolds gives 1.4868677977595417; BLME's DEMOTED percentile-window kernel gives 1.4902965112246134 -- +0.2306% from the reference, confirming the percentile path is a faithful port of what nolds computes. BLME's PROMOTED scaling-region headline gives 2.2611045586945897 on the identical distances, which is a deliberate departure from the reference window and is the value closer to the published truth (see paper_number).
- Paper (YES): Grassberger & Procaccia 1983 (PRL 50(5):346-349) publish D2 = 2.05 +/- 0.01 for the Lorenz attractor. On a fresh RK4 Lorenz trajectory BLME's shipped headline (the scaling-region fit) returns 2.2611045586945897, rel +10.30%, at R^2 0.9979. The demoted percentile-window fit returns 1.4902965112246134 (-27.30%) and nolds returns 1.4868677977595417 (-27.47%) on the same distances -- so BLME's promotion of the scaling-region window moved the estimator from 27% below the published value to 10% above it, and the residual is the known finite-N GP edge bias, which I measured independently on uniform d-cubes as -3% to -13% over d = 2..8.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): The dominant driver is the sample size, not the architecture. Holding the model and the corpus fixed and moving only num_samples, gpt2's headline runs 5.410822 -> 6.781262 -> 8.116969 -> 8.975651 across N = 100/200/500/1000 (rho(N, D2) = +1.0, span 1.66x), and the ratio to the resolvability bound is flat at 1.35-1.50 -- i.e. the number is tracking log10(N) rather than the geometry. Width is NOT the mechanism: gpt2 and distilgpt2 share d_model = 768 and read 6.781262223270339 vs 8.160623829901690 (+20.3%), with the SHALLOWER model higher. Hidden-state scale cannot enter at all (invariant to +/-0.000000% over the whole 1e-30..2.4e38 ladder).
- DEFECT: CODE, correlation_dimension.py:365-418: the resolvability gate the source describes is NOT the gate the source applies. The comment at lines 400-412 says the scaling-region estimate is 'GATED on resolvability', that it 'reads at_resolution_ceiling in 12 of 12 real-model runs', and that 'It is now NaN when censored'. What the code actually computes is `status = 'ok' if _decades >= _MIN_SCALING_DECADES else 'at_resolution_ceiling'` (line 636) -- a 0.05-decade SPAN floor -- and `_primary` is emitted whenever that passes. Measured on live production runs: gpt2 emits `correlation_dimension` 6.781262223270339 with status 'ok' against its own emitted Eckmann-Ruelle bound 4.6020599913279625 (1.47x), distilgpt2 emits 8.16062382990169 against the same bound (1.77x). The 2*log10(N) bound is REPORTED and never APPLIED, so the censored observation the comment says is suppressed is in fact the unprefixed headline feeding the feature matrix.
- DEFECT: CODE/DEAD BRANCH, correlation_dimension.py:305-320: the branch that returns the correct D2 = 0.0 for an atomic measure requires `_gp_correlation_dimension_plateau(distances)` to return status 'unidentified'. Measured, it returns 'insufficient' for a single atom (0 positive distances) and 'degenerate' for two- and three-atom clouds (r_lo == r_hi collapses the radius grid), so all three fall through to `return {'error': 'Failed to compute correlation integral across scales.'}`. The one input class the branch exists for cannot reach it.
- DEFECT: ESTIMAND DEVIATION (deliberate, documented, but not in the key name), correlation_dimension.py:290-300: whenever `_atom_frac > 0` the headline silently becomes the CONTINUOUS-PART exponent while still being called `correlation_dimension` with method 'hidden_state_grassberger_procaccia_scaling_region'. On mu = 0.04*delta_0 + 0.96*U([3,4]^3) that is 2.781014593223445 where GP's D2 is exactly 0 (a GP-faithful fit on the same distances gives 2.306266648394283, and 0.019 at 40% atoms). The disclosure is real -- `_meta_correlation_dimension_zero_pair_frac` = 0.0015615615615615615 and `_meta_correlation_dimension_fallback` = 'atoms_removed' -- and the branch is unreachable on the shipped corpus (zero_pair_frac measured 0.0 on both gpt2 and distilgpt2 at defaults), but a reader who filters on the unprefixed key alone cannot tell which estimand they got.
- DEFECT: CALIBRATION (documented), the headline is compressive at higher dimension: uniform d-cubes at N=2000 read 1.0066 / 1.9378 / 2.8216 / 3.7639 / 5.5488 / 6.9302 for true d = 1/2/3/4/6/8, i.e. +0.66% to -13.37%. Better than the demoted percentile window (-22% to -50%) and consistent with the code's own claim of -2.7% to -17.6%, but it is a monotone complexity proxy rather than a calibrated D2.
- DEFECT: CONFIG, tasks.yaml:218 `{num_samples: 200, pooling: mean, num_radii: 30}`: 200 mean-pooled documents give 19,900 pairs and a resolvability bound of 4.60, which every model's headline exceeds by 47-77%.
- FIX: Two concrete changes. (1) Apply the bound the code already computes: gate `correlation_dimension` (and `_meta_correlation_dimension_scaling_region`) to NaN when `sr_slope >= 2*log10(N)`, keeping the raw value in `_meta_correlation_dimension_scaling_region_raw` as the comment already promises -- at the shipped N = 200 that censors gpt2's 6.781262223270339 and distilgpt2's 8.16062382990169, which is the correct outcome for a 200-point cloud. (2) Make the atomic branch reachable: at correlation_dimension.py:314 the guard reads `elif _atom_frac > 0.0 and _sr[2] == 'unidentified'`, but control only reaches that elif when `_sr[0]` is already None or non-finite, so the status test is redundant AND exclusionary -- dropping it to `elif _atom_frac > 0.0:` lets 'insufficient' (1 atom, zero positive distances) and 'degenerate' (2 and 3 atoms, collapsed radius grid) route to the correct D2 = 0.0 instead of `return {'error': 'Failed to compute correlation integral across scales.'}`.

**codex — RED** · The ordinary-domain kernels reproduce their defining equations (identity/sign-flip Block Influence 0.0/2.0; e1->e1+e2 gives 0.292893291 versus the exact 0.292893219), but the contribution implementation is not faithful on finite inputs: for h=[[1,0],[s,0]], h'=[[1.5,0],[-s,0]], s=2.406159650500376e38, the defined mean is (0.5+2.0)/2=1.25 while BLME returns 0.5 because line 102 subtracts before scaling and line 108 discards the overflowed 2.0 token. ### `geometry_hsic`

**codex — GREEN** · Gretton Definition 2 gives biased HSIC = (m-1)^-2 tr(KHLH), whose common factor cancels in the raw endpoint quotient, while normalizing by the two self-HSICs gives Kornblith linear CKA; the current centered-Gram implementation computes both formulas. BLME returned 0.5576132566117588 against official Gram/feature values 0.5576132566117586/0.5576132566117589, stayed within 6.60e-16 relative of the official routines and 3.95e-16 of an explicit-H derivation, and returned the exact [X,Y,3X] raw anchor 9 with at most 7.96e-08 absolute error over the seven requested scales; fresh/cache outputs differed by 0.000e+00 and the recovered GPT-2 block satisfied ln_f(hooked)-hidden_states[-1] = 0.000e+00.

**opus5 — GREEN** · Both emitted estimands are exact against their own definitions. (1) The production kernel _nhsic_from_grams equals the FETCHED official Kornblith code (google-research/representation_similarity/Demo.ipynb, cka(gram_linear(.)) and feature_space_linear_cka) to a worst relative error of 5.396e-16 and 5.218e-16 over 20 seeds, and equals Gretton 2005's explicit-H biased estimator (m-1)^-2 tr(K H L H), normalized, to 4.572e-16 over 10 seeds; it reproduces the official notebook's own printed 0.55761 as 0.557613256611759 (rel 5.840e-06, i.e. exact to the 5 decimals printed). (2) hsic_compression_ratio_raw IS the manuscript's HSIC(0,L-1)/HSIC(0,0) = tr(K_0 K_{L-1})/tr(K_0 K_0) on centred Grams (the common 1/(n-1)^2 cancels): on the power-of-two anchors [X,Y,2X] and [X,Y,4X] it returns 4.0 and 16.0 with absolute error 0.000e+00, and 9.000000048010623 on [X,Y,3X] (4.801e-08 from the exact 9, which is float32 storage of 3X, not the kernel). Scale-safe: nHSIC holds to 3.906e-09 over 1e-20..1e20 and a joint rescale of all layers leaves the raw ratio at 11.234943513 to within 5.854e-08 over 1e-12..1e12. The post-final-norm recovery works as claimed: on gpt2 the hook fires 16/16 forwards and ln_f(hooked) - hs[-1] is exactly 0.0, so the deepest BLOCK output is measured rather than discarded. A degenerate all-zero layer returns NaN, not a fabricated 0.

- GitHub (YES): google-research/google-research @ master, representation_similarity/Demo.ipynb (Kornblith et al. 2019 official code) — I downloaded and executed the official Demo.ipynb, which prints its own reference values (Linear CKA from Examples: 0.55761; from Features: 0.55761; RBF 0.65483; debiased 0.51346). Running its verbatim gram_linear/center_gram/cka and feature_space_linear_cka against BLME's _normalized_linear_hsic on 20 shared seeds gives a worst relative error of 5.396e-16 and 5.218e-16, and on the notebook's own seed-1337 input BLME returns 0.557613256611759 against the printed 0.55761. Gretton et al. 2005 (ALT) released no code -- the 2005 paper predates the practice -- so I implemented its Definition-2 empirical estimator (m-1)^-2 tr(K H L H) with an explicit centering matrix and compared: worst relative 4.572e-16 over 10 seeds. BLME additionally satisfies the invariances Kornblith states in Section 3: orthogonal invariance to 1.388e-17, and correctly NOT invariant to non-isotropic rescaling (0.052632 vs 0.087882).
- Paper (PARTIAL): The BLME manuscript (sections/metrics_geometry.tex:220-241) publishes no numeric HSIC value, and Kornblith et al. 2019's figures are CKA heatmaps over CNN layer pairs that cannot be reproduced from a language model. What is reproducible, and I reproduced it: (a) the source paper's official artifact prints Linear CKA = 0.55761 on a fixed seed-1337 input, and BLME returns 0.557613256611759 -- relative 5.840e-06, which is exactly the rounding of the 5 printed decimals; (b) exact closed forms for the manuscript's own compression-ratio formula, which returns 4.0 and 16.0 with absolute error 0.000e+00 on [X,Y,2X] and [X,Y,4X]. PARTIAL rather than YES because (a) is a released-notebook value, not a number printed in either paper's text.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): CONFIRMED for hsic_compression_ratio_raw specifically. Its structural decomposition raw = nCKA(0,L-1) * sqrt(tr(K_{L-1}^2)/tr(K_0^2)) (verified to relative 1.581e-16) makes it a product of an alignment term and a residual-stream norm-growth term, and the norm term dominates: 14.352322797 against 0.782796184 on gpt2. Empirically Spearman(raw, n_layers) = +0.787 (p = 0.012) over 9 models with a 9474.3x range. Not a WIDTH proxy: isometric zero-padding d = 64 -> 2048 leaves it bit-identical at 0.312774552046, and Spearman(d_model) = +0.297 (p = 0.437). The normalized column is clean -- Spearman(params) = +0.017 (p = 0.966) -- and avg_adjacent_hsic's only near-significant association is Spearman(d_model) = +0.647 (p = 0.060), which n = 9 cannot settle. Note the synthetic path-refinement test DOES make avg_adjacent_hsic a depth counter at fixed geometry (0.839559308 at L = 4 -> 0.999264816 at L = 48), so its neutrality on real models (Spearman(n_layers) = +0.248, p = 0.520) is a property of real trajectories, not of the statistic.
- DEFECT: PAPER (not CODE): the manuscript's 'Normalization. Compression ratio is already a ratio in [0,1]' (metrics_geometry.tex:241) is FALSE for the manuscript's own formula. tr(K_0 K_{L-1})/tr(K_0 K_0) is a projection coefficient, not a correlation: setting layer L-1 = a * layer 0 makes it exactly a^2, measured 0.250000000 / 1.000000000 / 4.000000000 / 24.999999931 / 99.999999724 at a = 0.5/1/2/5/10, and 8 of 9 real models exceed 1 (max 67.371835 on SmolLM2-135M). The code is right and the sentence is wrong. The code says so at mutual_info.py:206-217; the .tex has not been updated.
- DEFECT: PAPER (not CODE): Eq. eq:HSIC (metrics_geometry.tex:228) writes the UNNORMALIZED (n-1)^-2 tr(K~^l K~^{l+1}) while adjacent_hsic / avg_adjacent_hsic / min_adjacent_hsic emit the NORMALIZED value. The code names its quantity correctly (class docstring: 'normalized HSIC ... equivalent to Linear CKA'), matches its cited source Kornblith 2019 to 5.4e-16, and the manuscript's own prose asserts the CKA equivalence -- but a reader taking Eq. eq:HSIC literally would compute a quantity that scales as the 4th power of the representation norm.
- DEFECT: CODE/DOC mutual_info.py:196-205: the note says hsic_deep_to_shallow_ratio is 'nCKA(anchor, L-1) / nCKA(anchor, 0)'. It is not: input_hsic starts at i = 1, so the denominator is nCKA(0, 1). Measured on [X, Y, 4X]: nCKA(0,1) = 0.195705995018, nCKA(0,2) = 1.000000000000, emitted ratio = 5.109705504473 = 1.000000000000 / 0.195705995018. Value is well-defined; only the docstring's index is wrong.
- DEFECT: CODE/NAMING mutual_info.py:218-220: hsic_compression_ratio is nCKA(0, L-1) -- a normalized alignment, not the manuscript's compression ratio, which now lives in hsic_compression_ratio_raw. Sitting next to a key that ends in _raw, the unsuffixed name reads as the primary quantity and is the more likely to be pooled with archived runs.
- DEFECT: CODE/DISCLOSURE mutual_info.py:192-195: adjacent_hsic and input_to_layer_hsic are emitted as raw LISTS whose length is n_layers - 1. They carry no _meta_ prefix, so whether they leak an absolute layer count into the feature matrix depends entirely on the downstream flattening in beyond_benchmarks, which this repo does not constrain.
- DEFECT: CODE/MINOR mutual_info.py:122-124: the fallback trim keyed on collected_layer_count only fires for a pre-hook cache. On gpt2 collected_layer_count returns 11 while 12 block outputs are collected, so if _last_is_block were ever mis-set to False a correct 12-layer collection would silently lose its deepest layer. The status flag is derived correctly today (n_swapped 16/16), so this is latent.
- FIX: No implementation change required for verdict GREEN. Non-blocking, in priority order: (1) PAPER -- delete or correct 'Compression ratio is already a ratio in [0,1]' in sections/metrics_geometry.tex:241; the measured counterexample is exact (layer L-1 = a * layer 0 gives exactly a^2; 67.371835 on SmolLM2-135M), and add that Eq. eq:HSIC as printed is the unnormalized form while the task reports the normalized one. (2) Report hsic_compression_ratio (Spearman(params) = +0.017, p = 0.966) rather than hsic_compression_ratio_raw (Spearman(n_layers) = +0.787, p = 0.012, 9474.3x range) as the headline, or report both with the decomposition raw = normalized * sqrt(tr(K_{L-1}^2)/tr(K_0^2)) stated, since that factor is 14.35 of gpt2's 11.23. (3) Fix the mutual_info.py:196-205 docstring: the denominator of hsic_deep_to_shallow_ratio is nCKA(0,1), not nCKA(0,0). (4) Rename hsic_compression_ratio to something like hsic_first_last_cka, so the pair is not read as {value, raw value} of the same quantity. (5) Prefix adjacent_hsic and input_to_layer_hsic with _meta_ or emit only their summaries, since their length is n_layers - 1.

### `geometry_hubness`

**codex — RED** · Radovanovic et al. define S_Nk as the standardized third moment of self-excluded k-NN occurrence counts, and the BLME manuscript selects cosine neighbors. On a fixed finite translated embedding fixture with 0/257 exact k10 boundary ties, an independent squared-chord calculation, cross-checked against the released neighbor code after removing its self hit, gives k10 skew/Gini 1.575612584051/0.538799981832, but evaluate() gives 2.788067312984/0.724652909204 (+76.951%/+34.494%) with 242/257 occurrence bins wrong (L1=3222); at translation 1e6, skew is 4.260635246241 versus 1.693383762627 (+151.605%).

- GitHub (PARTIAL): datapoet/hubminer; VarIr/scikit-hubness — The author-associated HubMiner repository was located, but its first official release is dated 2014-11-06 and the local environment has 1 Java runtime but 0 javac, Maven, or Gradle executables, so 0 author-code outputs were run. A clean local scikit-hubness clone at c36a058e67c34696182d3b8c5da089e287eb60bf was executed: after accounting for its score-path self-hit, ordinary k=5/10/50 occurrence arrays matched BLME in all 257 bins, skew differed by 0.0, and maximum Gini error was 5.55e-17; on the +1e3 fixture its adjusted k10 skew/Gini 1.575612584051/0.538799981832 also matched the independent answer and exposed BLME's 2.788067312984/0.724652909204. This is executed later third-party evidence, not a runnable paper-time author artifact, hence PARTIAL.
- Paper (PARTIAL): Radovanovic et al. publish six single-realization Euclidean S_N5 values for n=10000 and k=5 without a seed. Thirty fresh exact-Euclidean runs per condition gave mean+/-SD values for uniform d=3/20/100 of 0.166556+/-0.019876, 1.544048+/-0.063558, and 5.295652+/-0.951022 versus 0.121, 1.541, and 5.445 (+37.649%, +0.198%, -2.743%); normal d=3/20/100 gave 0.121465+/-0.023658, 2.049233+/-0.069962, and 10.220751+/-3.156233 versus 0.118, 2.055, and 19.210 (+2.936%, -0.281%, -46.795%). Four of six means reproduce within 2.94%, but the published normal-d100 value remains 2.848 local SD above the fresh mean and exceeds the fresh maximum 18.572612; merged defaults emit cosine k=10/50/100, so 0 shipped outputs use the numeric-anchor protocol.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): The 66-model archive shows material k10 Gini association with d_model (rho=-0.412576, p=0.00057646) and vocab_size (rho=+0.352119, p=0.00373819), but the fresh 50-pair 8x-vocabulary Gaussian control moves Gini only +0.495% and skew -3.439%. A separate 20-run fixed-V=4000 Gaussian width sweep from d=64 to 4096 moves mean Gini 0.173733->0.177184 (+1.986%) and skew 0.282806->0.320302 (+13.258%); architecture association is therefore real enough to disclose or residualize, but these controls do not show it dominating the learned signal.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY: hubness.py:113-150 forces the neighbor search through normalized float32 dot products; for the seed-20260907 257x24 fixture translated by +1e3, only 10 distinct off-diagonal similarity values remain, 24,566 pairs equal exactly 1, and 252/257 k10 boundaries become artificial ties although the float64 and long-double direct-distance references agree elementwise with 0 exact boundary ties. The resulting k10 count vector differs in 242 bins (L1=3222), moving skew 1.575612584051->2.788067312984 and Gini 0.538799981832->0.724652909204.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY AT THE SECOND REQUESTED TRANSLATION: at +1e6, float64 and long-double squared-chord references again have 0 occurrence mismatches and 0 exact k10 boundary ties, while current arithmetic has only 8 distinct off-diagonal similarities and artificial k10 ties in 252/257 rows. It changes 233/257 occurrence bins (L1=3694), skew 1.693383762627->4.260635246241 (+151.605%), Gini 0.539027085951->0.845299701737 (+56.820%), max 66->181, and top-1% mass 0.059922179->0.198832685.

The BLME add-on itself is correct: on the pinned vector it gives 0.5146666666666668 versus the independent pairwise definition 0.5146666666666667 (difference 1.11e-16), and task_metadata.py:54 now labels it accurately.

Cosine is explicitly allowed by the source and selected by the BLME manuscript, so this affects paper-number reproduction, not verdict.

At batch=2000 and V=262144, sims alone is 1.953125 GiB and the int64 argsort result adds 3.90625 GiB before sort workspace, contradicting both claims.

On 100 equal occurrence counts the archived percentile rule reports top-1% mass 1.0 while current exact-m reports 0.01, so archived top1pct fields must not be pooled with current output.
- FIX: Replace hubness.py:150's near-1 dot-product ranking with a cancellation-safe cosine ordering: for unit rows rank direct squared chord distances, ||u-v||^2, using chunked direct-difference accumulation, and recompute numerically ambiguous k/k+1 boundaries in higher precision while preserving the stable index tie rule. A local direct-chord candidate on the same stored inputs reduced +1e3 k10 count error from 242 bins/L1=3222 to 0/0 in float32, and a higher-precision direct implementation matched the long-double reference at all k=10/50/100 for both +1e3 and +1e6; add end-to-end regressions over both translations and all seven requested scales. Also reject or explicitly exclude zero/nonfinite rows, correct the Gini/topk memory documentation, expose the paper-number distance/k protocol only as an explicit config variant, add a size-matched null twin for max, and regenerate the 66 stale top1pct archive fields.

**opus5 — GREEN** · S_Nk is exactly Radovanovic et al. 2010's standardized third moment E(N_k - mu)^3/sigma^3 (verified against the fetched JMLR PDF, Sec. 3.2), and the whole pipeline reproduces an independent numpy reference (full stable argsort of cosine, self-masked) at 0.000e+00 on skew, max and top-1% mass over 3 seeds x 2 k values, with Gini agreeing with the pairwise definition to 8.33e-17. Cross-verified against the released scikit-hubness package run locally: BLME's occurrence vector is ELEMENTWISE IDENTICAL (np.array_equal True) to skhubness's own k_occurrence at k = 5, 10 and 50 once skhubness's self-hit is accounted for, and BLME's statistic kernel reproduces skhubness's k_skewness and gini to the last printed digit (0.403241817021 / 0.472377881684 / 0.058680214934 and 0.2157504 / 0.1447088 / 0.0685352). BLME uses the SELF-EXCLUDED convention, which is the one Radovanovic defines ('among the k nearest neighbours of all OTHER points'); skhubness's no-argument score() is the one that is wrong there. Arithmetic is exact across scale: skew/max/gini bit-identical (delta 0.00e+00) from alpha = 1e-40 to 1e+30, invariant to the requested k-set (1.7232808737106582 for k_values [2], [2,7], [2,7,10]) and to batch size (34.071788895 at batch 997/2000/8000 on gpt2).

- GitHub (PARTIAL): VarIr/scikit-hubness (run); datapoet/hubminer (authors', not run) — Neither cited paper ships code that I could run: Tomasev's own hubminer is Java (no javac here) and Radovanovic et al. 2010 released none. I cloned and RAN the third-party reference scikit-hubness, whose Hubness estimator implements exactly BLME's statistics: stats.skew(k_occurrence) and gini = sum_ij |x_i - x_j| / (2 n sum x). On 500 unit-norm 32-d points with metric='cosine', BLME's occurrence counts are elementwise identical to skhubness's at k = 5/10/50 modulo skhubness's self-hit (its score() with X=None calls _k_neighbors(X) with start=0, so the self hit is NOT filtered), and BLME's kernel returns skhubness's own numbers exactly. Marked PARTIAL rather than YES because scikit-hubness is a third-party reimplementation, not the code released with either paper.
- Paper (PARTIAL): Radovanovic et al. 2010 footnote 5 publishes six single-realisation S_N5 anchors (n = 10000, k = 5, Euclidean): uniform d = 3/20/100 -> 0.121 / 1.541 / 5.445 and normal d = 3/20/100 -> 0.118 / 2.055 / 19.210. Feeding exact Euclidean 5-NN counts through BLME's statistic kernel over 10 seeds: uniform 0.158576 +/- 0.013793, 1.536223 +/- 0.068358 (rel 0.31%), 5.025845 +/- 0.502066; normal 0.110002 +/- 0.013273, 2.066848 +/- 0.049240 (rel 0.58%), 9.761119 +/- 2.362696. Four of six sit within ~1 SD of the published single realisation; uniform d = 3 is 2.7 SD low and normal d = 100 is 4.0 SD high (both are the heaviest-tailed cases). A self-inclusion convention does NOT explain the d = 3 gap (it moves the mean to 0.187507, further away). PARTIAL also because the shipped configuration is cosine at k = 10/50/100, not Euclidean k = 5, so no shipped output is directly paper-comparable.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): Material but not dominant. On 61 archived models hubness_k10_gini has rho(d_model) = -0.4315 (and rho(vocab) = +0.3424) with joint architecture rank-OLS R^2 = 0.4611, while k10 skew is much weaker (R^2 = 0.1907). A pure vocabulary explanation is refuted: gpt2 and pythia-70m have 50,257 vs 50,304 rows yet fresh k10 skew is 34.071788895 vs 1.119447960 (30.4x) and gini 0.556695480 vs 0.319198457, while an 8x controlled Gaussian vocabulary sweep at d = 512 moves mean skew/gini only +5.3%/+1.7%. hubness_k*_max is the one output that is genuinely size-linked (+16.7% under the same null).

(1) Correct the hubness.py:72-84 docstring: drop the Gini attribution to Tomasev/Radovanovic (0 'gini' occurrences in the JMLR PDF) and say argsort, not topk. (2) Emit a zero-row count and exclude zero rows from both the query and the index, since cosine has no direction there. (3) Emit the exact k-boundary tie count (1/9/0 on gpt2 at k = 10/50/100) so the index tie-break is auditable, and document the convention. (4) Restore topk for the neighbour selection with an explicit boundary-tie repair, or scale batch_size by vocabulary -- the argsort's int64 index tensor is 4.19 GB at V = 262144 with the default batch of 2000, and OOMs outright at larger batches. (5) Report hubness_k*_max only against a size-matched null (+16.7% under an 8x vocabulary sweep). (6) Regenerate the 66 archived hubness rows before pooling them with current output.

### `geometry_intrinsic_dim`

**codex — RED** · Facco's Two-NN statistic depends only on mu=r2/r1 and is exactly invariant to positive global rescaling, but on the max-normalized default_rng(0) 500x32 Gaussian (500 distinct rows) the CPU fallback returns 24.233986884 at scale 1, 16.927948590 at 1e-22 (-30.1479%), and NaN at 1e-30, 1e-25, 1e25, and 2.406159650500376e38, while the released estimator on the same stored coordinates stays in 24.233986153-24.233986527. This value-changing counterexample makes the implementation non-faithful even though its ordinary-scale fit agrees with the released Ansuini implementation to at most 3.55e-15 on three adversarial fixtures.

- GitHub (YES): ansuini/IntrinsicDimDeep — Fetched the released IDNN/intrinsic_dimension.py and executed its one-based empirical-CDF, 90%-kept, origin-constrained regression locally. On ordinary, duplicate-coordinate, and equal-neighbor fixtures, BLME/released values were 6.416720901047417/6.416720901047420, 4.274974793707996/4.274974793707995, and 7.658184514358839/7.658184514358836 with 200/141/192 ratios retained, for maximum absolute disagreement 3.55e-15.
- Paper (YES): Facco et al. Fig. 1 reports the 10%-trimmed slopes 13.91 for a periodic 14-cube, 2.01 for a Swiss roll, and 22.16 for a radial Cauchy cloud in dimension 20 at N=2500. Ten fresh local realizations gave 13.934002 +/- 0.383016 (+0.1726%), 1.998271 +/- 0.035253 (-0.5835%), and 22.561274 +/- 0.325197 (+1.8108%), respectively.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): The 66-model middle-layer feature has rho=+0.52428979 with d_model, +0.44223548 with layer count, +0.39082529 with vocab_size, and +0.53623296 with log(params), all p<=0.00118. This is observational rather than a forced ambient-width effect: zero-padding one fixed 800x8 cloud to widths 64, 512, and 4096 moved the estimate only from 7.981259021 to 7.981259066 (relative span 5.73e-9), while the normalization registry has 0 entries for this task.

Forcing the fallback on the maintainer's reported seed-0 2000x16 and 2000x64 max-normalized Gaussians changes 14.742791384->4.795743969 (-67.4706%) and 37.722508336->25.908514098 (-31.3182%) at 1e-22, then returns NaN for both at 1e-23; the invariant measurements reproduce under the safe arithmetic but do not exercise or refute this shipped fallback.

Add a regression that forces torch.cuda.is_available() false and runs the exact 500x32 fixture at every SCALES value plus offsets 1e3/1e6, asserting all 500 ratios survive and agree with the released estimator; emit _meta_fit_sample_size. Correct task_metadata.py to identify one-based versus zero-based CDF ranks, add a real sample_size default or map num_samples in static mode, and reconcile the manuscript with the emitted layerwise 90%-trimmed statistic and absence of a d_model-normalized twin.

**opus5 — YELLOW** · The formula is Facco et al. 2017's linear-CDF Two-NN exactly (OLS through the origin of -log(1-F) on log mu, top 10% trimmed), and against skdim's released `_TwoNN` on the same real gpt2 cloud BLME reads 7.266648765114182 vs 7.09829793861948 (+2.37%), the documented mu<=1 pre-filter difference. But mu = r2/r1 is homogeneous of degree ZERO, so the estimand is EXACTLY invariant to a global rescale, and BLME is not, because `_compute_id` DOWNCASTS to float32 (intrinsic_dim.py:156-158) before `_two_nearest_neighbor_distances` promotes back to double -- defeating that function's own float64 rationale. On a 1000x8 integer lattice the float64 reference and BLME agree at 3.5847772232535577 at scale 1, 2, 3, 1e7, 1e-30, 1e-25 and at every power of two from 2^-100 to 2^+100, but BLME returns 5.028217657973436 at 1e-22 (+40.26%), 4.899603584081688 at 1e-23 (+36.68%), 5.028216765205537 at 1e-13, 4.8996032448629 at 1e13, 4.899603277516129 at 2e19, 4.899603311755563 at 1e25 and NaN at 2.406159650500376e38. The mechanism is exactly the one the gpt-5.6-sol RED pointed at: `mus = mus[mus > 1.0]` (line 183) drops exact ties, and whether a tie survives depends on the float32 rounding of the rescaled coordinates -- an exact binary rescale keeps them, a decimal one breaks them. Translation, also an exact invariance, fails harder on structured data: a 3-dimensional manifold in R^64 reads 3.1873385503162024 at the origin and 4.825032541136516 under +1e6 (+51.38%), a uniform 3-cube -21.02%, and a 5x-duplicated cloud with 1e-9 jitter goes finite -> NaN at +1e3. THE SPECIFIC gpt-5.6-sol NUMBERS DO NOT REPRODUCE: no fixture of mine yields ID ~24.23, nothing breaks at 1e-22 on a Gaussian cloud, and on REAL gpt2 hidden states (23272x768, fp32 and bf16) the profile is invariant to <=0.03% over 1e-30..1e25 -- but the property it claimed is broken IS broken, in the regime it guessed (duplicate-heavy data), at comparable magnitudes. On the paper's own domain the one surviving break is NaN at 2.406159650500376e38 on real gpt2 layers 0/5/11, from `overflow encountered in cast` at line 158.

- GitHub (YES): j-bac/scikit-dimension (skdim.id.TwoNN); efacco/TWO-NN — Ran the installed skdim 0.3.4 `skdim.id.TwoNN().fit(X).dimension_` head to head against BLME's `_compute_id` on the SAME real cloud -- gpt2's deepest block output from a bf16 checkpoint, 8718x768 -- giving skdim 7.09829793861948 against BLME 7.266648765114182, +2.3717%. That gap is BLME's `mus[mus > 1.0]` pre-filter (32 of 8654 mus dropped, plus 64 of 8718 rows with r1 == 0), which task_metadata already records as a deliberate divergence. I also built an independent float64 Two-NN (full pairwise distances, no float32 cast, same `_twonn_linear_fit`) and used it as the invariance oracle throughout; it agrees with BLME to the last digits wherever the float32 cast is harmless (3.5847772232535577 vs 3.584777223253557 on the lattice at scale 1).
- Paper (PARTIAL): Facco et al. 2017 publish Two-NN estimates for synthetic manifolds, not for a transformer's hidden states, so there is no like-for-like scalar. I reproduced the synthetic anchors instead: a 3-dimensional linear manifold embedded in R^64 reads 3.1873385503162024 (true 3, +6.2%), a uniform 3-cube 2.8051535971545243 (-6.5%), and an 8-dimensional manifold in R^768 reads 7.843645-8.048439 across widths (true 8, within 2%). Ansuini et al. 2019's ID profile shape -- a hunchback rising then falling -- is reproduced on real gpt2 (4.777 -> 7.658 at layer 8 -> 7.397 at layer 11), though the paper's CNN magnitudes are not the reference for a language model.
- Confound (NONE): Not a size proxy on either axis I could isolate. Ambient width: rho(d, ID) = +0.029 over d = 16..4096 at fixed intrinsic dimension 8 (span 1.079x, non-monotone). Hidden-state SCALE: invariant to <=0.03% over 1e-30..1e25 on real gpt2 layers 0, 5 and 11 (4.821678811821326 / 7.017021113680995 / 7.164204082928521 at scale 1, worst deviation -0.030% at any rung below 2.4e38). The residual dependence is on the point count (rho(N, ID) = -0.771, span 1.203x from N=100 to 20000), which the 20000-row cap largely equalizes across the zoo.
- DEFECT: CODE, intrinsic_dim.py:156-158: `X_tensor = X.detach().float()` / `torch.from_numpy(np.asarray(X, dtype=np.float32))` downcasts to float32, and `_two_nearest_neighbor_distances` then does `X.to(device).double()` -- so the float64 promotion whose own comment says it 'brings the error to ~1e-11' operates on data already rounded to float32. Two live consequences. (a) OVERFLOW: at 2.406159650500376e38 the cast emits `RuntimeWarning: overflow encountered in cast` and the estimate is NaN -- on REAL gpt2 hidden states at layers 0, 5 and 11, i.e. on the paper's own domain. (b) TRANSLATION: Two-NN is exactly translation invariant, but a 3-dimensional manifold in R^64 moves 3.1873385503162024 -> 4.825032541136516 (+51.38%) under +1e6, a uniform 3-cube 2.8051535971545243 -> 2.215419895064037 (-21.02%), and a near-duplicate cloud (base repeated 5x plus 1e-9 jitter) goes 1.3949953665081232 -> NaN under +1e3 because float32 spacing at 1e3 erases the jitter and every r1 becomes 0.
- DEFECT: CODE, intrinsic_dim.py:183 `mus = mus[mus > 1.0]`: exact ties (r1 == r2) are silently discarded, so on duplicate-heavy or lattice-like data the estimate depends on WHICH ties survive float32 rounding, and that depends on the scale. Measured on a 1000x8 integer lattice: 3.5847772232535577 at scales 1 / 2 / 3 / 1e7 / 1e-25 / 1e-30 and at every power of two 2^-100..2^+100, against 5.028217657973436 at 1e-22 (+40.26%), 4.899603584081688 at 1e-23, 4.8996032448629 at 1e13 and 4.899603311755563 at 1e25 and NaN at 2.406e38. A 5x-duplicated cloud with 1e-9 jitter swings 1.3949953665081232 (scale 1) -> 1.0047292931621026 (1e-13, -27.98%) -> 1.1023473426870238 (1e-30, -20.98%) against a float64 reference of 5.017652 -- i.e. the estimate is not merely scale-dependent there, it is wrong at every scale. On real gpt2 states 138 of 23272 rows have r1 == 0 and 39-68 mus are dropped, which is small enough (0.17-0.29%) that the shipped numbers are not materially affected.
- DEFECT: DOCUMENTATION vs CODE, intrinsic_dim.py:214-218: the comment justifies 'float64 + no-mm compute mode' as the fix for catastrophic cancellation, but the caller has already destroyed the extra precision. The comment is true of the function it annotates and false of the pipeline.
- DEFECT: CONFIG/ARCHIVE, tasks.yaml:197 `layerwise: true`: this branch runs for every archived model, and the file's own header records that all 66 blme_verified_a envelopes carry a `lid_layer_L` computed on ln_f(b_{L-1}). The current tree passes `replace_final_norm=True` on both routes and I confirmed `_meta_last_entry_is_block_output` is True on a live gpt2 run, so the fix is in place -- the archive still needs re-collecting.
- DEFECT: DOCUMENTED CLAIM UNDERSTATES THE GAP, docs/VERIFICATION_STATUS.md:21 states 'bit-identical to skdim _TwoNN on Pareto samples; fresh generic clouds 0.1-0.2%'. On a fresh REAL cloud -- gpt2's deepest block output, 8718x768 -- skdim gives 7.09829793861948 and BLME 7.266648765114182, +2.3717%, an order of magnitude outside the stated band. task_metadata.py:55 is the accurate record (0.328% at N=1000, 17.744% at N=20, attributed to the mu<=1 pre-filter); the status document is not.
- FIX: Stop downcasting in `_compute_id`: accept float64 through to `_two_nearest_neighbor_distances` (`X_tensor = X.detach().to(torch.float64)` / `np.asarray(X, dtype=np.float64)`), which removes the 2.406e38 NaN on real gpt2 states, restores exact translation invariance, and costs nothing since the device path immediately calls `.double()` anyway. Separately, make the tie handling scale-stable: rather than `mus > 1.0`, drop mus within a RELATIVE tolerance of 1 (e.g. `mus > 1 + 8*eps`) and emit the dropped count as `_meta_lid_n_tied_mus`, so a lattice-like cloud reports the same estimate at every scale instead of swinging 40%.

### `geometry_isoscore`

**codex — GREEN** · Rudman et al. Eq. 4.1 reduces independently to (tr(C)^2 / ||C||_F^2 - 1) / (d - 1); the current centered-SVD kernel agreed with that trace/Frobenius derivation to maximum absolute error 2.220e-16 on 7 adversarial clouds and with all 9 published Table 2 anchors to 2.220e-16. Across the requested 7 scale factors from 1e-30 through 2.406159650500376e38 its maximum relative drift was 8.204e-09; exact-preserving translations by 1e3 and 1e6 drifted at most 8.225e-16, and orthogonal rotation plus coordinate permutation each changed the score by 0.000e+00.

- GitHub (YES): bcbi-edu/p_eickhoff_isoscore (IsoScore==2.0.1) — I read the authors' repository and executed released IsoScore 2.0.1 against the same 7-cloud panel; BLME's maximum absolute disagreement was 3.805e-08 on the 300x16 anisotropic cloud, 2.030e-08 on the N<d 20x100 cloud, and 2.911e-08 on the rank-1 endpoint (BLME 0.000000000000 versus package 0.000000029110). At the 7 requested scales the package produced NaN, inf, or an SVD error at 5/7 points and returned 0.200000000000 at 1e13 versus BLME/independent 0.180462798457; after a 1e6 translation BLME returned 0.270052185029 versus the represented-cloud reference 0.270052185029 and package 0.270119220018.
- Paper (YES): Rudman et al. Table 2 publishes iota(I_9^(k))=(k-1)/8 for k=1..9, namely 0.000 through 1.000 in 0.125 steps. Exact paired-axis clouds passed through the current kernel reproduced all 9 numbers with maximum absolute error 2.220e-16, including k=5 at 0.500000000000, and the independent Eq. 4.1 reduction agreed within 2.220e-16 on 7 additional clouds.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): After removing 1 bit-identical duplicate checkpoint, all n=65 local blme_verified_a models resolved: raw IsoScore had Spearman rho=-0.427950 with d_model (p=3.765e-4), -0.307738 with n_layers (p=0.01264), -0.096859 with vocab_size (p=0.44275), and -0.462889 with log(parameters) (p=1.034e-4), with joint log-architecture R^2=0.365506. isoscore_effective_dims reduced those associations to -0.074703/-0.030938/-0.029466/-0.134215 (all p>=0.286) and R^2=0.027453; all 65 deduplicated archive results used num_samples=200 while the current merged study value is 1000, so this confirms the structural mechanism rather than a current-protocol model result.
- DEFECT: CODE/INTEGRATION at src/blme/tasks/geometry/isotropy.py:341-366: _cache_ok checks is_populated and document count but not whether hidden states exist. With configured num_samples=40, a populated logits-only cache holding 39 documents made 1 self-collection call and returned isoscore=0.685835853252, whereas the same cache holding exactly 40 made 0 self-collection calls and raised AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'float'; require a non-None hidden-state capability before selecting the cache route.
- DEFECT: CODE/AUXILIARY at src/blme/tasks/geometry/isotropy.py:217-236: _isoscore_null_floor claims to retain the requested N/d but caps only N. Generate the full N,d Gaussian in chunks while accumulating the mean and covariance moments, then compute the participation-ratio form without allocating Nxd.

- DEFECT: DOC/CONFIG at src/blme/tasks/configs/defaults.yaml:14-25 and src/blme/tasks/geometry/isotropy.py:378-488: the merged values are num_samples=1000 and min_points_per_dim=50, but comments say the ratio is enforced and NaN-gated while the code only reports a flag. The current 1000-document corpus yielded 134543 GPT-2 tokens, N/d=175.186 at d=768 but 32.847 at d=4096; a successful 20x3 run at N/d=6.667 still emitted isoscore=0.685835853252 with _meta_sampling_regime_ok=false. Correct the comments and use an explicit point budget if the study intends the 50x condition to hold across widths.

On a local 2-layer GPT-2 fixture with 8x24 tokens, hidden_states[-1] equalled ln_f(raw final block) to 0.000e+00, yet the representation choice changed IsoScore from 0.428438383209 to 0.446468175287 (+4.038%); emit _meta_isoscore_post_final_norm=true.

**opus5 — GREEN** · The kernel is untouched this round and remains the released IsoScore: _isoscore vs the installed IsoScore 2.0.1 package gives 0.9667461859774082 vs 0.9667461841527681 on an isotropic 2000x64 Gaussian (rel 1.89e-09), rel 2.64e-07 on a rank-8-in-R^64 cloud and 1.52e-05 under extreme anisotropy (where BLME is the MORE accurate of the two, because the official route squares the data into torch.cov before the SVD). The algebra matches Rudman et al. Eq. 4.1 term for term. This round's change is CONFIG only (study num_samples 200 -> 1000) and it does what it claims on gpt2: driving the real evaluate(), _meta_points_per_dim goes 30.30 -> 175.19 and _meta_sampling_regime_ok goes False -> True, with isoscore 0.000659629 -> 0.000791527 (+20.0%), reproducing the recorded figure exactly. The decision NOT to NaN-gate on the ratio also remains correct, and I re-derived why: at 1000 documents the corpus yields 134,543 gpt2 tokens, so points_per_dim = 134543/d clears the declared 50 only for d_model <= 2690 -- gating would still discard every model wider than that, i.e. most of the zoo's large end, to avoid a bias I measure at well under a percent on real states.

- GitHub (YES): IsoScore (PyPI 2.0.1; bcbi-edu/p_eickhoff_isoscore) — The official package is installed on this host. I read IsoScore.IsoScore's source (torch.cov -> svdvals -> pcs*sqrt(n)/||pcs|| -> defect -> rescale) and ran it head-to-head against _isoscore on four clouds; worst relative disagreement 1.52e-05, on the case where the official's cov-then-SVD route loses precision. BLME's only deliberate departures are the float64 promotion, the S/S[0] rescale (both exactly cancelling) and a [0,1] clamp the reference lacks.
- Paper (YES): Rudman et al.'s defining anchors reproduce: an isotropic Gaussian at n >> d scores 0.9667 against the paper's -> 1 limit (the finite-N floor at this (n,d) is 0.9668, so the value sits AT the floor), and a k-dimensional cloud embedded in d ambient dimensions scores (k/d - 1/d)/(1 - 1/d) -- 0.110955 at d=64 against the closed form 0.111111 (0.14%) and 0.0017070 at d=4096 against 0.00170898 (0.11%).
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Raw isoscore rho -0.433 with d_model (p=2.8e-04), -0.310 with n_layers, -0.463 with log-params over 66 models; the zero-padding isometry shows the association is the structural 1/(d-1) factor. isoscore_effective_dims removes it (rho -0.089, p=0.48) and is invariant under the same isometry to 8.4e-15 (7.990170056143993 at d=64 and 7.9901700561439215 at d=4096).
- DEFECT: 1000 IS NOT ENOUGH, AND WHETHER IT IS ENOUGH DEPENDS ON THE MODEL. Driving the real evaluate() at N = 200 / 1000 / 2000 documents: gpt2 isoscore 0.000659629 / 0.000791527 / 0.000794920 -- +20.0% then +0.43%, so gpt2 has converged by 1000. pythia-160m, the SAME width (768) and the same 177 points per dimension, gives 0.139130920 / 0.064517918 / 0.089038856 -- -53.6% then **+38.0%**, and it is not even monotone (isoscore_effective_dims 107.71 -> 50.49 -> 69.29). A 38% move between 1000 and 2000 documents on one of the two models tested means the shipped 1000 does not pin the value, and the residual bias is model-dependent, which is exactly the property that made 200 unusable.
- DEFECT: THE CONFIG CHANGE SILENTLY BROKE `_meta_isoscore_null_floor`, the field that exists so a reader can judge the sampling regime. _isoscore_null_floor caps the synthetic draw at 40M cells, and its docstring says the subsample 'carries the same bias' because it holds N/d fixed -- it does not; it holds N*d fixed and therefore CHANGES N/d. At 200 documents (N=23,272, 17.9M cells) the cap did not bind and the reported floor was exact. At the new 1000 it does: reported 0.985425757 against the true floor at (134543, 768) of 0.994294470, understating it by -0.892%; at 2000 documents reported 0.985425757 against a true 0.996808505, -1.142%. Both N=1000 and N=2000 report the IDENTICAL floor, which is the fingerprint. Immaterial for gpt2 (whose isoscore is 1000x below the floor) but the field is now wrong precisely in the regime the change created.
- DEFECT: CONFIG, still open at the wide end: at 1000 documents the realized supply is 134,543 tokens (gpt2 tokenizer, 512-token window), so _meta_sampling_regime_ok is False for every model with d_model > 2690 -- measured points_per_dim 32.8 at d=4096, 26.3 at 5120, 25.0 at 5376, 18.8 at 7168, all under the declared min_points_per_dim: 50. Models with larger vocabularies produce FEWER tokens for the same corpus, so the real figure for Llama/Qwen-class checkpoints is worse than this.
