The code deliberately does NOT gate -- there is a 20-line comment saying so 400 lines away. The config file documents behaviour the code was changed to stop having.

- DEFECT: isoscore_effective_dims IS the covariance participation ratio (verified: iso*(d-1)+1 equals (sum lam)^2/sum(lam^2) to rel 0.0 / 7.8e-16 / 2.2e-16 on three clouds), which geometry_svd already emits as participation_ratio. The two must not both enter a feature matrix.

Sweep num_samples on at least one more model before treating 1000 as converged (pythia-160m still moves +38.0% from 1000 to 2000), and either raise it or emit a per-run convergence check. Delete the stale 'NaN-gated below it' sentence from defaults.yaml, and record the 200->1000 change as archive-incompatible in the study config note.

### `geometry_lid`

**codex — GREEN** · Ma et al. Eq. 4 is exactly -k/sum_i log(r_i/r_k): the paper derivation and BLME both give 1.5338845123190095 on distances [1,2,3,4,5]. Across the seven requested scales from 1e-30 through 2.406159650500376e38, BLME agreed with the released mle_batch arithmetic to maximum relative error 3.9224492549645126e-16; translations 0/1e3/1e6 were identical at 17.96404775763491, the former 100x2048 adversary was 22.144526420679867 versus 22.144526420679885 (8.021651967871311e-16), and a 160-case hostile sweep had 0/800 statistic mismatches above 1e-10.

- GitHub (YES): https://github.com/xingjunm/lid_adversarial_subspace_detection — I fetched util.py and executed its lines 411-420 locally: float32 cast, scipy cdist, full row sort, removal of the first self-distance, and -k/sum(log(v/v[-1])). BLME's mean matched that released arithmetic within 3.9224492549645126e-16 over 7 extreme scales, exactly at all 3 translations, and within 8.021651967871311e-16 on the former 2048-dimensional selector counterexample, for which all 100/100 rows correctly escalated.
- Paper (NONE): Ma et al. Figure 1 prints approximate LID values 1.53 and 4.36, but publishes 0 machine-readable point coordinates or noise realization for that illustration, and its CIFAR/MNIST/SVHN detector tables do not publish a transformer-hidden-state LID scalar. The independently reproducible Eq. 4 anchor is 1.5338845123190095, but it is a formula check rather than reproduction of a published fixture.

With only 4 models and 2 distinct widths those observational values cannot confirm a size proxy, while the exact width-10-to-4096 intervention gives raw span 0.0; the vocabulary/family association and model-dependent 0.9494431949628122-1.2099737088068487 representation-mode ratios keep confounding suspected rather than cleared.

A config request max_queries=37 also still emitted 500 estimates because the helper's cap is not forwarded.
- DEFECT: CODE/PROTOCOL, lid.py:257-327: layerwise=true reports the raw deepest block while layerwise=false reports the post-final-norm representation under the same lid_mean/lid_std/lid_min/lid_max/lid_median names. **opus5 — GREEN** · THE DUPLICATE IS GONE AND THE REMAINING KEY SET IS DUPLICATE-FREE, WHICH I CHECKED EXHAUSTIVELY RATHER THAN BY READING. `lid_last_layer` no longer exists (lid.py:279-285 states why). Running the STUDY config (num_samples 200, layerwise True, shared cache) on gpt2 and pythia-160m, the layerwise output is {lid_mean, lid_std, lid_min, lid_max, lid_median, layer_lid_mean, lid_first_layer, lid_mid_layer} plus five `_meta_` keys, and a pairwise scan over every unprefixed scalar finds ZERO exactly-equal pairs on either model. The three depth-aligned scalars are distinct by construction and measured distinct: gpt2 lid_first_layer 11.606143066708176 (= layer_lid_mean[0]), lid_mid_layer 15.14286444038038 (= layer_lid_mean[6]), lid_mean 15.279754746373662 (= layer_lid_mean[11]). The list `layer_lid_mean` is summarised by the study pipeline into mean/std/min/max/slope/q25/q50/q75, none of which is its last element, so nothing in the flattened feature matrix duplicates `lid_mean` either -- except accidentally on 3 of the 66 archived models, where the deepest layer happens to be the maximum. `_meta_lid_mode` is present and correct on BOTH modes ('layerwise' with the cache, 'single_layer' without), and `_meta_lid_max_over_median` is present. The estimator itself is unchanged and remains bit-exact against xingjunm's `mle_batch`.

- GitHub (YES): xingjunm/lid_adversarial_subspace_detection (util.py mle_batch) — Parity against the released `mle_batch` is already executed and pinned in this repo (tests/tasks/parity, LID vs mle_batch 2.2e-9; 0.000e+00 on the all-query mean against full exact search with duplicates retained). - Paper (PARTIAL): Ma et al. 2018 publish LID values for CIFAR/SVHN adversarial-subspace detection, not for transformer hidden states, so there is no directly reproducible figure. The reproducible anchor is the estimator's known bias factor: BLME implements the -k (Ma et al. Eq. 4) variant, which is k/(k-1) times the Levina-Bickel MLE, i.e. +5.26% at the shipped k=20 and +11.1% above the (k-2) bias-corrected form -- this is stated in the docstring and is the correct relationship.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Layerwise `lid_mean` on the 61-model archive is Spearman +0.507 with d_model (p=3.1e-05), +0.442 with n_layers and +0.420 with parameter count. That is expected -- an intrinsic-dimension estimate is bounded above by the ambient dimension -- but it is a first-class feature column with no d_model-normalized twin. The single-layer mode is much milder on a 14-model panel (+0.206 with d_model, p=0.48) which is itself evidence that the two modes are different quantities.

It is the estimator's floor for query points with degenerate neighbourhoods, not a measurement, and `is_excluded_predictor('geometry_lid.lid_min')` returns KEPT.

(2) Give the two modes distinct key names (e.g. `lid_mean_output_repr` for single-layer, `lid_mean_block_last` for layerwise) rather than relying on a `_meta_` tag to disambiguate columns that a pooled feature matrix will merge anyway. (3) Prefix `lid_min` with `_meta_`: 0.0 exactly on 8/14 models, a floor rather than a measurement. (4) Guard `lid_mid_layer` against n <= 2, where it coincides with `lid_mean` by construction.

### `geometry_lipschitz`

**codex — RED** · The detailed BLME source defines each L_l over all n rows as ||delta h||/(||h||+epsilon), but the headline implementation drops zero-norm rows and uses ||delta h||/||h||; only one metadata mean uses epsilon. At epsilon=1e-8, [0,1]->[0,3] returns headline mean/max/fraction 2.000000000/2.000000000/0.000000 versus 0.999999990/0.999999990/1.000000, while zero->zero returns NaN/NaN/NaN versus 0.000000000/0.000000000/1.000000; the fraction disagreement holds for every epsilon>0.

- GitHub (NONE): https://github.com/avirmaux/lipEstimation — I inspected the local official avirmaux/lipEstimation checkout at commit 336b6ccfecec6d965fda1798da8f9e204998766b: its 22 tracked Python files contain 0 hidden-state relative-change or contractive-fraction routines. The dedicated live comparison passed 15/15 tests and both its matrix-power routine and BLME's exact SVD returned sigma_max=4.000000000 on diag(-3,0.5,2,-1.25,4), but that is an operator/spectral-norm kernel rather than this diagnostic, so there are 0 released like-for-like outputs.
- Paper (NONE): The 2 cited motivation papers publish 0 transformer hidden-state relative-change values, and searches of both full texts found 0 occurrences of 'transformer', 'relative change', or 'contractive'; the BLME manuscript supplies 1 displayed definition and 0 model values. The internal k=3 anchor is numerically sound at 0.5874010519681995 versus 4^(1/3)-1=0.5874010519681994 (absolute error 1.11e-16), but it is not a published target number.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): The causal depth intervention leaves the endpoint exactly 4.000000x but gives means 3.000000000000/1.000000000000/0.587401051968/0.319507910773/0.189207115003/0.122462048309/0.059463094359 and rho(depth)=-1.000000. A common coordinate shift of 0/1e3/1e6 leaves ||delta h||=12.449899597989 but moves the reported ratio 3.327375628243/0.007183148964/0.000007187948 (462910.36x), while the four real models give Spearman rho(mean,depth)=-0.894427 and rho(contractive_fraction,depth)=+0.942809; these are comparability confounds, not implementation-verdict evidence.
- DEFECT: VERDICT-DRIVING SOURCE MISMATCH (CODE: src/blme/tasks/geometry/lipschitz.py:165-216,250-283; SOURCE: beyond_benchmarks/paper/sections/metrics_geometry.tex:101). The displayed definition includes +epsilon and all n rows, while the public mean/max/fraction omit epsilon and filter input_norms==0. With the code's epsilon=1e-8, 1->2 emits 1.000000000/1.000000000/0.000000 versus 0.999999990/0.999999990/1.000000; [0->0,1->3] emits 2.000000000/2.000000000/0.000000 versus 0.999999990/0.999999990/1.000000; and zero->zero emits NaN/NaN/NaN versus 0/0/1. The metadata epsilon mean is correct at 0.999999990 on the mixed fixture, but no matching epsilon-based max or fraction is emitted. The compact catalogue at metrics_catalogue.tex:31 omits epsilon, so the prose is internally inconsistent, but the explicit displayed formula is not implemented by the primary outputs.
- DEFECT: EXTREME SCALE DEFECT (CODE: lipschitz.py:150-166). On finite, nonzero float32 states h0=s*[1,-2,3]/6 and h1=s*[-4,5,-6]/6, the requested scales 1e-30/1e-25/1e-13/1/1e13/1e25/2.406159650500376e38 produce 3.327375628243/3.327375564314/3.327375493896/3.327375582592/3.327375500662/3.327375702890/NaN. An independent reference that promotes the represented states before subtraction gives 3.327375628243/3.327375602020/3.327375594267/3.327375599865/3.327375625591/3.327375652772/3.327375608177: the first 6 agree within 3.755e-8 relative, but the last fails because h_next-h_l overflows before _rownorm performs its float64 cast.
- DEFECT: FAILED-HOOK FALLBACK PUBLISHES AN INCOMPLETE OR WRONG POPULATION (CODE: lipschitz.py:132-140). With last_is_block=false, a true three-update profile [1,2,3] is truncated and reported as mean/max 1.500000/2.000000 instead of 2.000000/3.000000; for a one-block model with embedding=1, raw block output=2, and stored post-final-norm output=80, the len>2 exception reports 79.000000 instead of the raw update 1.000000. The false provenance flag is truthful and the hook succeeded on 4/4 real checkpoints, but either reachable fallback should fail closed rather than publish a non-all-L headline.
- DEFECT: NON-FINITE INPUTS ARE SILENTLY COERCED (CODE: lipschitz.py:44-48,185-215). _rownorm treats a row whose maximum is NaN as a zero-norm row; combining a valid [1,0]->[2,0] row with [1,0]->[NaN,3] therefore reports mean/fraction 0.500000/1.000000 instead of the finite-row-only 1.000000/0.000000. This is a robustness failure outside the finite-paper domain and was not used to make the implementation verdict RED.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY/DEPTH AND ORIGIN. The same x->4x function changes mean by 50.4515x over 1 to 24 updates and flips contractive_fraction from 0.000000 to 1.000000 between 2 and 3 updates. Independently, translating both states by 1e6 changes 3.327375628243 to 0.000007187948 while preserving the update norm at 12.449899597989; matched block decomposition and representation origin are required for cross-model use.
- DEFECT: NORMALIZED VARIANT FAILS A FUNCTION-PRESERVING CONTROL (CODE: lipschitz.py:264-267). For x->3x, [1,2,3] reports max=1.000000000, max_layer_frac=0.000000000, and mean=0.750000000; prefixing two identity updates as [1,1,1,2,3] preserves the endpoint and max exactly but moves max_layer_frac to 0.666666666667 and mean to 0.375000000. Runtime NORMALIZERS has 0 entries, so there is also no normalized relative_change_mean/max twin; the emitted fractional-position metadata is RED rather than a successful cross-depth correction.
- DEFECT: CONFIG/PROTOCOL AXIS ONLY. resolve_task_config('geometry_lipschitz') returns exactly {'num_samples': 20}; the study file overrides this to 200 documents, and 0 epsilon keys are configurable even though the metadata path hard-codes 1e-8. On the current 20-document corpus slice, GPT-2 tokenizers produce 1702 tokens and Pythia tokenizers 1693, so a document count does not fix the scored row population; none of these configuration facts contributes to the implementation RED.
- DEFECT: CACHE/FRESH HOT PATH IS CLOSED. On the same 20 eligible WikiText-103 passages (7192 characters), distilgpt2/pythia-70m/gpt2/pythia-160m yield 7/7/13/13 states and 1702/1693/1702/1693 tokens, the raw-final-block hook is true on 4/4, and cache versus fresh differs by 0.000e+00 across all 7 checked scalars. Current means are 2.232131619011/3.229663468120/1.313293224871/1.968557698757, and independent float64 recomputation differs by at most 3.5932e-9, confirming the embedding and successful-hook routes rather than the failed-hook fallback.
- DEFECT: CACHE HOOK LEAK FIX IS CLOSED (CODE: src/blme/cache.py:440-503). **opus5 — GREEN** · The code computes the manuscript's relative-change ratio L_l = (1/n) sum_i ||h_i^(l+1) - h_i^(l)|| / ||h_i^(l)|| together with its mean, its maximum over l, and the fraction of contractive transitions (L_l < 1) -- all three keys present -- over ALL L residual updates including embedding -> block 0 and the hook-recovered RAW deepest block (_meta_last_entry_is_block_output = true on 4/4 models I ran). It is EXACT against a closed form: factorising the map x -> 4x into k identical blocks makes every L_l equal 4^(1/k) - 1, and BLME returns 3.000000000000 / 1.000000000000 / 0.587401051968 / 0.319507910773 / 0.189207115003 / 0.122462048309 / 0.059463094359 at k = 1/2/3/5/8/12/24, relative error 0.00e+00 at EVERY k. THE EPSILON DISPUTE, SETTLED BY MEASUREMENT ON REAL DATA: I ran the shipped task end to end on gpt2, distilgpt2, pythia-70m and pythia-160m and compared the headline against the paper's literal (||h||+eps) form, which the code now also emits as _meta_relative_change_mean_paper_eps -- 1.313293224871 vs 1.313293222933 (rel 1.476e-09), 2.232131619011 vs 2.232131615812 (1.433e-09), 3.229510209745 vs 3.229510179807 (9.270e-09), 1.968497502589 vs 1.968497485699 (8.580e-09). On the paper's own domain the two definitions are numerically indistinguishable to nine digits, so there is no value-changing counterexample inside it. Outside it the epsilon form is the WRONG one to prefer: on the fixed pair ([1,-2,3],[-4,5,-6]) -> ([2,-1,1],[1,1,1]) the epsilon-free ratio is exactly 0.8678893129713239 at scales 1, 1e-2, 1e-6, 1e-8, 1e-10, 1e-13, 2^-80 and 2^70, while the paper's literal form reads 0.8678893114804780 -> 0.8664015001372745 -> 0.0554133315918974 -> 0.0000596769934102 over scales 1 / 1e-6 / 1e-10 / 1e-13, a 15.66x collapse at 1e-10 -- contradicting the SAME manuscript paragraph (metrics_geometry.tex:103) that asserts 'The relative-change ratio is already dimensionless', and contradicting the SAME manuscript's own catalogue entry (metrics_catalogue.tex:31), which writes the formula WITHOUT any epsilon. The manuscript also never assigns epsilon a value, so its displayed form is not a fully specified estimand while the code's is. I ADJUDICATE FOR THE CODE against round-5 codex: the code implements the invariance its source claims, matches the source's own compact definition verbatim, and reproduces the source's displayed form to <= 9.3e-09 on every real model; metrics_geometry.tex:101 should drop '+ epsilon'.

- GitHub (NONE):  — No code was ever released for this quantity. The two cited motivation papers (Miyato et al. 2018; Virmaux & Scaman 2018) release spectral-normalisation and operator-norm estimation code, which computes a DIFFERENT quantity (a sup over input pairs / a spectral-norm bound), not a per-token relative-change ratio over hidden states; the module docstring correctly disclaims the identification. The verifiable anchor is therefore analytic, and it is exact (4^(1/k) - 1 at seven values of k, relative 0.00e+00).
- Paper (NONE): The BLME manuscript states the definition at metrics_geometry.tex:101 and metrics_catalogue.tex:31 and publishes no numeric value for it; the two cited motivation papers publish operator-norm and spectral-norm figures for a different quantity. The reproducible anchor is internal and analytic: an exact k-block factorisation of x -> 4x returns 4^(1/k) - 1 to relative 0.00e+00 at k = 1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 12 and 24.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): The dependence on depth is structural, not merely correlational: holding the end-to-end function EXACTLY fixed and changing only the block count moves relative_change_mean by 50.4x (3.000000000 at k=1 to 0.059463094 at k=24). On the four-model panel the headline runs 1.313293 (gpt2, 12 layers), 1.968498 (pythia-160m, 12), 2.232132 (distilgpt2, 6), 3.229510 (pythia-70m, 6), i.e. Spearman rho(n_layers) = -0.894 with the two 6-layer models on top -- exactly the sign the fixed-function intervention predicts.
- DEFECT: CODE (latent, fabricates a legal value), lipschitz.py:148-154: `_rownorm` initialises its output to zero and selects `nz = (max|M| > 0)`, so a row whose maximum is NaN takes the false branch and is assigned norm 0.0 -- I verified directly that `_rownorm([[nan, 1.0]])` returns 0.0. When the NaN is in the DIFFERENCE the emitted ratio becomes exactly 0.0, a legal relative-change value, instead of being withheld; this contradicts the file's own degenerate-layer branch, which was changed to NaN precisely to avoid a sentinel that looks legal. Latent: no NaN rows on any of the four models.
- DEFECT: PAPER (action required in the paper repo), sections/metrics_geometry.tex:101: the displayed L_l carries '+ epsilon' with no value assigned, while metrics_catalogue.tex:31 writes the same quantity WITHOUT epsilon and metrics_geometry.tex:103 asserts the ratio is 'already dimensionless'. Only the epsilon-free form satisfies the latter two: measured 0.8678893129713239 at every scale from 2^-80 to 2^70 against the epsilon form's 0.0554133315918974 at 1e-10. The manuscript, not the code, should change.
- DEFECT: COVERAGE (fallback path), lipschitz.py:115-127: when the deepest-block hook does not fire the code drops the LAST collected entry and publishes a headline over L-1 of the L residual updates; with only two collected entries the `len(layer_indices) > 2` guard skips the trim and a LayerNorm output is scored as a residual update. Disclosed via _meta_last_entry_is_block_output, which read true on all four models.
- DEFECT: SCALE BOUNDARY, lipschitz.py:141-157: `h_next - h_l` is formed in the STORED dtype before `_rownorm` promotes to float64, so a float32 input near 2^127 overflows in the subtraction. Outside tests/test_scale_invariance.py's stated ladder (max 1e20) and outside float32's own representable pair range.
- DEFECT: CODE (cosmetic), lipschitz.py:148: `_rownorm` is redefined here and duplicates `utils.safe_norm`, the shared scale contract, so this task is invisible to a grep for that contract even though the 13/13 scale suite passes.

### `geometry_magnitude`

**codex — RED** · On 3 distinct clouds and 291 matched scales, current `_magnitude` agrees with released Magnipy to maximum absolute error 2.842170943040401e-14 and maximum relative error 1.005620538400775e-15. But Limbeck et al.'s twin invariant requires X={0,1} and Z={0,0,1} to agree: at t=ln(19), BLME returns 1.900000000000 for X and 1.886569920263 for Z (-0.706846%), while released high-level Magnipy de-duplicates Z from 3 rows to 2 and returns 1.900000000000 for both, so this is a concrete implementation-fidelity RED.

- GitHub (YES): aidos-lab/magnipy — Executed the clean released checkout at commit 7d49b90f110db42fe666ada2964fea020d2cb1dd: its 5 upstream tests passed, and its Cholesky distance-matrix path matched BLME over 3 clouds x 97 scales with maximum absolute/relative errors 2.842170943040401e-14/1.005620538400775e-15. Its high-level `Magnipy` path removed the 1 duplicate from the 3-row twin, used a 2x2 distance matrix, and returned X/Z curves with maximum difference 0.000e+00; BLME's non-deduplicating path differed by -0.357594% to -0.743772% over t=0.01 to 10.
- Paper (PARTIAL): Limbeck et al. Table S.1 publishes MAGAREA=4.602 for both the two-point X and duplicate-point Z. On the paper's 10-point linear grid to t_cut=ln(19), BLME gives X=4.601552722569 (rounds to 4.602; -0.009719% versus the table) but the paper-ordered Z=[1,0,0] gives 4.582358960046 (-0.426794% versus 4.602 and -0.417115% versus X), with equivalent row permutations falling as low as 4.574598610416; released Magnipy gives 4.601552722569 for both, so one published entry reproduces and its required twin does not.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Across all 66 locally re-read historical envelopes (each git_hash 33743e8, 100 points, 10 scales), `magnitude_auc` has Spearman rho=+0.501605/+0.418114/+0.649968/+0.577388 with d_model/n_layers/vocabulary/log-parameters (p=1.78e-5/4.77e-4/3.52e-9/3.87e-7), while `magnitude_median_scale` has rho=-0.611451 with vocabulary (p=4.94e-8) and -0.553564 with log-parameters (p=1.43e-6). The former cache confound is closed: a current `evaluate()` fixture at configured n=100 returned area/auc/dimension=127.79791353920334/12.763428776058642/1.7931811614539663 with maximum difference 0.000e+00 for no cache and 50/100/200/1000-document caches; only the exact 100-document cache was read, so the remaining measured channel is representation scale/architecture, not cache size.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY, magnitude.py:78, 116-135, 461-472: the public task never converts its row multiset to the finite metric set required by Definitions 3.1-3.3 and the twin property. For X rows [0,1] and sorted Z rows [0,0,1], released Magnipy removes the duplicate and has max |curve_X-curve_Z|=0.000e+00; BLME instead solves a singular 3x3 zeta after adding 1e-2 I and returns 1.886569920263 rather than 1.900000000000 at t=ln(19) (-0.706846%), paper-grid MAGAREA 4.574598610416 rather than 4.601552722569 (-0.585761%), and registered `magnitude_area` 18.482779875728 rather than 18.613780544942 (-0.703783%).
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY/SYMMETRY, magnitude.py:116-128: singular-matrix branch selection depends on row order. Three permutations of exactly the same Z have a maximum magnitude spread 0.014872495679 at t=9.011018251665; [0,0,1] versus [1,0,0] gives paper-grid areas 4.574598610416 versus 4.582358960046, registered AUCs 1.978316541368 versus 1.280185028403, and 10 versus 8 accepted ladder scales. At t=1.96295931944, one permutation raises and takes the 1e-2 perturbation at condition number 7.79e16 while the other accepts a direct solve at condition number 1.28e32, violating the paper's explicit permutation symmetry.
- DEFECT: CODE/PROTOCOL, not verdict leakage, magnitude.py:291-321: the effective merged configuration is num_samples=100, n_scales=10, scale_span=10.0, layer_idx=-1, but registered `magnitude_area` integrates 512 points to a per-cloud hard-coded 10/median(distance) cutoff. The paper uses a common 95%-cardinality convergence cutoff; on its unit two-point example the registered area is 18.613780544942 versus the matching paper-grid value 4.601552722569 (+304.510861%), so the registered absolute number is not the paper's MAGAREA protocol even where the kernel is exact.

On the current 20x5 fixture, s=1e-30 to 2.406159650500376e38 moves AUC 1.886e-14 to 13.795856951, dimension 1.345e-14 to 0, and area 1.163e32 to 4.835e-37; the 66-model historical rho for AUC versus d_model/vocabulary/log-parameters is +0.501605/+0.649968/+0.577388.

- DEFECT: CACHE FIX VERIFIED / RESIDUAL COST, magnitude.py:418-447 and core.py:215-249/345-367: the equality guard now rejects 50, 200, and 1000 cached documents for configured n=100, accepts exactly 100, passes replace_final_norm=false and max_tokens_per_doc=None, and all 5 branch-controlled outputs are bit-identical (maximum difference 0.000e+00). Their AUC correlations are useful confound evidence (+0.501605/+0.418114/+0.649968/+0.577388), but 0 of 66 can certify current implementation outputs and the panel must be rerun before pooling with new results.
- FIX: Before any row subsampling, collapse exact duplicate hidden-state rows with `np.unique(X, axis=0)` (preserving deterministic selection from the unique set), use the quotient cardinality in every denominator, and emit raw/unique/duplicate counts; do not tolerance-cluster distinct points. Add end-to-end tests against high-level Magnipy for X/Z equality, all row permutations, Table S.1's 4.602/4.602 pair, and the 7 requested scales plus 1e3/1e6 translations; a low-level singular-D parity test is not sufficient. Keep the exact cache guard, but either provide an exact 100-document cache slice or report the actual self-collected document count. Separately preserve the raw scale-dependent quantities, add a distinctly named promoted scale-free output only after it passes both the 8.78e-9 scale and 0.000e+00 twin bars, implement the paper's shared 95%-convergence cutoff at orchestration level (or rename the current 10/median window as a BLME auxiliary), correct the <=3.6e-15 duplicate claims, and rerun all 66 historical envelopes.

**opus5 — YELLOW** · On a genuine metric space the kernel is EXACT: BLME's `_magnitude` against the installed magnipy at matched t gives worst relative deviation 7.654812283983186e-16 over 2x1, 20x3, 60x16 and 100x64 clouds at 8 scales each, `_median_heuristic_scale` is BIT-IDENTICAL to magnipy's (0.9733630757834314 and 0.6100329519772284), and the Leinster two-point closed form |tX| = 2/(1+e^{-td}) is reproduced to 2.2e-16 at t = 0.01/0.1/1/10/100. But I CONFIRM the still-open gpt-5.6-sol RED, on the paper's own twin example. Magnitude is an invariant of the metric QUOTIENT, so duplicating a point must not change it; magnipy enforces this explicitly (it prints 'Out of the 3 observations in X, only 2 are unique.' and returns a BIT-IDENTICAL magnitude vector for X = {0, 1} and Z = {0, 0, 1}, `magnipy identical: True`). BLME keeps the singular 3x3 zeta, falls through `np.linalg.solve` to the Bunch perturbation `zeta + 1e-2 I` (magnitude.py:78, 128-131), and returns a DIFFERENT number: 1.454154504046096 against 1.4621171572600098 at t = 1 (-0.5446%), and -0.3890% / -0.4637% / -0.6556% / -0.7438% at t = 0.1 / 0.5 / 2 / 10. On the shipped summary that is `magnitude_area` 18.482779875727587 against 18.613780544942244, -0.703783% -- the same -0.7% the RED quoted. It is not confined to the toy: a 30-point cloud in R^8 with every point doubled deviates -0.186108% (t=0.1) to -0.497512% (t=10) where magnipy is again bit-identical. YELLOW rather than RED because the deviation is sub-1%, confined to degenerate inputs, and the estimator is exact everywhere else. THE CACHE-GUARD FIX I WAS ASKED TO VERIFY IS REAL AND EXACT: driving the production evaluate() five ways -- alone, with cache_num_samples 50 / 100 / 1000, with a geometry_rsa co-runner, and with a geometry_isoscore co-runner that sizes the shared corpus to 1000 documents -- `magnitude_area` is 5.097372834840682 in ALL FIVE, bit-identical, with `_meta_magnitude_source` = 'self_collected' and `_meta_magnitude_n_docs` = 100 every time. The previously reported oversized-cache read of 5.689075 (+11.6%) is gone.

- GitHub (YES): aidos-lab/magnipy — Ran the installed magnipy head to head. (a) `Magnipy(X, ts=...).get_magnitude()` vs BLME `_magnitude(D, t)` at matched t: worst relative deviation 7.654812283983186e-16 over four clouds x 8 scales (e.g. 13.670158877232653 vs 13.670158877232653 on 60x16, 71.94158613404554 vs 71.94158613404555 on 100x64). (b) `magnipy.magnitude.scales.median_heuristic_from_distances` vs BLME `_median_heuristic_scale`: bit-identical on both clouds tested. (c) The duplicate-point case, which is where they diverge: magnipy de-duplicates (its own message: 'Out of the 3 observations in X, only 2 are unique.') and returns identical vectors for a cloud and its duplicated twin in 2 of 2 cases, while BLME differs by -0.19% to -0.74%. (d) `get_magnitude_dimension()` under rescaling: magnipy 1.8382038515330983 (s=1) -> LinAlgError (s=100) -> 0.0 (s=1000), against BLME 1.7936065439630873 -> 1.5772162915708001 -> 0.01410772770871137.
- Paper (YES): Leinster's closed form for a two-point space at distance d, |tX| = 2/(1 + e^{-t d}), is the published anchor Limbeck et al. build on. BLME reproduces it to 2.2e-16 or better at every t I tried: t=0.01 gives 1.0049999583337499 against the exact 1.00499995833375 (rel -2.21e-16), t=0.1 1.04995837495788 (exact, rel 0.0), t=1 1.4621171572600098 (rel 0.0), t=10 1.999909204262595 against 1.9999092042625952 (rel -1.11e-16), t=100 exactly 2.0. The one-point property |tX| = 1 is hard-coded and correct.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): The dominant driver of four unprefixed keys is the hidden-state NORM, not the geometry: on ONE fixed 100-point cloud in R^64, rescaling over the realistic 1e-3..3e3 range moves magnitude_area by a factor of 1e6 (exactly 1/s), magnitude_low_scale by +5675%, magnitude_med_scale by +88.6%, magnitude_auc by +44.5%, and magnitude_dimension from 2.2405 to 1.35e-08. Since residual-stream norms differ by orders of magnitude between model families, these read the norm. The point count is a second channel -- magnitude is bounded in [1, n] and saturates at n, which is why magnitude_high_scale and magnitude_ratio were correctly demoted after reading exactly 100.000000 and 1.000000 on all 61 archived models. `_meta_magnitude_area_norm` is free of the scale channel (4.15e-16 over 68 orders of magnitude).
- DEFECT: CODE, magnitude.py:78 + 128-135: the duplicate-point axiom (Limbeck et al. Table S.1; magnitude is an invariant of the metric quotient) is violated. A singular zeta from coincident points is handled by adding `_SINGULAR_EPS = 1e-2` to the diagonal and solving the PERTURBED system, which answers a different question. Measured against magnipy, which de-duplicates and is bit-identical between a cloud and its twin: the paper's own {0,1} vs {0,0,1} pair gives BLME 1.454154504046096 vs 1.4621171572600098 at t=1 (-0.5446%), and `magnitude_area` 18.482779875727587 vs 18.613780544942244 (-0.703783%). A 30x8 cloud with every point doubled: -0.186108% to -0.497512%. Reachable on real data -- gpt2's cached token cloud carries 138 exactly-duplicate rows in 23272.
- DEFECT: DOCUMENTED CLAIM IS FALSE, docs/VERIFICATION_STATUS.md:19 states 'geometry_magnitude | <=3.6e-15 vs magnipy (5 paths) incl. duplicate clouds', and E.3 says 'BLME's branch is closer to dedup ground truth'. Measured, the duplicate case is exactly where they part: magnipy IS the dedup ground truth (it prints its own de-duplication notice and returns a BIT-IDENTICAL magnitude vector for a cloud and its duplicated twin in 2 of 2 tests), and BLME deviates from it by -0.186108% to -0.744% -- roughly 1e12 times the claimed 3.6e-15 bound. Non-degenerate clouds do hold at 7.65e-16, so the bound is right for 'proper metric space' and wrong for 'incl. duplicate clouds'.
- DEFECT: DOCUMENTATION vs MEASUREMENT, magnitude.py:290-299: the comment says t_cut is 'Expressed in units of t_med so the integral is invariant to a global rescaling of the cloud, which MagArea on a raw t axis is not' and that anchoring on 1/median(d) 'makes the normalized area exactly invariant'. The second clause is true of `_meta_magnitude_area_norm` (4.15e-16 over the whole ladder) and FALSE of `magnitude_area`, which the comment sits directly above: measured 47163.963 / 47.163963 / 0.047163963 at s = 1e-3 / 1 / 1e3, i.e. exactly 1/s. A reader taking the comment at face value would treat a pure norm reading as scale-free.
- DEFECT: SHARED WITH THE REFERENCE, magnitude.py:199-215: the +/-scale_span ladder is anchored at t_med = 1/sqrt(median/2), which scales as 1/sqrt(s) while the magnitude function shifts as 1/s, so `magnitude_dimension` collapses under ordinary rescaling (2.24052884 -> 0.000140544655 from s=1 to s=1000; exactly 0.0 at s >= 1e13). magnipy degrades the same way (1.8382 -> LinAlgError at s=100 -> 0.0 at s=1000), so this is a property of the estimator as published rather than a BLME transcription error -- but it makes the key uncomparable across models whose norms differ.
- DEFECT: COST/DEAD PATH, magnitude.py:420-424: `_cache_ok` requires `_cached_docs == num_samples` EXACTLY, and core.py sizes the shared corpus to max(cache_num_samples, 200, task counts) >= 200 while magnitude's num_samples is 100 -- so the shared-cache branch can never be taken at the shipped config. I measured `_meta_magnitude_source` = 'self_collected' in 5 of 5 configurations including cache_num_samples = 100. The guard is correct and the numbers are now stable, but the task always pays its own forward pass and `_meta_magnitude_n_docs` degenerates to `num_samples` on that route, so it still cannot reveal a coupling it never has.
- DEFECT: DEMOTED-BUT-BEST, the only exactly scale-invariant member `_meta_magnitude_area_norm` (0.5352698299290679, worst rel 4.15e-16 across 1e-30..2.406e38) carries a `_meta_` prefix and is therefore excluded from the feature matrix, while four norm-dominated keys are not.
- DEFECT: NORMALIZED VARIANT FAILS THE SAME AXIOM, HARDER, magnitude.py:352: `_meta_magnitude_area_norm` = mag_area / (t_range * n). Duplicating every point of a 30x8 cloud moves it 0.6253877058401692 -> 0.3119033181123454, -50.126407% -- worse than the -33.7% the source comment records -- because the division by n responds to duplication while magnitude does not. So the ONLY exactly scale-invariant member (4.15e-16 over the full ladder) is also the member most sensitive to duplication. Both properties should hold; a de-duplication before zeta would give both at once.
- FIX: De-duplicate before building zeta, exactly as magnipy does: collapse coincident rows with `np.unique(X, axis=0)` (or a tolerance-based grouping) before `_pairwise_distance_matrix`, and record the collapsed count as `_meta_magnitude_n_duplicates_collapsed`. That restores the metric-quotient invariance the source paper's Table S.1 requires and makes the {0,1} / {0,0,1} twin agree exactly, as the reference already does. Separately, correct the comment at magnitude.py:290-299 so it names `_meta_magnitude_area_norm` rather than `magnitude_area` as the scale-invariant quantity, and consider promoting `magnitude_area_norm` out of `_meta_` since it is the only member that survives a cross-model norm difference.

### `geometry_mahalanobis`

**codex — RED** · At ordinary scale, the declared C=1 distance kernel agrees with sqrt(-2*term_gau) from the released implementation to worst relative error 2.57e-15 across four independent fixtures. But Mahalanobis distance is exactly invariant to every nonzero uniform rescaling: on one fixed finite, full-rank, all-nonzero dyadic fixture BLME returned mean 2.850706174 at scale 1, identically 0 at 1e-30 and 1e-25, up to 9.0116% relative error at 1e-13, and no distances at 1e13, 1e25, or 2.406159650500376e38; an exact +1e6 translation preserving every pairwise distance changed scores by up to 10.1465% and reduced rank correlation to 0.964005.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): pokaxpoka/deep_Mahalanobis_detector — Fetched the authors' 388-line lib_generation.py and locally executed its C=1 scoring identity term_gau=-0.5*(x-mu)^T P (x-mu) against BLME using the same precision: worst relative distance error was 2.57e-15 over shapes 25x768, 50x128, 200x64, and 30x1024. This is PARTIAL because the released estimator uses per-class means, EmpiricalCovariance, and max-over-class confidence, whereas BLME declares a single-centroid Ledoit-Wolf adaptation; on one fixed C=1 anisotropic fixture that covariance substitution changed distances by 41.1434% on average and 55.8861% at maximum.
- Paper (NONE): Lee et al. report vision-detector quantities such as 90.9% TNR for a ResNet CIFAR-100/LSUN setting, but publish 0 values for a C=1 language-model Gaussian over mean-pooled sentence states with paired token-shuffle OOD. The current local GPT-2 protocol gives AUROC 0.975616 with 25 fit, 25 ID-score, and 25 paired OOD-score rows, so there is no like-for-like paper number or meaningful relative error to report.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Width is a direct mechanical confound for raw distance: the controlled d=64..1024 sweep had rho=+1.000000 and 3.994090x growth. The fitted scoring function is architecture-dependent too: current local GPT-2 versus Pythia-70M runs emitted shrinkage 0.078732818 versus 0.261544049 (3.321919x), d/n_fit 30.72 versus 20.48, and ID d^2/d 0.643468742 versus 1.844518355; the exact zero-padding control further moved d^2/d 1.117988058 -> 0.281613628 (-74.8107%) while changing pairwise geometry by 0.000e+00.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY, mahalanobis.py:121-150 plus :335/:340: the live path passes float32 arrays directly into mean, Ledoit-Wolf covariance, precision, and quadratic arithmetic. On the all-finite/all-nonzero full-rank fixture, scale 1 gave mean distance 2.850706174, 1e-30 and 1e-25 silently collapsed all 50 distances to 0, 1e-13 changed individual distances by up to 9.0116% (rho 0.954190), and 1e13/1e25/2.406159650500376e38 returned [] after overflow. Driving the full five-split evaluate() path likewise moved ID d^2/d 1.089621752 -> 1.678645566 at 1e-13 (+54.0576%), emitted 0 at 1e-25, and returned an error at 1e13 despite every input being finite.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY, mahalanobis.py:121-150: translation invariance also fails in live arithmetic. On dyadic inputs, +1e6 preserved all pairwise distances exactly (maximum change 0.000e+00) but moved individual Mahalanobis scores by up to 10.1465% and rho to 0.964005; the full task moved mean ID 2.929316219 -> 2.833933411 (-3.2561%), ID d^2/d 1.089621752 -> 1.019789527 (-6.4089%), and AUROC 0.533952 -> 0.547392.
- DEFECT: NORMALIZATION/CODE, mahalanobis.py:507-526: all three d^2/d outputs remain feature-eligible but fail an exact isometric-embedding control. Zero-padding one unchanged 16-D geometry through d=32/64/128/256 kept every pairwise distance bit-identical (maximum delta 0.000e+00) while ID d^2/d moved 1.117988058 -> 0.983473732 -> 0.702129811 -> 0.457444869 -> 0.281613628 (-74.8107%); normalized_ok is therefore RED even though a fully isotropic null stayed within 4.58% over d=64..1024.
- DEFECT: PROTOCOL/CODE, mahalanobis.py:280 versus :393: the corruption generator remains hard-coded to RandomState(42), while the configurable seed controls only fit/score partitions. End-to-end instrumentation at seeds 0 and 7 produced identical shuffled input-id tuples for 50/50 rows, so changing the advertised run seed changes the split but not the OOD intervention.

The current five-split averaging reduces seed variance, but it does not add independent corpus observations or repair this rank deficiency; this belongs on protocol/comparability, not implementation faithfulness.

The ordinary analytic test therefore misses both the silent 50/50 zero collapse and the high-scale [] failure.
- FIX: Before centering or covariance estimation, promote X_train and X_test to float64; center both with the same float64 training mean, divide both centered arrays by a common power-of-two scale, and fit LedoitWolf(assume_centered=True) on the rescaled training array. That candidate was finite on 7/7 requested scales with worst relative scale error 2.79e-08 and +1e6 translation error 8.40e-11, while preserving the estimator because a common scalar cancels exactly; fail closed on any nonfinite distance instead of returning []. Add the exact scale/translation regressions, wire the corruption to an explicit ood_seed (or the run seed), replace the ordering-independence claim or hash stable sample identities, report mean/range shrinkage across all five splits, and demote the d^2/d family unless its zero-padding failure is explicitly intended. Keep the repaired advancing split generator: it produced 5/5 unique sets in all 15 schedules and current pooled AUROC sd fell 47.1%; do not replace it based on the withdrawn +52.3% claim, though a separately named mean-per-split AUROC may be emitted.

**opus5 — YELLOW** · The distance kernel is EXACT against the released reference. I transcribed pokaxpoka/deep_Mahalanobis_detector's `get_Mahalanobis_score` scoring line -- `term_gau = -0.5 * (z-mu) P (z-mu)^T`, distance = sqrt(-2*term_gau) -- and ran it on the same Ledoit-Wolf precision as BLME's `_compute_mahalanobis_distances`: worst relative deviation 4.32e-16 across shapes 25x768, 50x128, 200x64 and 30x1024 (e.g. 41.348787891157464 vs 41.34878789115746). The FIX I was asked to verify is real: `_rng_split = _perm_rng` now advances ONE generator across splits, and replaying the exact code path gives 5 of 5 DISTINCT fit sets with split0 != split1 at every one of 15 (n_id, seed) combinations -- n_id = 25/50/100 x seeds 42/0/7/1/2026 -- where the re-seeded version reproduced split 0 exactly. The OOD pairing survives: the live production run reports n_id_fit = 25, n_id_score = 25 and n_ood_score = 25, i.e. `X_ood_full[_si]` restricts the OOD arm to the same held-out indices in every split. The CAVEAT a reader must know is the one I was asked to re-measure, and it REPRODUCES on the post-fix tree: the headline `auroc` is computed on distances POOLED across splits (mahalanobis.py:474-489) rather than averaged over them, and over 20 seeds on gpt2 at the shipped config that makes it NOISIER, not less noisy -- sd 0.024750 at n_splits=1 against 0.037685 at n_splits=5, +52.3%, with the mean moving 0.981280 -> 0.973792 and the range widening from [0.897600, 1.000000] to [0.821824, 0.994560]. Every OTHER statistic behaves as split-averaging should: mahalanobis_sq_per_dim_id sd 0.104886 -> 0.043709 (-58.3%), ood_separation_ratio 0.182764 -> 0.072133 (-60.5%), mean_mahalanobis_id 1.685262 -> 0.833668 (-50.5%). So the aggregation is right for four keys and wrong for the headline. The prior recording of this (+49%) is confirmed, and yes -- it is the expected consequence of pooling scores whose scale differs by split, because each split fits its own shrunk covariance.

- GitHub (YES): pokaxpoka/deep_Mahalanobis_detector — Reimplemented the reference's scoring line verbatim (`term_gau = -0.5 * torch.mm(torch.mm(zero_f, precision), zero_f.t()).diag()`; BLME's claimed identity is distance == sqrt(-2*term_gau)) and ran it against BLME's `_compute_mahalanobis_distances` on the SAME Ledoit-Wolf precision matrix and centroid. Worst relative deviation over four shapes: 4.317875520572742e-16 (25x768 3.54e-16, 50x128 4.32e-16, 200x64 2.84e-16, 30x1024 1.61e-16). The scoring kernel is therefore a faithful port; the divergence from Lee et al. is architectural (single centroid, shrinkage) and is declared in task_metadata as an adaptation.
- Paper (NONE): Lee et al. 2018 publish TNR/AUROC for CIFAR/SVHN/LSUN image OOD detection with ResNet/DenseNet features, per-class means and a tied within-class covariance. BLME runs a single-centroid sentence-level proxy on WikiText with token-shuffled OOD, so no published number is comparable. The reproducible anchor is the reference kernel (4.32e-16) plus the null calibration: dist^2/d should be ~1 for a correctly specified Gaussian, and the live gpt2 ID value is 0.6434686395707678 at a fit of n=25 in d=768 (d/n_fit = 30.72, so the covariance is rank-24 and shrinkage is doing the work).
- Confound (CONFIRMED): The raw distance is a width reading by construction: dist^2 ~ chi2(d) under the null, so mean_mahalanobis_* grows as sqrt(d_model). The per-dim variants remove that leading term but not the model dependence -- the source records 0.0237 vs 2.1219 at identical d=1024 -- and the shrinkage coefficient, which differs ~10x across the zoo, is itself an architecture-linked quantity that changes the scoring function. Measured here: the estimand is exactly invariant to scale (1.1e-15 at 2.4e38) but NOT to the change of basis a different architecture supplies (11.6% under a general affine map).
- DEFECT: CODE, mahalanobis.py:474-489: the headline `auroc` is computed on the CONCATENATION of per-split distances rather than as the mean of per-split AUROCs. Each split fits its own shrunk covariance, so the distance SCALE differs between splits and pooling degrades the ranking. Over 20 seeds on gpt2 at the shipped config, sd goes 0.024750 (n_splits=1) -> 0.037685 (n_splits=5), +52.3%, and the mean drops 0.981280 -> 0.973792 -- while every other statistic gets 50-60% LESS variable over the same change. The split-averaging that was added to stabilise the task therefore destabilises the one number the task is read on.
- DEFECT: ADAPTATION (declared), mahalanobis.py:106-152: Ledoit-Wolf shrinkage breaks affine invariance by 11.6% under a general GL(64) map, and the realised shrinkage I measured live is 0.07873287796974182 (gpt2), 0.10948853939771652 (distilgpt2) and 0.2619228959083557 (pythia-70m) -- a 3.3x spread over three small models alone. `_meta_ledoit_wolf_shrinkage` and `_meta_d_over_n_fit` (30.72 live) disclose it, which is the right handling, but a cross-model comparison of the raw distances is not licensed.
- DEFECT: PROTOCOL/CONFIG, tasks.yaml:224 `num_samples: 50`: the fit is n = 25 in d = 768, i.e. a rank-24 empirical covariance, and the score set is 25 ID against 25 paired OOD. At that size a single split's AUROC has a 20-seed range of [0.897600, 1.000000]; the auroc is also near the ceiling (mean 0.981280), so it discriminates poorly between models.
- DEFECT: SCOPE (declared), single-centroid C=1: Lee et al. Eq. 2 uses per-class means with a pooled within-class covariance and a max over classes. task_metadata already records that on a bimodal ID cloud with OOD at the global mean the single-centroid form inverts (AUROC 0.021 measured in the module header) where Lee et al.'s form gives 1.0.
- FIX: Average the AUROC over splits instead of pooling the distances: compute `roc_auc_score` inside the split loop on that split's own `_d_id` / `_d_ood` and report the mean (keeping the pooled value under `_meta_auroc_pooled` so archived numbers stay recoverable). That converts the headline from the one statistic that got 52.3% noisier under split-averaging into one that behaves like the other four (50-60% less noisy). Optionally also emit a per-split sd as `_meta_auroc_split_sd`.

### `geometry_matrix_entropy`

**codex — RED** · Wei et al.'s statistic centers every token vector, row-normalizes the centered vectors, and computes the entropy of their trace-normalized covariance, so adding one common vector must leave it unchanged. For Y=[[-0.125,-0.375],[-0.3125,-0.1875],[-0.5,0.125],[-0.25,-0.3125]], an independent evaluation is 0.674737554091 both before and after adding [1e6,1e6], but _matrix_entropy changes from 0.674737556330 to 0.133782971591 (-80.1726%) even though all four translated rows remain finite and distinct; therefore the implementation does not compute the paper-defined statistic on this nondegenerate input.

- GitHub (YES): waltonfuture/Diff-eRank@b1f38b0e994433731c0f7057f931cb4608677e7b — Executed the locally recovered released utils/entropy_dataset.py (SHA-256 6a75adb8cf94d8c04bc3cd7c7af180b24621dbd646f2360162bcd4b63a04969e) on the same inputs. Across 240 seeded ordinary matrices, BLME versus the released routine had raw-H max/mean absolute differences 1.00536e-5/1.16791e-6, while BLME versus an independent high-precision derivation was much closer at 1.64671e-7/4.29874e-8; on the translated counterexample the released routine itself raises _LinAlgError while the derivation remains 0.674737554091. The reference's model(id)[0] representation was also verified to equal hidden_states[-1] at max absolute difference 0.0 on GPT-2, Pythia-70M, and Pythia-160M, and BLME fresh/cache/direct headline results were identical, so the raw-HF-tuple revert is on the reference-faithful side.
- Paper (PARTIAL): Wei et al. report Wikipedia matrix entropy 0.7003 for Pythia-70M and 0.6580 for Pythia-160M. On the first 200 locally cached WikiText-103 validation passages at max_length=512, BLME measured 0.607324505 and 0.563427928 (relative errors -13.2765% and -14.3727%); their gap was 0.043896577 versus the paper's 0.0423 (+3.7744%). This is only partial reproduction because the released protocol uses 10,000 random Wikipedia training documents at max_length=2048, not this available local corpus/window.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Width confounding is directly demonstrated by the isometric d=512 -> 4096 zero-padding intervention: geometry and raw H are unchanged (relative raw-H change 1.06e-7), but the normalized headline falls 25.0000%. In the 66-model archive the normalized headline also correlates with d_model at rho=-0.483117 (p=3.98e-5) and depth at rho=-0.339186 (p=0.00534), materially more than raw H's rho=+0.032951 and +0.048767.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY: matrix_entropy.py:60,87 performs centering in the storage precision. On the exactly representable four-row Y fixture, adding the exactly representable common offset 1e6 changes H/log(d) 0.674737556330 -> 0.133782971591 (-80.1726%), while a high-precision computation on the exact stored values stays 0.674737554091 and the released routine becomes non-finite and raises. This violates the translation invariance implied algebraically by Wei et al. Definition 4.1.
- DEFECT: CODE/NUMERICS: on X=[[8,-4],[2,-2],[-1,-1],[-2,-1]] normalized to maxima {1e-30,1e-25,1e-13,1,1e13,1e25,2.406159650500376e38}, H/log(d) ranges 0.131902298309..0.131902631525, with worst relative drift 2.52623e-6 from scale 1 although the independent value on each exact stored input is invariant to about 3e-17. The analytic two-direction anchor remains correct at H=0.693147182465 versus log(2)=0.693147180560, so the fault is input-conditioned rather than a wholesale formula error.
- DEFECT: CODE/PROVENANCE: matrix_entropy.py:337-338 sets _meta_last_entry_is_block_output from tensor availability, not from whether a last-block hook/substitution actually succeeded. For a valid model whose blocks live in an unrecognized weird_stack ModuleList, get_layers(model) is None, yet the flag is true; the reported deepest-profile entropy 0.717231731967 differs from the manually hooked block output 0.730589839438 by -0.013358107471. The separate hook-lifetime repair at cache.py:501-503 is verified closed: an injected second-forward exception left hook counts [0,0] -> [0,0].
- DEFECT: TEST COVERAGE: 57 selected matrix-entropy parity, scale, task, post-final-norm, and hook-leak tests pass, but test_scale_invariance.py:118-127 tests only scalar rescaling of torch.eye(4), lines 230-238 test one more scale fixture, and neither applies a large common translation. Consequently the 80.1726% failure and the false provenance flag survive the focused suite.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY, NOT VERDICT: the paper-defined H/log(d) headline changes by -25.0000% when a fixed 91-point simplex is merely zero-padded from d=512 to d=4096, and its archive rho with width is -0.483117 versus +0.032951 for raw H. This determines comparable/normalized_ok/confounds only and is not a premise of the RED implementation verdict.
- DEFECT: CONFIG/PROTOCOL, NOT VERDICT: src/blme/tasks/configs/defaults.yaml:49-50 resolves to 10 passages and the study override uses 200, while released entropy_dataset.py:65-78 randomly selects 10,000 training documents with max_length=2048; production falls back to 512 at matrix_entropy.py:223-229. For the measured GPT-2 mean profile this would turn 0.474679969084 into 0.071447169184, so current outputs must not enter either double-normalization path.

Version the schema and update the stale count/numbers before pooling results.
- FIX: Promote the working tensor before matrix_entropy.py:87 and retain higher precision through centering, row normalization, and SVD (or implement an equivalently validated compensated centering path). The tested full-high-precision prototype makes the translated fixture exactly invariant at 0.674737554091, reduces the seven-scale spread to about 5e-17, and changes 240 ordinary outputs by at most 1.69971e-7. Derive _meta_last_entry_is_block_output from an observed successful block substitution rather than non-null token containers; add end-to-end regressions for the listed Y fixture at offsets 0/1e3/1e6 and for an unsupported module layout; remove the downstream second log(d) division, version the archive key change, and refresh metadata. Preserve cache.py's current try/finally hook removal, which passed the injected-exception check.

**opus5 — GREEN** · The numeric core is the strongest of the nine I reviewed. `_matrix_entropy` is BIT-EXACTLY scale-invariant over the whole representable range: on a seeded (128,64) matrix scaled by exact powers of two, entropy = 3.9091129302978516 at 2^-120, 2^-100, 2^-40, 2^0, 2^40 and 2^100 -- rel 0.000e+00 at every one, with 2^127 the first scale where the input itself is not representable. (An earlier sweep at decimal scales showed a 0.66% spread; that was rounding in my own input construction, not the code -- the power-of-two ladder isolates the estimator and it is exact.) The rescale-before-centring ordering at matrix_entropy.py:79-87 is correct, the row-L2 step at :95-97 factors the row max out before norming, and the resulting Z has unit-norm rows so `torch.linalg.svdvals` at :111 has sigma_max <= sqrt(N) and cannot reach the float32 overflow that `safe_svdvals` exists for. tests/tasks/parity/test_matrix_entropy_parity.py imports the production `_matrix_entropy` (unlike the repe sibling) and passes. The headline correctly follows Wei et al.'s released recipe: `_meta_headline_is_post_final_norm = True` measured on gpt2 via both the self-collect and shared-cache routes, with matrix_entropy = 0.2555756647884853 and mean_matrix_entropy = 0.4746799544310399 identical on both.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): waltonfuture/Diff-eRank — I did not re-execute the released Diff-eRank code this round; the standing evidence is the dedicated parity file, which I did run -- tests/tasks/parity/test_matrix_entropy_parity.py imports the production `_matrix_entropy` from blme and passes (part of 21 passed). What I verified directly is the property the released recipe implies: the headline is taken from the untouched post-`ln_f` entry rather than the hooked block output, confirmed by `_meta_headline_is_post_final_norm = True` on both collection routes.
- Paper (PARTIAL): The task's metadata records that absolute values do NOT reproduce the paper at either protocol (+16.1%/+17.6% at Wei et al.'s 2048-token protocol, -15.4%/-16.3% at BLME defaults) while the model-to-model DELTA does (0.0389/0.0420 against a published 0.0423, i.e. -8.0%/-0.7%). I did not re-measure the delta this round -- my cross-model panel had only completed gpt2 when I finished -- so I am reporting the recorded status rather than a fresh reproduction. PARTIAL is the honest grade: one component matches, the absolute level does not.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): The promoted headline divides by log(d) while its cap is log(N-1), so the division adds rather than removes a width term (recorded rho -0.483/-0.247 vs +0.033/+0.155 for the raw form). I did not re-measure over a zoo this round; the mechanism is documented and dimensionally clear.
- DEFECT: PROVENANCE FLAG IS ASSERTED FROM TENSOR PRESENCE, NOT DERIVED FROM THE SUBSTITUTION. matrix_entropy.py:337-338 computes `_meta_last_entry_is_block_output = bool(per_layer_tokens is not None and _postnorm_tokens is not None)`. Neither operand records whether the hook actually fired. On the cache route, a cache whose `_last_block_raw` is absent still returns a full per-layer dict, and `_pn` is still non-None, so the flag reports True while the profile's deepest entry is `ln_f(b_{L-1})`. On the fresh route the same holds: matrix_entropy.py:196 appends to `post_norm_last` unconditionally and :199-201 substitutes only `if _h_raw is not None and _h_raw.shape == hs[-1].shape`, with no counter -- so `per_layer` is populated identically whether or not the hook attached. This is exactly the failure mode the SIBLING task documents and guards against: schatten.py:606-609 says 'DERIVED, not asserted. Hard-coding True made this flag lie on any architecture where get_layers() returns None', and schatten.py:421 therefore derives it from `_n_fwd > 0 and _n_sub == _n_fwd` while cache.py:160-169 maintains `_last_block_substituted` for precisely this purpose. matrix_entropy consults neither. On gpt2 the flag reads True and IS correct (measured on both routes), so the lie is latent rather than live -- it needs an architecture `get_layers` cannot discover -- but the headline flag `_meta_headline_is_post_final_norm` sitting two lines above it is derived properly, which makes the inconsistency within one dict harder to defend.
- DEFECT: `safe_svdvals` HAS ZERO CALL SITES IN THE ENTIRE REPO. utils.py:262 defines it for a real and verified bug -- I measured np.linalg.svd returning sigma_max = inf on a (120,16) float32 matrix at scale 2.406159650500376e38, which drives `effective_rank` to NaN, against safe_svdvals returning 9.3192481e38 and erank 15.727193, exactly the scale-1 value; scipy.linalg.svdvals and torch.linalg.svdvals both overflow identically. But `grep -rn safe_svdvals` over the whole repository returns exactly one line: its own definition. This task does not use it (correctly -- its row-L2 step makes it unnecessary), schatten and weight_norms re-implement the same logic inline, and about ten other SVD/eigen consumers (isotropy.py:56,273; collapse.py:126,130; contextualization.py:116,169; unembedding.py:97,104; spectral.py:233; activation_sinks.py:216; procrustes_linearity.py:184; attention_rank.py:44; rmt_bulk.py:268,277,485) use the unguarded calls. The helper is correct and dead.
- DEFECT: ARCHIVE INCOMPATIBILITY UNDER AN UNCHANGED KEY: the metadata records that all 61 archived models hold RAW H (1.10-3.94) under the key `matrix_entropy` while the code now writes NORMALISED H (0.16-0.61) under that same key -- measured 0.2555756647884853 normalised against 1.6979909777641295 raw on gpt2, a 6.6x difference. The rename to `matrix_entropy_raw` is the right fix for new runs, but nothing in the emitted dict distinguishes an old file from a new one, so pooling is silently possible.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY, NOT VERDICT: the promoted normalised headline is the MORE width-dependent of the two emitted forms (recorded rho -0.483/-0.247 vs +0.033/+0.155), the opposite of what a normalised twin usually buys.
- FIX: Derive the profile flag instead of asserting it: on the cache route read `getattr(cache, '_last_block_substituted', False)` immediately after the `replace_final_norm=True` call and BEFORE the `replace_final_norm=False` call (the ordering geometry_schatten just had to fix for the same reason); on the fresh route count substitutions and require `_n_sub == _n_fwd`, as schatten.py:421 does. Separately, either wire `safe_svdvals` into the unguarded SVD consumers listed above or delete it -- a verified helper with zero call sites is a claim of protection that nothing collects.

### `geometry_mp_bulk_deviation`

**codex — YELLOW** · The MP support and atom are implemented correctly on analytic anchors: at gamma=0.25 the code gives [0.25, 2.25], at gamma=4 it gives atom mass 0.75, and over gamma={0.05,0.25,0.5,0.9,1.0} its CDF differs from adaptive quadrature by at most 1.351187016e-07. Fresh null/planted controls returned exactly 0/1/3/5/20 upper outliers as constructed. A value-changing defect remains just below gamma=1: at gamma=7168/7169 and x=2.347737514e-6, _mp_cdf returns 0.000725958665 versus independently converged 0.000906808110; on a valid trace-normalized MP-quantile spectrum this makes KS=0.000250603835 instead of 0.000069803029, a 3.590157x error.

The official Staats archive exposes 1 ZIP of 4.6 MB but 0 like-for-like implementations of this first/mid/last activation-correlation aggregate, so executable-source coverage is partial rather than a full task reproduction.

In the g25 path, detect the 0.125 <-> 0.5 retained-dimension cycle, search/select a feasible subset, validate |D_eff/(N-1)-0.25| before scoring, emit achieved gamma, and fail closed if infeasible; the fresh fixture must produce gamma 0.25, spike energy 0.503150935018, and KS 0.484375000000. Separate the 0.05 edge buffer from theory-labeled outputs, raise the 20,000-token cap to at least 21,505 for the 15 D=5120/5376 runs that the cached corpus can support, and explicitly mark the D=7168 run unavailable unless at least 28,673 tokens are supplied. Add the measured width, basis-rotation, row-duplication, seven-scale, and +1e3/+1e6 translation regressions without using those protocol/comparability results to alter the implementation verdict.

**opus5 — GREEN** · The Marchenko-Pastur machinery is correct against analytic anchors I computed independently. (a) `_mp_cdf` against numerical quadrature of the closed-form density sqrt((t+ - t)(t - t-)) / (2 pi gamma t): worst absolute deviation 6.369022408093699e-08 over gamma = 0.05/0.25/0.5/0.9/1.0/1.5/2.0/4.0 x 9 points each, with F(t+) = 1.0000000000 at EVERY gamma -- so the gamma > 1 point mass 1 - 1/gamma and the gamma = 1 density singularity are both handled. (b) `_ks_distance` on a genuine MP spectrum falls as O(1/sqrt(D)) exactly as the null requires: 0.019830 / 0.013467 / 0.006136 / 0.003681 at D = 100/200/400/800 with N = 4D. (c) `_estimate_sigma2` recovers the scale on isotropic bulks to within 2.53% across 28 ORDERS OF MAGNITUDE and the whole aspect range -- worst |rel| 2.5241% over sigma^2 in {1e-12, 1e-6, 1, 1e6, 1e12, 1e16} x gamma in {0.1, 0.5, 0.9}, best +0.0345%. (d) The KS statistic is exactly scale and translation invariant, as it must be on a correlation matrix: 0.013090691111312211 at scale 1 and worst relative deviation 5.41e-14 over 1e-30 / 1e-25 / 1e-13 / 1e13 / 1e25 / 2.406159650500376e38, and 1.98e-11 under a +1e6 translation -- it survives the rungs that broke stable rank, CKA and homology. THE REVERT I WAS ASKED TO CONFIRM IS IN PLACE AND RESTORES THE PRIOR VALUES: the merged production config reads max_tokens = 20000, and driving the real evaluate() on gpt2 at 20000 vs 32000 gives `_meta_mp_n_tokens_first/mid/last` = 20000 vs 23272 (the cap binds at 20000; at 32000 the corpus supply of 23272 binds instead) and `_meta_mp_gamma_*` = 0.0384019200960048 vs 0.03300244940054144, a -14.06% shift. 76 of 86 numeric keys differ between the two settings. The family the 32000 change was made for barely moves: mp_outlier_frac_g25_mean 0.09722222222222222 -> 0.09635416666666667 (-0.89%), mp_spike_energy_g25_mean 0.5310461776068547 -> 0.5293429945061563 (-0.32%), mp_ks_distance_g25_mean 0.4241471677211375 -> 0.4237990016673401 (-0.08%), while the gamma-matched headline family moves +1.40% to +3.20%. So the revert costs under 1% on the target family and restores the archived aspect ratio for everything else -- it was the right call.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): AndreaMonaco/scikit-rmt (CDF cross-check); no reference implementation exists for this diagnostic — There is no released implementation of 'MP bulk deviation of transformer hidden states' to port -- the task is BLME's own construction on top of Marchenko & Pastur's 1967 law, and task_metadata says so ('parity-ready', scikit-rmt for a CDF cross-check only). I therefore verified against the MATHEMATICS rather than a repo: `_mp_cdf` versus scipy.integrate.quad of the closed-form MP density at 8 aspect ratios x 9 evaluation points (worst 6.37e-08, F(t+) = 1.0 exactly at every gamma including 1.0, 1.5, 2.0 and 4.0 where the atom at zero must be carried), and `_ks_distance` versus the O(1/sqrt(D)) null rate on true MP spectra (0.019830 -> 0.003681 as D goes 100 -> 800).
- Paper (PARTIAL): Marchenko & Pastur 1967 publish the law, not a number for language models, so there is no table entry to reproduce. What IS reproducible is the law's own content, and BLME reproduces it: the bulk edges (1 +/- sqrt(gamma))^2 are exact by construction, the CDF integrates to 1.0000000000 at every gamma tested, the gamma > 1 point mass 1 - 1/gamma is present (F(t+) = 1.0 at gamma = 1.5, 2.0, 4.0), and a genuine MP spectrum's KS distance to the law decays as 1/sqrt(D). Baik-Ben Arous-Peche is cited but the emitted family is deliberately relabelled 'isotropy deviation' rather than a BBP spike test, which is the honest call given that standard MP is the wrong null for a heteroscedastic Sigma at any sigma^2.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): gamma = D/(N-1) is d_model over the token count, so the gamma-matched family compares each model against a width-determined null: gpt2 at 768/20000 = 0.0384019200960048, and a 4096-wide model at the same token budget would sit at 0.2048, a 5.3x different aspect ratio and therefore a different reference law. The `_g25` family removes this by construction (gamma pinned at 0.25 for everyone) and is measurably insensitive to the aspect shift that moves the gamma-matched family (under 1% versus 1.4-3.2% for a 14.06% gamma change). The token supply itself is a second, tokenizer-linked channel: at num_samples = 200 the corpus yields 23,272 tokens on gpt2 and 23.7k-25.7k on the other families, so the cap at 20000 equalizes it for models that clear it and silently does not for models that do not.
- DEFECT: CONFIG (correctly reverted), tasks.yaml:200 + defaults.yaml: max_tokens is back to 20000. I confirm the diagnosis behind the revert. At 32000 the cap does not bind on any small model -- `_meta_mp_n_tokens_*` reports 23272, the corpus supply -- so the change altered gamma (0.0384019200960048 -> 0.03300244940054144, -14.06%) and moved 76 of 86 numeric keys while buying under 1% on the `_g25` family it targeted (mean keys -0.89% / -0.32% / -0.08%). The lever for widening coverage is num_samples, which increases the supply, not max_tokens, which only caps it.
- DEFECT: COVERAGE, the `_g25` family needs D/0.25 tokens, i.e. 4*d_model. At the shipped 20000-token cap that is satisfied only for d_model <= 5000, so the only cross-model-comparable family in this task is NaN for the widest checkpoints in the zoo. On gpt2 nothing is NaN (I measured zero NaN keys of 86 at 20000).
- DEFECT: MODEL-DEPENDENT NULL (declared), the gamma-matched family scores each model against a different MP law because gamma = d_model / n_tokens. This is documented in the module header and mitigated by the _g25 twins, but the unprefixed `mp_*_mean` keys are still exported and are the ones a naive feature matrix will pick up.
- DEFECT: ARCHIVE, geometry_mp_bulk_deviation is absent from all 66 `results/blme_verified_a/*.json` envelopes and from all 26 `results/blme/*.json` -- I checked every file. So the 'archive incompatibility' argument for the revert does not apply to those archives; the revert is justified on the measurement (76 of 86 columns moved for under 1% on the target family), not on protecting stored numbers.

- GitHub (YES): rhubarbwu/neural-collapse (neural_collapse/measure.py); xyhan-github/neuralcollapse (authors' notebook) — Fetched neural_collapse/measure.py from rhubarbwu/neural-collapse main this round and re-implemented `covariance_ratio` verbatim in both released variants -- metric='pinv' (V_inter = M_centred^T M_centred / K, trace(pinv(V_inter) @ V_intra)/K, Papyan et al.) and metric='svd' (scipy.sparse.linalg.svds top-(K-1) pseudo-inverse, Han et al.) -- and ran them against BLME's `_neural_collapse_metrics` on five identical K=4, 7-per-class, d=20 fixtures. Because the label-permutation null moves in lockstep, a null-referenced NC1 is not confounded while the raw column is. SUSPECTED rather than CONFIRMED because 0 of the 66 archived blme_verified_a runs contain this task, so there is no cross-model correlation to estimate.

**opus5 — GREEN** · The task computes what the manuscript defines (metrics_catalogue.tex:24 -- 'overall (strided sliding window, 1024-token context pinned across models), plus a rare/frequent-token decomposition and bits-per-byte'), and I reproduced the reference procedure head to head. Running BLME's strided_perplexity and the HuggingFace docs/source/en/perplexity.md snippet verbatim beside it on the FULL WikiText-2-raw test split (4,358 rows, window 1024 / stride 512, gpt2, one GPU on this host): BLME 25.17047937898196 against the HF snippet's 25.17040443420410, relative 2.98e-06 -- and the residue is entirely the HF snippet's own `num_loss_tokens = num_valid_tokens - input_ids.size(0)` quirk, which drops exactly one token per window (287,643 BLME vs 287,083 HF, difference 560 = the window count). BLME also matches HuggingFace's published figure for that page, 25.17041, to relative 2.8e-06. I REFUTE the round-5 codex RED on its verdict-driving item. Its claim is that the shared-offset EVEN SPLIT misattributes bytes at the scored/unscored boundary; the split is real (for GPT-2 `emoji`+`b` the exact byte ownership is [3,1,1] and BLME assigns [2,2,1], so 3 rather than 2 scored bytes, ratio 1.5000) but I measured its cost on the actual corpora and it is EXACTLY ZERO. I computed exact per-token byte ownership independently from GPT-2's byte-level token strings -- which sum to the corpus byte length exactly (1,296,370 = 1,296,370 on WikiText-2; 59,285 = 59,285 on the shipped default corpus) -- and compared the scored denominator: WikiText-2 exact 1,296,368 against BLME's reported 1,296,368 (difference 0 bytes, relative 0.000e+00), shipped corpus exact 59,281 against BLME's implied denominator 59,281.0000 (difference 0). The reason is structural, not luck: at the shipped stride = window/2 the ONLY unscored position in the whole stream is position 0, and 236 of 287,644 tokens share a span but none of them sits at that boundary. Codex's `emoji` case is a 5-byte, 3-token corpus, not the paper's domain.

- GitHub (YES): huggingface/transformers (docs/source/en/perplexity.md reference procedure); EleutherAI/lm-evaluation-harness (bits_per_byte convention) — Ran the HuggingFace reference procedure verbatim in the same process as BLME's strided_perplexity on the full WikiText-2-raw test split with gpt2: 25.17040443420410 (HF) vs 25.17047937898196 (BLME), relative 2.98e-06, the residue being the HF snippet's per-window token subtraction (560 tokens over 560 windows). I ALSO built an independent exact byte-ownership reference from GPT-2's byte-level token strings and cross-checked BLME's byte denominator on two corpora at 0 bytes error. NOTE, correcting an earlier Opus number: the round-5 file claimed relative 9.795e-08; my own head-to-head is 2.98e-06, which is what the module docstring at utils.py:553-556 already states.
- Paper (YES): HuggingFace's published WikiText-2-raw GPT-2 figure for this exact protocol is 25.17041; BLME returns 25.17047938, relative error 2.8e-06. The GPT-2 paper's own 29.41 is NOT reproducible here and should not be claimed: Radford et al. measure it under a zero-shot protocol with inverted detokenization that BLME deliberately does not implement, and the module docstring correctly retracts an earlier claim that 21.80 was 'the published GPT-2 small number' (21.80 is BLME's own default-corpus value, which I confirm at ppl_overall = 21.80481314894843). Shannon (1948), the manuscript's cited source for bits-per-byte, defines the quantity and publishes no language-model figure.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): This is a capability measure by construction, not a structural one -- the module's own docstring calls BPC 'the cleanest single-number capability proxy', and normalization.SIZE_LINKED_BY_MEANING already records 'perplexity is a capability measure'. The leakage is that the exclusion does not fire: normalization.CONTROL_TASK_PREFIXES contains only 'consistency_calibration.' and 'interpretability_prediction_entropy.', so is_feature('geometry_perplexity.ppl_overall'), '.bits_per_byte' and '.ppl_rare' all return True and a metric that falls monotonically with parameter count is admissible as a 'structural' predictor. The SIZE_LINKED_BY_MEANING entry is also keyed 'geometry_perplexity.perplexity', a key this task never emits (it emits ppl_overall), so even that note is inert.
- DEFECT: STUDY LEAKAGE (not a kernel defect), normalization.py:176-179 and 248-252: geometry_perplexity's seven public outputs all pass is_feature(), and the SIZE_LINKED_BY_MEANING note is keyed on a metric name ('geometry_perplexity.perplexity') that the task does not emit. Add 'geometry_perplexity.' to CONTROL_TASK_PREFIXES or state in the manuscript that this task is a target/control.
- DEFECT: CODE (real, measured cost 0 on both shipped corpora), geometry/utils.py:730-778: tokens sharing one source offset span divide that span's bytes EVENLY, while exact byte ownership is recoverable from the byte-level token string -- GPT-2 `emoji`+`b` is exactly [3,1,1] and BLME assigns [2,2,1]. It can only change the denominator when a shared-span group straddles the scored/unscored boundary; at the shipped stride = window/2 there is exactly one unscored position in the stream, and on WikiText-2 (236 shared-span tokens of 287,644) and on the shipped default corpus the resulting denominator is exact to 0 bytes. Fixable by taking len(convert_ids_to_tokens(id)) for byte-level BPE, but the measured benefit at the shipped protocol is zero.
- DEFECT: PROTOCOL, perplexity.py:100-103 and 189-201: ppl_rare / ppl_freq are per-document 512-truncated statistics while ppl_overall is the joined strided value -- measured ppl_overall_perdoc 37.90883129 against ppl_overall 21.80481315 on gpt2 (1.738x). Disclosed by _meta_ppl_bucket_protocol and contained by the within-protocol ratio, but the two families are not one decomposition.
- DEFECT: CONVENTION: bits_per_byte is bits per SCORED byte (the stream's first token is never scored and its span is excluded). Measured 1,296,368 of 1,296,370 bytes on WikiText-2 (1.5e-06) and 59,281 of 59,285 on the shipped corpus; the key name does not say 'scored'.
- DEFECT: DOC, perplexity.py:13-20 and geometry/utils.py:566: the class docstring says BPC divides by 'UTF-8 characters' where the implementation (correctly) counts Python code points, and strided_perplexity's docstring documents a 5-tuple return although every path returns 8 values.

### `geometry_phd_dimension`

**codex — YELLOW** · From the paper's E_alpha(n) proportional to n^(1-alpha/d), I re-derived d=alpha/(1-m); the current alpha-powered MST total, per-size median, OLS slope and alpha/(1-m) inversion are correct, giving 2.030161933/1.983664319/1.955619173 on uniform R^2 at alpha=0.5/1.0/1.5, while all 7 local GPTID parity tests passed. YELLOW, not RED: a generic cloud was invariant over 1e-30 through 2.406159650500376e38 to 5.39e-13 relative and under a +1e6 translation to 1.20e-13, but a finite duplicate-free cloud with a required diameter-length MST bridge changed from 19.8318792712 at scale 1 to 17.5703380630 at 1e25 (-11.4036%) because the Prim sentinel is not strictly larger than the distances; GPTID reproduces the same failure bit-exactly.
