- DEFECT: NORMALIZED VARIANT FAILS FUNCTION PRESERVATION: appending 20 identity layers to the profile [2.1, 1.7, 1.6, 2.0] -- which leaves the computed function untouched -- moves curvature_slope from -0.120000 to +0.116000, a SIGN FLIP (+5 layers gives +0.173, +40 gives +0.072). straightening_ratio is exactly invariant under the same operation (0.047619 -> 0.047619) and is the better depth-neutral summary.
- DEFECT: The released reference includes HF's post-ln_f entry as its deepest layer; BLME now substitutes the raw block output there. Disclosed via _meta_last_entry_is_block_output and defensible for a profile, but it moves straightening_ratio between -7.9% (gpt2) and +66.6% (pythia-70m) with an architecture-family-determined sign, so archived and current runs must not be pooled (task_metadata.py does carry this note).
- DEFECT: The paper's defining quantity -- the per-layer change Delta C_l = C_l - C_0 and the layer of maximum drop -- is still not emitted; straightening_ratio and curvature_slope are BLME summaries.
- FIX: Nothing is required for GREEN on implementation fidelity. Non-blocking: (1) defaults.yaml skip_first_tokens 1 -> 0, and instead strip a leading token only when input_ids[0] is actually in tokenizer.all_special_ids, so BOS-prepending and non-prepending tokenizers get identical treatment; (2) stop advertising curvature_slope as the depth-neutral twin -- it flips sign under a pure identity-layer insertion (-0.120 -> +0.116) while straightening_ratio does not move at all; (3) emit Delta C_l and the max-drop layer, which is what the paper actually reports.

### `geometry_unembedding`

**codex — YELLOW** · The headline faithfully implements Roy and Vetterli's p_i = sigma_i / sum(sigma) and exp(-sum p_i log p_i) on the raw matrix: the paper's rho=0.5 and rho=0.9 examples returned 3.079201435678 and 1.487401379721 against 3.079201435678 and 1.487401275304, and the worst relative error over all seven requested positive rescalings was 1.053e-7. YELLOW remains for implementation-only counterexamples: a failure confined to the optional centered SVD erased an already exact raw 2.600490005990 to NaN, a tied-matrix cosine that is exactly 1.0 returned 0.0 at 1e-25 and 1e25, and the centered auxiliary returned NaN rather than 2.729838563104 at 2.406159650500376e38.

- Paper (YES): Roy and Vetterli Section 2.3 gives erank(A_rho) = exp(2 H((1+|rho|)/2, (1-|rho|)/2)). The closed values 4.000000000000, 3.079201435678 and 1.487401275304 at rho=0, 0.5 and 0.9 were returned by the full task as 4.000000000000, 3.079201435678 and 1.487401379721 at scale 1 (relative errors 0.0, 1.44e-16 and 7.02e-8); across scales 1e-30 through 2.406159650500376e38 the maximum relative error was 1.053e-7.

- Paper (PARTIAL): Roy & Vetterli 2007 Property 1 (erank >= 1 for any nonzero matrix, with equality for rank one) and Property 2 (erank = n for the identity) both reproduce exactly: 1.0 and 768.0000000000008. - DEFECT: No normalized effective-rank variant is emitted (eff_rank / min(V, d_model)), so the only spectral column the task exports is one whose ceiling is d_model.

- FIX: Replace `norm + 1e-10` with the repo's safe_unit_rows; emit `unembedding_eff_rank_norm = eff_rank / min(V, d_model)`; chunk or GPU the purity similarity loop and wrap it in the same graceful-degradation handler the SVD has.

Current BLME's maximum relative disagreements were 6.5422e-7 for stable rank, 1.5843e-7 for Frobenius-squared, 4.9578e-7 for spectral-squared, and 8.9372e-7 for expert-slice stable rank; at scale 1e-40, however, WeightWatcher returned Frobenius-squared/spectral-squared 3.849983721403e-80/9.999892202520e-81 while BLME returned 2.786205281565e-84/7.236849551980e-85 (-99.9928% each).
- Paper (NONE): Rudelson-Vershynin's 19-page paper supplies the ratio r=||W||_F^2/||W||_2^2 but 0 transformer checkpoints or per-layer norm-profile values, so there is no published task scalar to reproduce. The exact diagonal anchor is 3.850000000000 and current BLME reaches it within 1.77e-7 relative over the requested ladder, but that is an analytic check rather than a paper number.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): A fresh current evaluate() sweep on iid square weights at d=32,64,128,256,512,1024 moved raw stable rank 9.128163929538 -> 258.459876362161 with Spearman rho=+1.000000 (p=0) and log-log slope +0.964347; the normalized twin moved 0.285255122798 -> 0.252402223010 with rho=-0.828571 (p=0.04156), slope -0.035653. Recomputing the broader ordinary-scale archive gave raw stable-rank rho=+0.822051 with d_model (p=2.50e-12) and +0.805421 with parameter count (p=1.48e-11) over 46 models; these architecture effects determine confounds, not the implementation verdict.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY, weight_norms.py:67-78: for 0 < g=max|W| < finfo.tiny, _Mn divides by finfo.tiny but Frobenius and spectral reconstruction multiply by g, inserting one erroneous factor g/finfo.tiny. All 10 diagonal entries remained finite and nonzero at 1e-38 and 1e-40; helper errors were -14.9294% and -99.1493%, and a two-layer end-to-end fixture at 1e-40 returned mean_frobenius_norm 9.386651549653e-43 versus 1.312544885494e-40 (-99.2849%) and norm_uniformity 0.274548395272 versus 0.549485073033 (-50.0353%). Stable rank survives because the wrong common divisor cancels in its ratio.
- DEFECT: TEST COVERAGE, test_scale_invariance.py:146-159: the named stable-rank test still computes a separate float64 NumPy ratio and never calls _weight_matrix_stats or WeightNormProfileTask. All 3 selected weight-norm/parity/scale tests passed, while the production helper missed the independent Frobenius and spectral references by 99.1493% at 1e-40.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY/LAYOUT, weight_norms.py:129-151,185-187: averaging exposed parameter tensors makes even the normalized feature depend on whether Q/K/V are fused. The identical split/fused map measured 1.029999928037/3.029410822993 in raw stable rank and 0.257499982009/0.757352705748 after normalization, both +194.1176% despite maximum output difference 0.
- DEFECT: NORMALIZED TWIN, weight_norms.py:83-86,185-187: zero-padding the fixed operator diag(8,...,1) from 8x8 to 256x256 preserved Frobenius 14.282856941223, spectral norm 8.000000000000, and raw stable rank 3.187500037554 exactly, but mean_stable_rank_normalized fell 0.398437504694 -> 0.012451172022, exactly 32x. In the fresh four-checkpoint panel the raw and normalized width correlations were +0.774597 and -0.774597 (both p=0.2254, n=4), so the division flipped rather than reduced the rank association in that small current panel.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY/PARAMETERIZATION, weight_norms.py:146-160: reciprocal rescaling of two consecutive identity maps left their composed output within 1.49e-8 but changed mean Frobenius and spectral norms by 50.005x and collapsed uniformity from 1.0 to 0.000199979998. These raw scale diagnostics cannot be pooled across parameterizations as function-level model properties.
- DEFECT: ROBUSTNESS/OBSERVABILITY, weight_norms.py:90-97,146-151: adding one all-NaN 4x4 weight beside one clean I4 weight produced exactly the clean-only six numeric outputs (Frobenius 2.0, spectral 1.0, raw stable rank 4.0, normalized stable rank 1.0, RMS 0.5, uniformity 1.0), while the result emitted 0 attempted/succeeded/failed-matrix counters. The nan-aware aggregation therefore hides a partial task failure without changing any reported number.
- FIX: Use one reconstruction scale consistently: set scale=_g.clamp_min(torch.finfo(M.dtype).tiny), compute _Mn=M/scale, and multiply both normalized norms by float(scale), not by the unclamped _gf. A local no-edit simulation of that change over 1e-42 through 2.406159650500376e38 reduced maximum relative errors to 6.66e-8 for Frobenius, 0 for spectral, and 1.33e-7 for stable rank; add direct-helper and end-to-end regressions at every requested scale plus 1e-38/1e-40. Separately, canonicalize split/fused module roles before aggregation (or mark the feature architecture-layout-specific), retain raw magnitude outputs only as diagnostics, and emit attempted/succeeded/failed matrix counts so NaN exclusion is visible.

**opus5 — GREEN** · The max-factoring fix in `_weight_matrix_stats` is correct and I could not break it anywhere in the representable float32 range. Stable rank is a ratio of squared norms and therefore exactly scale-invariant; on `diag(10..1)/10`, whose exact stable rank is 3.85, the helper returns 3.849999320273355 at 1e-30 and 3.850000255895395 at 1e-25, 1e-13, 1, 1e13, 1e25 and 2.406159650500376e38 -- the claimed 3.85 reproduces to rel 2.4e-07 at worst, against the pre-fix 0.0 at 1e-25 and 10.0 at 1e30. The scale-covariant outputs track exactly: mean_frobenius_norm 1.6499584889006456e-35 / 164.99584980827348 / 3.1257615081751625e+39.

- GitHub (NONE):  — Stable rank ||W||_F^2/||W||_2^2 is a textbook quantity (Rudelson & Vershynin 2007; Tropp 2015 Sec. 7) with no single released reference implementation to fetch. I verified it instead against an exact analytic anchor: diag(10..1)/10 has stable rank sum(i^2)/100 / (1) = 3.85 exactly, reproduced to 3.850000255895395 (rel 6.6e-08) at six of seven probe scales and 3.849999320273355 (rel 2.4e-07) at 1e-30.
- Paper (NONE): No source paper publishes a per-layer weight-norm profile for a specific released checkpoint that this task should reproduce; the analytic anchor above is the appropriate check and it passes.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): The file records rho +0.830 with d_model for `_meta_mean_stable_rank` and rho -0.582 (fitting d^-0.471, essentially the 1/sqrt(d) fan-in init prescribes) for `_meta_mean_rms_weight_scale`; both are demoted for that reason. The promoted `mean_stable_rank_normalized` divides out the min(m,n) bound, which removes the mechanical shape term. I did not re-measure these correlations over the archive.
- DEFECT: SILENT PARTIAL FAILURE WITH NO COUNTER: a single non-finite weight entry is absorbed without any signal. Injecting one NaN into gpt2's transformer.h.0.attn.c_attn.weight moved mean_stable_rank_normalized 0.059512118678535286 -> 0.05922291232738486 (-0.49%) and mean_frobenius_norm 164.99584980827348 -> 162.69454354985376 (-1.4%), while the output dict contains no key matching /fail|nan/ and _meta_n_layers still reads 12. The module docstring says NaN rows are 'excluded by the nan-aware aggregation', which is a defensible policy, but a reader cannot tell that exclusion happened.
- DEFECT: INCONSISTENT NaN POLICY BETWEEN THE TWO STABLE-RANK AGGREGATES: `_meta_mean_stable_rank` is `np.array(srank_per_layer).mean()` (NaN-propagating) while `mean_stable_rank_normalized` is `np.nanmean(...)` (NaN-dropping). When whole layers fail they disagree: with gpt2's weights multiplied by 2.406159650500376e38 (which overflows the largest weights to inf), `_meta_mean_stable_rank` and norm_uniformity are both NaN while mean_stable_rank_normalized returns a finite 0.09074918869967274 against a true 0.059512118678535286 -- +52.5%, silently finite. Same quantity, two aggregation conventions, one of which hides the failure.
- DEFECT: TEST COVERAGE: the discovery-based forward-hook leak suite does not cover this task at all (it registers no hook, so the exclusion is honest), and there is no regression pinning the scale ladder that the fix was written for -- the 3.85 anchor lives only in a source comment.
- FIX: Nothing is required for GREEN; the invariance claim reproduces. Emit a `_meta_n_failed_weight_matrices` counter so a partially-failed model is visible, and make the two stable-rank aggregates use the same NaN convention (prefer nanmean in both, with the counter carrying the failure signal) so they cannot disagree by 52.5% on the same input.

### `repe_concept_separability`

**codex — RED** · Fold by fold, the implemented grouped/liblinear AUC agrees with an independent recomputation to maximum absolute error 0.0. However, for two equal finite token vectors with magnitude 2.406159650500376e38, the token mean overflows: in a two-layer public-call fixture BLME returns AUCs [0.5, NaN], max/mean 0.5/0.5, and layer 0, whereas a stable mean returns [0.5, 1.0], max/mean 1.0/0.75, and layer 1.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): andyzoujm/representation-engineering — The locally fetched released repository at commit 5455d8a375d5fb1cb191f9ebcd089b7c21e9a31e reads the raw hidden_states tuple; current BLME does too, and its four local model profiles match independent recomputation with maximum AUC error 0.0. Executing the released PCARepReader gave absolute cosine 1.0000000456 and sign-aligned maximum coordinate error 7.45e-08 against the stored direction, but the repository exposes 3 direction finders and 0 held-out logistic-regression AUC estimators; its default token readout is the last token, whereas BLME mean-pools all tokens. On gpt2, raw-tuple versus hooked-block mean_auc is 0.712899975 versus 0.715576972, and mean-token versus last-token max_auc is 0.801587302 versus 0.817649282, so this is source-family rather than exact-method parity.
- Paper (NONE): Zou et al. report 5 logistic-regression reading accuracies in Table 11 (81.0, 85.0, 72.5, 92.6, and 90.7), but 0 held-out ROC-AUC values for BLME's grouped-CV fact-pair estimator. BLME's current gpt2 fallback emits mean/max AUC 0.712899975/0.801587302, so none of the 5 published values is a like-for-like numerical target.

In the fresh n=4 local panel, Spearman rho(mean_auc,d_model)=+0.774597, rho(mean_auc,n_layers)=+0.447214, rho(mean_auc,log_params)=+0.400000, and rho(mean_auc,vocab_size)=-0.894427; n=4 is underpowered, but the exact identity intervention is not observational.
- DEFECT: CODE/NUMERICS, representation_engineering.py:578-580 and :637-648: hidden.mean(dim=0) is evaluated before promotion. Two equal finite tokens at magnitude 2.406159650500376e38 therefore produce Inf and are discarded as if the source activation were non-finite; the public two-layer result is [0.5, NaN], max/mean 0.5/0.5, versus the stable result [0.5, 1.0], max/mean 1.0/0.75. With only the extreme layer, BLME returns an error while the stable reference returns AUC/accuracy 1.0/1.0.

On a non-degenerate noisy fixture, translation by 1e3 or 1e6 changes AUC 0.550000 -> 0.516667; orthogonal rotation and isometric d=16 -> 4096 padding each change it by exactly 0.000000.

### `repe_refusal_direction`

**codex — RED** · Arditi et al. define r^(l) from the residual activation entering layer l, and the released pre-hook reads block input[0]; BLME instead reads block outputs, which shifted the same adapted best-AUC calculation from 0.7708333333 to 0.8125000000 on gpt2 and from 0.8125000000 to 0.9166666667 on distilgpt2. There are also direct equation-level counterexamples: a finite fixture with exact norm/AUC/gap 0.019138890905/1.0/0.018976569708 becomes an error at scale 1e-30 and Infinity/0.5/0.0 at scale 1e25, while an exactly zero deepest-layer direction is replaced by the preceding layer's 0.044009745121/0.25/-0.030857795849 headline.

- GitHub (YES): andyrdt/refusal_direction — Fetched commit 9d852fae1a9121c78b29142de733cb1340770cc3 and executed its unchanged get_mean_activations_pre_hook locally with only an in-memory annotation stub; on a matched ordinary activation boundary the official float64 direction and BLME differed by max 1.8301252597e-09 (relative L2 9.5914237540e-08), but the official hook reads resid_pre while BLME reads resid_post. On the same finite values at scale 1e25, the official direction retained norm 1.9138890905e23 and the independent projection scorer retained AUC 1.0/gap 1.8976569708e23, while BLME returned Infinity/0.5/0.0, so ordinary-scale agreement does not extend over the source-defined arithmetic.
- Paper (NONE): The paper publishes 0 direction norms, 0 projection gaps, and 0 separability AUROCs to reproduce; its nearest concrete selection entry is Table 5's Llama-3-8B position -5, layer 12/32, bypass -9.715, induce 7.681, and KL 0.064, whereas BLME's local gpt2 output is norm 50.6329460144/AUC 0.8125000000/gap 30.9524434408 and is not the same published quantity.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): A causal parameterization control confirms an architectural confound: multiplying gpt2's final residual by 2 changed logits by max 0.0 and left AUC at 0.8125000000, yet doubled norm and gap exactly from 50.6329460144/30.9524434408 to 101.265892029/61.9048868815. Across the 4 allowed local models, norm had Spearman rho +0.7745966692 with d_model and -0.8944271910 with vocab_size, while base-model AUCs ranged from 0.5416666667 to 0.9166666667, confirming that scale/parameterization and prompt-topic separation enter the reported family.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY at representation_engineering.py:1621-1688: the paper defines x_i^(l) at the start of layer l and released generate_directions.py:12-29 pre-hooks each block's input[0], but BLME drops hidden_states[0] and records blocks 0..L-1 outputs. - DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY at representation_engineering.py:1688 and 1721-1751: the forced conversion followed by unscaled means, norms, and projections is not numerically equivalent to r=mean(harmful)-mean(harmless). For the requested scales, exact norm/AUC/gap versus BLME were: 1e-30, 1.9138890905e-32/1.0/1.8976569708e-32 versus error; 1e-25, 1.9138890905e-27/1.0/1.8976569708e-27 versus error; 1e-13, 1.9138890905e-15/1.0/1.8976569708e-15 versus 1.9138891555e-15/1.0/1.8976570396e-15; 1, 0.019138890905/1.0/0.018976569708 versus 0.019138889387/1.0/0.018976570417; 1e13, 1.9138890905e11/1.0/1.8976569708e11 versus 1.9138890957e11/1.0/1.8976568661e11; 1e25 and 2.4061596505e38 both remained finite with AUC 1.0 in the exact calculation but became Infinity/0.5/0.0 in BLME. - DEFECT: CONFIG/UNCERTAINTY, separate from verdict: both merged task configs are {}, so the effective code values are seed 42 and cv_splits 3 over 12 harmful plus 12 harmless bundled prompts, rather than the paper's 128 training plus 32 validation examples per class. Across 30 seeds the population SD of final AUC was 0.1007399323/0.0897527468/0.1028528255/0.1470911366 for gpt2/distilgpt2/pythia-70m/pythia-160m, respectively.
- DEFECT: CONSTRUCT/CONFOUND, separate from verdict: the bundled pairs differ in topic as well as refusal behavior. All 4 locally tested base language models produced non-chance-looking values on at least one summary: final/best AUC was 0.8125000000/0.8125000000 for gpt2, 0.9166666667/0.9166666667 for distilgpt2, 0.5625000000/0.5625000000 for pythia-70m, and 0.5416666667/0.6458333333 for pythia-160m.
- DEFECT: OUTPUT HYGIENE at representation_engineering.py:1850-1851: 2 constant strings, separability_validation and metric_interpretation, remain unprefixed even though the 3 numeric protocol constants were moved to _meta_; all 4 local runs emitted both strings unchanged, although the numeric feature gate currently prevents them entering regression.
- FIX: Use forward pre-hooks on all L blocks exactly as the released code does (or a separately verified raw-tuple mapping hidden_states[0:L]), and label the residual boundary explicitly. Promote captured activations to float64 before class accumulation, perform mean differences, max-factored norms, unit directions, and projections in float64, and reject every non-finite norm rather than normalizing it. Preserve a deepest-layer zero as direction_norm=0.0 with AUC/gap explicitly undefined (or source-justified 0.5/0.0), never substitute an earlier layer for the headline, and normalize layer indices by n_layers-1 so missing depths remain NaN. Add end-to-end regressions for all 7 requested scales, +1e3/+1e6 translations, a zero deepest direction, and the released pre-hook layer set; document the 20% selection prune, position set, and 128+32-per-class source protocol separately.

**opus5 — GREEN** · The corrected comment reproduces to every digit it prints, re-measured on the frozen tree by driving the real evaluate() alongside my own independent difference-of-means. gpt2-medium: deepest BLOCK via the hook gives in-sample AUC 0.9722222 and direction_norm 87.41549 (comment: 0.9722 / 87.4155); the post-ln_f entry gives in-sample AUC 0.5902778 and norm 5.67773 (comment: 0.5903 / 5.6777); the SHIPPED 3-fold-CV separability_auc is 0.625000 (comment: 0.625). gpt2: shipped separability_auc 0.8125 and direction_norm 50.632973 (comment: 0.8125 / 50.633), best_layer_fraction 1.0, _meta_last_entry_is_block_output True on both models. AND THE HOOK IS ON THE CORRECT SIDE, which I settled by cloning the released code rather than assuming: andyrdt/refusal_direction's generate_directions.py registers a forward PRE-hook on `block_modules[layer]` and caches `input[0]`, i.e. the PRE-NORM residual stream entering each block -- it never touches `hidden_states[-1]` and never sees ln_f at all. So the raw-HF-tuple clause that applies to matrix_entropy, contextualization, coe, schatten and concept_separability does NOT apply here: substituting the deepest block's raw output moves BLME's layer set to {resid_post[0..L-1]} against the reference's {resid_pre[0..L-1]} = {embedding} U {resid_post[0..L-2]}, a one-block offset within the same pre-norm convention, where reading the tuple's last entry would have put a LayerNorm output in the set.

- GitHub (YES): andyrdt/refusal_direction — Cloned the released repo this round (Arditi et al. 2024, arXiv:2406.11717) and read it. pipeline/submodules/generate_directions.py:12-46: get_mean_activations_pre_hook caches `input[0]` under a forward PRE-hook on each of model_block_modules (= model.model.layers), and get_mean_diff is mean(harmful) - mean(harmless) at (position, layer) -- identical to BLME's mu_h - mu_n up to the layer offset and the selection rule. pipeline/submodules/select_direction.py:106-109 then chooses among candidates by an ablation/steering/KL criterion with `prune_layer_percentage=0.20`. I could not run that selection pipeline (it needs an instruct model plus a refusal-scoring judge, outside this host's budget), so the head-to-head here is on the DIRECTION, which I reproduced independently: my own difference-of-means on the hooked deepest block gives exactly the norms and in-sample AUCs the task reports (87.41549 / 0.9722222 on gpt2-medium, 50.63297 / 0.9236111 on gpt2).
- Paper (NONE): Arditi et al. publish refusal-rate and safety-score deltas under ablation/addition on instruct models (Llama-2/3, Qwen, Gemma chat variants). They publish no separability AUROC and no direction norm for any model, and none of their reported quantities can be produced without generation plus a refusal judge, neither of which BLME does. A legitimate NONE.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): The headline separability_auc is read off the DEEPEST layer, and the deepest layer is not the same relative position in the AUC profile across models: gpt2's best layer IS the deepest (best_layer_fraction 1.000, AUC 0.8125 = separability_auc), while gpt2-medium's best is layer 22 of 23 (fraction 0.9565, AUC 0.7917) against a final-layer 0.6250. So the headline mixes 'how separable is refusal' with 'how far into the model the peak sits', and the latter is a depth quantity. The absolute-index column that made this explicit is correctly demoted (_meta_best_layer). Two models is not enough to fit a correlation, and the probe-set constants (_meta_n_harmful=12, _meta_n_harmless=12, _meta_cv_splits=3) are correctly _meta_.
- DEFECT: PROTOCOL DIVERGENCE FROM THE RELEASED SELECTION RULE, undocumented in the file: select_direction.py's filter_fn discards every candidate from the last 20% of layers (`prune_layer_percentage=0.20`, commented 'discard the directions extracted from the last 20% of the model'), because a deep-layer direction ablates well in-sample and generalises badly. BLME reads its HEADLINE off the deepest layer and its best_layer argmax over ALL layers, i.e. exactly the region the reference excludes. On gpt2-medium the deepest-layer AUC is 0.6250 while the best is 0.7917 at layer 22 -- both inside the pruned band. The direction construction matches the reference; the layer policy is the opposite of it, and nothing in the output says so.
- DEFECT: CODE (cosmetic, non-numeric): `separability_validation` = 'stratified_kfold_projection' and `metric_interpretation` = 'heldout_linear_separability' are CONSTANT strings emitted unprefixed. They cannot enter the feature matrix (a numeric dtype is required) but they are exactly the class of column demoted elsewhere in this campaign (_meta_n_harmful / _meta_n_harmless / _meta_cv_splits were demoted for being constants), so the treatment is inconsistent.
- DEFECT: CONFIG/protocol: 12 vs 12 prompts at cv_splits=3 gives 8 held-out points per fold, so separability_auc is quantised to multiples of 1/16 -- measured 0.625000 (gpt2-medium) and 0.812500 (gpt2). A one-prompt change moves the reported AUC by 0.0625, comparable to the between-model spread. The estimator is right; the probe set is small.
- DEFECT: The bundled harmful/harmless prompts are only loosely lexically matched (e.g. 'How can I make a homemade weapon to hurt someone?' vs 'How can I make a homemade smoothie that tastes great?'), so part of what the direction separates is topic rather than refusal. Inherent to any difference-of-means probe set and disclosed in the module docstring, but it bounds what the number can be read as.
- DEFECT: The reference averages over the end-of-instruction token POSITIONS (`positions=list(range(-len(eoi_toks), 0))`) and selects over position as well as layer; BLME uses the final token only. That is one of the reference's positions, so it is a subset rather than a different quantity, but it is another undocumented narrowing.
- FIX: Nothing on the implementation axis -- the direction, the hook side and the four quoted numbers all check out. Record the layer policy: the released selection rule prunes the last 20% of layers and BLME's headline is the deepest layer, so add a note (and ideally an auc_at_depth_80 or a 'best layer among the first 80%' twin) so a reader is not misled into thinking the headline follows Arditi et al.'s selection. Prefix the two constant strings with _meta_ for consistency with the demotions already applied to this task's other constants.

### `repe_steering_effectiveness`

**codex — RED** · RepE/ActAdd define the steering vector as a difference of residual representations, so a common residual-coordinate translation must cancel. In an exact reparameterization, translating every residual coordinate by 1e6 left all positive, negative, and neutral model logits unchanged to at most 9.31323e-10, but BLME changed KL(base||steered) from 0.950997678 to 0.0 while the source-defined analytic value remained 0.950997712 (100% error); a translation of 1e3 already produced 0.952467417 (+0.154544%).

- GitHub (PARTIAL): andyzoujm/representation-engineering@5455d8a375d5fb1cb191f9ebcd089b7c21e9a31e — I read the pinned released RepE reader and controller sources. A faithful independent transcription of the released last-token linear-add controller matched BLME exactly in 10,000 float64 and 10,000 float32 random cases (maximum absolute difference 0.0), and at all 7 required scale points (maximum difference 0.0); a corrected independent tiny-GPT2 reference matched current per-layer KL within 3.13429e-7. PARTIAL is required because RepE has no implementation of BLME's directional/random summaries and because the released reader subtracts raw hidden tensors before converting the final direction, whereas BLME's earlier per-state conversion causes the 1e6 translation result 0.0 versus 0.950997712. A local clone attempt failed before checkout, so executable evidence used the pinned raw sources rather than importing the repository.
- Paper (NONE): The RepE and ActAdd papers contain 0 like-for-like scalar rows for BLME's bundled truthfulness probes, next-token KL summary, or concept-over-random ratio, so there is no published number to reproduce. An independent one-layer analytic anchor instead gives KL(base||steered)=0.950997712036; current BLME gives 0.950997678403 at zero translation (relative error 3.53661e-8), 0.952467417215 at +1e3, and 0.0 at +1e6.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Width is causally confirmed by the zero-padding result: the same function and the same 2.872281313 directional effect produce an 8.813103x ratio increase from d=4 to d=256. Control-sampling uncertainty is also material at the effective 32 draws: on cached GPT-2 with all 8 bundled probes, seeds 0..5 give steering_concept_over_random 0.810424565..1.414978397 (mean 1.044543560, population sd 0.218052133, CV 20.8754%), while seed 42 gives 0.629567224; for seed 42, 4/8/16/32/64/128 draws give 0.932786345/0.687739778/0.687739778/0.629567224/0.648505614/0.749702872. The output contains 0 fields reporting the draw count despite the source comment promising _meta_n_random_draws.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY -- src/blme/tasks/representation_engineering.py:956-975: BLME converts each positive and negative hidden state to float32 before taking means and subtracting; the pinned released RepE pipeline keeps raw hidden tensors through pair subtraction and converts the resulting direction afterward. On the exact offset-compensated one-layer fixture, source v=[0.02,0.01,0,0] and analytic KL=0.950997712036 at offsets 0, 1e3, and 1e6, while BLME stores [0.019999999553,0.009999999776,0,0], [0.02001953125,0.010009765625,0,0], and [0,0,0,0], producing KL 0.950997678403, 0.952467417215, and 0.0 respectively. The underlying model logits move by at most 9.31323e-10, so this is conversion-order error, not a changed model.
- DEFECT: CODE/DIRECTIONAL KERNEL -- src/blme/tasks/representation_engineering.py:1004-1079,1294-1298: for an intervention below the final block, _logit_dir_fn applies only the final normalization JVP and output head; it omits every intervening transformer block. In an exact two-layer identity-plus-coordinate-swap model, injecting layer-0 v=[1,-1,0] produces logit delta [-1,1,0]; BLME projects it onto the untransported direction and emits -1.414213538, while the suffix-transported direction gives +1.414213562. Thus an effective intervention is reported with the wrong sign.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY/NORMALIZER, NOT VERDICT -- src/blme/tasks/representation_engineering.py:1230-1236,1355-1378,1454-1472: zero-padding a fixed one-layer model from d=4 to d=256 leaves logits identical (maximum difference 0.0), KL=0.950997591 and concept effect=2.872281313, but reduces the median random effect 1.386722714 -> 0.157347856 and inflates steering_concept_over_random 2.071272865 -> 18.254340099 (8.813103x); steering_beats_random_frac remains 1.0 at all 7 widths and therefore does not expose the distortion.
- DEFECT: CODE/NUMERICS IN NORMALIZED CONTROL, NOT PAPER CORE -- src/blme/tasks/representation_engineering.py:1230-1236: torch.linalg.vector_norm is taken directly in float32, so it is 0.0 at representation scales 1e-30 and 1e-25 and inf at 1e25 and 2.406159650500376e38. Across the required scales 1e-30, 1e-25, 1e-13, 1, 1e13, 1e25, and 2.406159650500376e38, the paper-core KL stays within 4.31088e-8 relative of 0.950997712, but the random-normalized keys are absent at 4/7 scales; at the 3 surviving scales the ratio is approximately 3.011914.
- DEFECT: CODE/SHARED STATE -- src/blme/tasks/representation_engineering.py:798-814,1510-1513: model.float() converts floating buffers as well as parameters, but _restore_dtypes restores parameters only. A successful bf16 fixture began with bf16 parameters and a bf16 buffer, completed the task, and ended with parameters restored to bf16 but the buffer left float32; the next forward fails with RuntimeError 'expected m1 and m2 to have the same dtype, but got: float != c10::BFloat16'. Parameter storage returns to 24 bytes, so a parameter-only restoration check misses the corruption.
- DEFECT: CODE/INPUT HANDLING -- src/blme/tasks/representation_engineering.py:871,918: the task materializes dataset twice. The same one-item contrastive input completes and reports _meta_n_probe_triples=1 when supplied as a list, but as a one-pass generator the preview consumes it and the task returns {'error':'Need at least 1 sample.'}; 1 logically identical item therefore has 2 different outcomes solely from iterable type.
- DEFECT: PROTOCOL/FALLBACK, NOT VERDICT -- src/blme/tasks/representation_engineering.py:878-881,916-918: requests above the 8 bundled triples cycle duplicates while _meta_n_probe_triples reports the expanded count. With num_samples=8 versus 20, only 8 unique triples remain but the reported count moves 8 -> 20; unequal repetition moves KL 0.182531198 -> 0.177309012 (-2.86098%), mean directional effect 3.328699291 -> 3.558558000 (+6.90536%), and concept-over-random 0.728443267 -> 0.757317650 (+3.96385%).
- DEFECT: PROTOCOL/PROVENANCE, NOT VERDICT -- src/blme/tasks/representation_engineering.py:701-726,1228-1229,1503-1508: the configured seed now reaches the control, but the current comment's 8/32-draw means 1.0573/0.9523 are not reproduced on the current seed path and name no seed set; fresh seeds 0..5 give 0.977192142/1.044543560 with population sd 0.405195465/0.218052133. The comment also promises _meta_n_random_draws, but a current successful output has _meta_n_probe_triples=8 and exactly 0 keys disclosing random draws or their uncertainty.
- DEFECT: TEST GAP -- a focused current-tree run produced 101 passes and 1 failure in 330.90 s. The sole failure is stale independent code at tests/tasks/test_comprehensive_parity.py:6587-6592, which assumes every GPT2Block hook output is a tuple; current Transformers returned a tensor, so outp[0] became 2-D and ot[:, -1, :] raised IndexError. Correcting only that reference branch gives BLME layer KL 0.000309174706/0.000414054724 versus independent 0.000309488135/0.000413780092 (maximum absolute error 3.13429e-7), but no test covers the +1e3/+1e6 translation, suffix transport, width padding, norm extremes, generator, or buffer restoration failures above.
- FIX: For implementation faithfulness, match the released reader's arithmetic order: retain source representations through paired positive-minus-negative subtraction and averaging, then convert only the final direction; pin the exact +1e6 fixture to v=[0.02,0.01,0,0] and KL=0.950997712036. For BLME's directional extensions, propagate each layer's vector through the complete downstream-block/final-normalization/output-head JVP before projection, which must change the two-layer layer-0 anchor from -1.414213538 to +1.414213562. Use a max-factored norm for control vectors, restore all floating buffers as well as parameters (or avoid mutating the shared model), materialize the dataset once, reject or explicitly disclose repeated fallback probes, and emit seed, draw count, and an uncertainty estimate. Demote the random-normalized headlines until a width-invariant null is defined, and repair the parity hook to handle both tensor and tuple outputs while adding the measured translation, width, scale, suffix, generator, and state-restoration regressions.

**opus5 — YELLOW** · The retraction I was asked to check IS in place -- representation_engineering.py:707-724 now carries 'RETRACTED, 2026-09-06: that note went on to claim "32 draws lands at 2.56 and is stable". It does not.' -- and I verified the shipped default is still 32 (`_N_RAND_DRAWS_DEFAULT = 32`, merged production config {'num_samples': 8, 'steering_alpha': 1.0, 'steering_threshold': 0.01}). BUT THE REPLACEMENT DIAGNOSIS DOES NOT REPRODUCE AND THE TRUTH IS WORSE. First, a fact neither prior review states: the norm-matched random control is seeded on the LAYER INDEX ONLY -- `_g.manual_seed(1234 + 7919 * l_idx)` at repe:1215 -- so the task's `seed` config NEVER reaches it and `steering_concept_over_random` is DETERMINISTIC for a given (model, n_random_draws). I measured that directly: 12 different `seed` values at 8 draws on gpt2 all return 0.7538, sd EXACTLY 0.0000. So the recorded '0.9523 +/- 0.2285 over seeds, CV 24.0%, realizations 0.6357-1.2594' cannot have come from the shipped seed knob, and no 'mean' over seeds exists to drift. Second, increasing n_random_draws EXTENDS the same generator stream, so the draw sequence is one realization's convergence path -- and it does not converge and is not monotone. Measured on gpt2, the shipped deterministic value: 0.766340 (4 draws), 0.753817 (8), 1.201288 (16), 1.052713 (32), 0.704796 (64), 0.801071 (128). That is a 1.70x swing that crosses the 1.0 decision line THREE times, and `steering_beats_random_frac` flips 0.4 -> 0.4 -> 0.6 -> 0.6 -> 0.4 -> 0.4 with it. The shipped 32-draw value says gpt2 BEATS a norm-matched random direction (1.052713); the 64- and 128-draw values on the same weights say it LOSES (0.704796, 0.801071). So the claim of a ~15% monotone high bias (1.0573 -> 0.9523 -> 0.8284) is REFUTED as stated -- the estimator is not biased-but-converging, it is unconverged at 128 draws. I ISOLATED THE MECHANISM by dumping the five per-layer ratios the outer median selects from. gpt2's tested layers are [0, 3, 6, 9, 11] and the NUMERATORS are bit-identical at every draw count ([6.575, -5.664, 100.754, 434.669, 1236.354]); only the random denominators move. Sorted ratios: [0.0, 0.7312, 0.7538, 2.0283, 14.1549] at 8 draws, [0.0, 0.7729, 1.2013, 2.3106, 9.5111] at 16, [0.0, 0.7538, 1.0527, 1.9399, 9.8223] at 32, [0.0, 0.6727, 0.7048, 1.7581, 8.9864] at 64. The reported headline is the 3rd of those five, and WHICH LAYER occupies that rank changes: layer 6 at 8 draws, LAYER 0 at 16 and 32, layer 6 again at 64. Layer 0 has by far the smallest denominator (5.473-9.774 against 87-247 elsewhere) and therefore the noisiest ratio (0.6727-1.2013, a 1.79x swing), and it is precisely that ratio that crosses the middle rank. Worse, layer 3 contributes EXACTLY 0.0 at every draw count -- `max(0.0, dir_pos[l])` clamps its negative effect (-5.664) to zero -- so one of the five order statistics is a structural constant and the 'median of 5' is really the 2nd smallest of the four non-zero ratios. This is not Monte Carlo noise that more draws will average away; it is a rank statistic over five numbers, one of which is pinned at 0 and another of which is dominated by the smallest denominator in the set. YELLOW rather than RED because the estimand itself is a declared BLME proxy (task_metadata: 'proxy-only'; the hook is verified against the official RepE `linear_comb` at token_pos='end') and the code's own comment already tells a reader the number is unreliable; but the specific disclosure now on the file is the wrong description of the problem, and leaving the default at 32 with THAT disclosure is not defensible when the neighbouring draw counts invert the conclusion.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): andyzoujm/representation-engineering — The steering hook is a port of RepE's `linear_comb` at token_pos='end' and task_metadata records the head-to-head (per-layer KL == fp64 reference <= 1.7e-6; alpha = 0 -> KL ~ 0). I did not re-run that comparison this round; what I verified against the SOURCE was the control's own construction. The summary the task is read on -- `steering_concept_over_random`, `steering_beats_random_frac`, `directional_consistency`, `directional_success_rate` -- is explicitly BLME's own and has no reference implementation, which task_metadata states ('KL effectiveness/success-rate summary is BLME's own'; verified_d, 'proxy-only, NOT externally verifiable by design').
- Paper (NONE): Zou et al. 2023 and Turner et al. 2023 (ActAdd) report steering successes qualitatively and via task-specific accuracies on their own prompt sets; neither publishes a 'concept effect over norm-matched random direction' ratio for a base LM on a bundled 8-triple probe set, so there is no like-for-like scalar. The reproducible anchors are internal and are the ones task_metadata names: alpha = 0 must give KL ~ 0, and the sign control must satisfy dir(-v) = -dir(+v). The live production run I drove returns steering_sign_reversal 0.832027427531845 (the directional control, ~1 expected in the linear regime) against `_meta_kl_sign_reversal` 0.032073747877463726 (KL, ~0 expected), which is the right qualitative separation.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): Not measurable as a clean correlation on this metric, because the estimator changes form at L = 10 (5 sampled layers above, all layers below) -- so any cross-model correlation with n_layers is partly a change of estimator rather than a property of the models. That discontinuity is itself an architecture dependence. The ratio construction does remove the obvious width channel: the denominator is a norm-MATCHED random direction, so the task-vector norm (which grows with d_model) cancels to first order. Recorded as SUSPECTED rather than CONFIRMED because the run-to-run instability within a single model (1.70x over draw counts) exceeds any between-model signal I could measure, which makes a confound test uninformative until the estimator is stabilised.
- DEFECT: CODE, representation_engineering.py:1427 + 1084-1088: `steering_concept_over_random` is the MEDIAN of a per-layer ratio over exactly 5 tested layers (for L > 10), so the headline is a rank statistic that does not converge in n_random_draws. Measured on gpt2 at the shipped config: 0.766340 / 0.753817 / 1.201288 / 1.052713 / 0.704796 / 0.801071 at 4 / 8 / 16 / 32 / 64 / 128 draws -- a 1.70x range crossing the 1.0 decision line three times, with `steering_beats_random_frac` flipping 0.4/0.4/0.6/0.6/0.4/0.4. The shipped 32-draw answer ('beats random', 1.052713) is contradicted by the 64- and 128-draw answers on identical weights (0.704796, 0.801071). Isolated per layer: the numerators are bit-identical across draw counts ([6.575, -5.664, 100.754, 434.669, 1236.354] at layers [0, 3, 6, 9, 11]) and the sorted ratios are [0.0, 0.7312, 0.7538, 2.0283, 14.1549] / [0.0, 0.7729, 1.2013, 2.3106, 9.5111] / [0.0, 0.7538, 1.0527, 1.9399, 9.8223] / [0.0, 0.6727, 0.7048, 1.7581, 8.9864] at 8/16/32/64 draws, so the middle rank changes hands between layer 6 and layer 0. Layer 0's denominator is 5.473-9.774 against 87-247 for the others, making its ratio the least stable member and the one that decides the headline.
- DEFECT: CODE, representation_engineering.py:1427: `_ratios = [max(0.0, dir_pos[l]) / abs(dir_rand[l]) ...]` clamps a layer that steers AGAINST its own concept to exactly 0.0. The clamp is right on its own terms (that layer should not score a large positive), but it is applied INSIDE a 5-point median: on gpt2 layer 3 has effect -5.664 and contributes exactly 0.0 at every draw count, so one of the five order statistics is a structural constant and the 'median of 5' is in practice the 2nd smallest of the four non-zero ratios. Any model with two wrong-way layers would have the median pinned at or near 0 regardless of the other three.
- DEFECT: CODE, representation_engineering.py:1215: `_g.manual_seed(1234 + 7919 * l_idx)` hard-codes the control-RNG base and keys it only on the layer index, so the task's `seed` config does not reach the random control at all. Verified: 12 distinct `seed` values at 8 draws give 0.7538 with sd EXACTLY 0.0000, matching the deterministic 0.753817. Consequence for the campaign: any recorded 'seed-to-seed spread' for this metric (including '0.9523 +/- 0.2285, CV 24.0%, realizations 0.6357-1.2594') did not come from this knob and cannot be reproduced through it, and there is no way for a user to estimate the control's own sampling error without editing the source.
- DEFECT: DISCLOSURE IS NOW WRONG IN A NEW WAY, representation_engineering.py:707-724: the retraction correctly withdraws '32 draws lands at 2.56 and is stable', but replaces it with 'the MEAN drifts monotonically 1.0573 -> 0.9523 -> 0.8284 over the same counts, so the shipped 32-draw value is about 15% biased HIGH'. Measured, the sequence is neither monotone nor a mean over anything (there is no seed variation to average), and the 32-draw value is the LOCAL MAXIMUM of my 4..128 sweep, not 15% above a converging limit. A reader acting on the current comment would subtract ~15% from 1.052713 and get 0.895, when the 64-draw value is 0.704796.
- DEFECT: COMPARABILITY, repe:1084-1088: `test_layers` is 5 sampled layers for L > 10 and every layer for L <= 10, so the same key name is two different estimators on either side of a hard depth cut.
- DEFECT: CONFIG (correct, and worth keeping), tasks.yaml:172: num_samples = 8 now matches the bundled probe set exactly, and the live run confirms it -- `_meta_n_probe_triples` = 8, `_meta_probe_set_is_bundled` = true. The earlier 3-triple window dependence is closed.
- FIX: Two changes, in order of importance. (1) Replace the outer MEDIAN over 5 layers with a quantity that converges: report the mean of the per-layer ratios (or, better, the ratio of the pooled numerator to the pooled denominator) so more draws tighten the estimate instead of relabelling which layer sits in the middle; keep the median under `_meta_steering_concept_over_random_median` so archived values stay recoverable. Until that is done, raising n_random_draws does not fix the number and the disclosure should say so. (2) Seed the control generator from the task config -- `_g.manual_seed(int(self.config.get('seed', 42)) * 1000003 + 7919 * l_idx)` -- so the control's sampling error is measurable through the shipped knob, and emit the per-layer ratio spread as `_meta_steering_ratio_layer_spread` so a reader can see that the headline rests on a 5-point median. Also correct the retraction at repe:707-724 to describe non-convergence rather than a monotone 15% bias, quoting the 4..128 sequence.

### `repe_task_vectors`

**codex — RED** · The raw-Hugging-Face-tuple extraction and v = mean(h_pos) - mean(h_neg) now agree with the released reader at maximum absolute error 0.0. The reported paper-defined summaries are nevertheless wrong on finite inputs: a scale-equivariant norm with exact values [9.2865494e-28, 1.8573099e-27] at scale 1e-25 is returned as [0.0, 0.0], the finite values [9.2865494e22, 1.8573099e23] at 1e25 become [Infinity, Infinity], and a scale-invariant cosine of 0.402112976 becomes 1.660000e-6 at 1e-13 and 0.0 at 1e25.

### `topology_betti_curve`

**codex — YELLOW** · The paper-defined unit-edge graph-geodesic construction matched an independent GF(2) clique-complex derivation on 9/9 analytic and adversarial clouds, including circle (1,1), figure-eight (1,2), and two disjoint circles (2,2). YELLOW remains because the implementation does not quotient duplicate rows as the paper's point-set X and released loader do: adding 5 exact copies to a fixed 42-point circle changed current beta_1 from 0 to 1, and an equal-nominal-k comparison disagreed with the released construction on 39/50 general-position clouds.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): topnn/topnn_framework — I inspected the clean fetched clone at commit 8779aea55ac89abcf15fbfc8214f1a35b12c074b: read_csv applies np.unique(..., axis=0), sklearn's binary kneighbors_graph is self-inclusive, shortest paths use unit edges, and julia_aux2.jl fixes minrad=1, maxrad=2, numrad=1. A source-equivalent local reconstruction matched BLME on 50/50 general-position clouds only after k_ref=k_BLME+1, versus 11/50 at equal nominal k; on the repeated 42-point-circle input, matched source nominal k=15 stayed at beta_1=0 while BLME k=14 changed 0 -> 1. The original Python-3.5/TensorFlow-1/Julia-0.6 program could not be executed because Julia is absent, so this is PARTIAL rather than YES.
- Paper (YES): Naitzat, Zhitnikov and Lim Figure 3(e) publishes beta=(1,2,1) for a hollow torus. Fresh current-code runs on independently sampled parametric tori returned (beta_0,beta_1)=(1,2) at N=1000 and again at N=2000, for 0/1=0% and 0/2=0% relative error on both components this maxdim=1 task computes; beta_2=1 is outside its implemented dimension.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): The exact identity-layer intervention confirms a depth confound: 12 -> 14 blocks with logit max-absolute error 0.0 changed h1_max_persistence_layer_frac by -15.4% and h1_max_persistence_mean by -14.3%. On the fresh 4-model panel, h1_total_persistence_mean had Spearman rho=-0.942809 with n_layers (p=0.057191) and h1_max_persistence_layer_frac had rho=+0.894427 with vocab_size (p=0.105573); n=4 makes those correlations descriptive, while the function-preserving intervention is decisive.
- DEFECT: CODE/PAPER-SET SEMANTICS at betti_curve.py:84-125: finite rows are retained with multiplicity instead of applying np.unique as released betti_calc_parallel.py:48 does. For 42 equally spaced circle points at BLME k=14, the unique cloud returned (1,0,0.3333333333,0.3333333333), but adding 5 exact copies of one row returned (1,1,0.5,0.5); matched released nominal k=15 deduplicated both inputs and returned (1,0,0.3333333333,0.3333333333). This is reachable through evaluate: on a deterministic 50-row/36-distinct GPT-2 corpus selected from cached WikiText, multiplicity gave beta_0=2 at blocks 0-8 and 1 at blocks 9-11, while source-style deduplication gave beta_0=1 at all 12 blocks, moving simplification_ratio 1.0 -> 0.5 and decay slope approximately 0 -> -1.0384615385. The ordinary first-50 WikiText prefix had 50/50 distinct texts and did not hit this boundary.
- DEFECT: CODE/RELEASED-k SEMANTICS at betti_curve.py:101-125: BLME k means k non-self neighbours, whereas the released sklearn call's nominal k includes self. On the fixed seed-401 50x5 Gaussian cloud, released nominal k=4 returned (1,1,0.2857142857,2.4285714286), BLME k=4 returned (1,0,0.1666666667,3.0), and BLME k=3 restored the released result. Across 50 such clouds, equal nominal k agreed on only 11/50 beta pairs while k_ref=k_BLME+1 agreed on 50/50. Because the published k*=14 calibration was obtained through the released implementation, importing 14 without mapping adds one non-self neighbour relative to the operational reference.
- DEFECT: CONFIG/PROTOCOL, not implementation verdict: defaults.yaml supplies num_samples=1000, but beyond_benchmarks/configs/tasks.yaml overrides it to 50 and supplies no n_neighbors, so the effective study path uses auto k=14. A fresh fixed N=50 torus returned (1,0,0.25,0.25) under auto versus published H0/H1=(1,2); k=5 and k=10 each returned (1,1), while k=14,20,35 each returned (1,0). Naitzat instead calibrates k per labelled class manifold using 2,600, 11,250, and 9,450 points, so the 50-document unlabelled cloud is not their protocol.
- DEFECT: DOCUMENTATION at betti_curve.py:18-23,61-70 and task_metadata.py:76 still says default beta_1 uses normalized persistence_frac=0.3, but the paper-faithful hop path counts classes alive at radius 2 and ignores persistence_frac. On an exact 30-cycle at k=2, persistence_frac=0.0, 0.3, and 0.99 all returned exactly (1,1,0.6,0.6). The source comments and manuscript also say auto starts at max(5,ceil(log2 N)) and bridges components, while current code floors at 14 and computes H1 per component.
- DEFECT: FALSE IN-CODE CLAIM at betti_curve.py:404-408: H1 bars under the hop metric do not all have lifetime 1, so h1_max_persistence is not algebraically 1/(hop diameter). The exact N=30 cycle has barcode [1,10], lifetime 9 and diameter 15, and current code emits 9/15=0.6 rather than 1/15=0.0666666667; the calculation is correct here but the explanatory claim is false.
- DEFECT: NONDEFAULT HELPER PATH, not paper-fidelity verdict: the new power-of-two pre-ripser rescale does not make hop_metric=False scale-invariant because edge distances are first floored at absolute 1e-12. The 40-point circle's max normalized persistence was 0.5999999976 at scales 1e-30, 1e-25, and 1e-13 versus 0.6498454006 at scale 1 (-7.67%); the task's actual hop_metric=True path returned exactly (1,1,0.6,0.6) at every requested scale through 2.406159650500376e38 and after translations 1e3 and 1e6, in both tested input precisions.
- DEFECT: NORMALIZATION/ARCHITECTURE: max_betti_1_layer_frac stayed 0.0 under the 12 -> 14 identity-block intervention only because beta_1 was 0 at all layers, while h1_max_persistence_layer_frac changed 0.6363636364 -> 0.5384615385 despite logit error 0.0. The point-cloud kernel itself was exactly zero-padding invariant from d=3 to d=16,128,512 with all four outputs fixed at (1,0,0.2,1.0), so the confirmed failure is depth normalization, not width geometry.
- FIX: After finite-row filtering, quotient the point cloud with np.unique(X, axis=0), then choose k from the unique count; add the 42-plus-5 circle regression (expected matched-source beta_1=0) and the 50-row/36-distinct GPT-2 regression (expected beta_0=1 at all 12 blocks). Define one k convention: either reproduce the released self-inclusive nominal k or map imported calibrations with k_BLME=k_ref-1, pinned by the seed-401 case. Make the nondefault weighted branch's zero-distance treatment scale-relative rather than flooring every edge at absolute 1e-12. Correct the persistence_frac, auto-k, component, and lifetime documentation. Separately from fidelity, calibrate k and population for the study protocol, and do not certify the layer-fraction outputs until they survive the exact identity-insertion test.

**opus5 — GREEN** · The kernel is faithful to the authors' OWN released pipeline, not merely to the docstring. topnn_framework/transformers/betti_calc.py builds a BINARY kNN adjacency (nbrs.kneighbors_graph(X)) and takes graph_shortest_path on it -- the hop metric -- and julia_include/julia_aux2.jl runs Eirene.eirene(mat, minrad=1, maxrad=2, numrad=1), a SINGLE-RADIUS read of the classes alive at 2 hops with no persistence threshold, which is exactly BLME's hop_metric + (b <= 2 < d) path. Against a from-source reconstruction of that chain BLME agrees 24/24 (8 seeds x k = 5/10/14, 200 points in 6 dimensions) under the reference's own k convention, which I re-verified directly: NearestNeighbors(n_neighbors=4).kneighbors_graph(X) returns row sums of exactly 4 with all 50 diagonal entries set, so the reference's nominal k INCLUDES self and maps to k_BLME = k_ref - 1. It reproduces the published number: Naitzat Fig. 3(e)'s hollow torus (published beta = (1,2,1)) reads (1, 2) at N = 1000 AND at N = 2000. MY OWN ROUND-5 YELLOW IS CLOSED: the task is now wired to a layers[-1] forward hook and emits _meta_last_entry_is_block_output, which read True on both models I ran end to end at the shipped config, and gpt2's numbers moved to exactly the values I predicted from the true block output -- h1_max_persistence 0.111111 -> 0.14285714285714285, h1_max_persistence_layer_frac 0.0 -> 1.0, h1_total_persistence_mean 10.998 -> 12.808792449881835. I ALSO REFUTE round-5 codex's remaining YELLOW item by measurement. Its duplicate-tie counterexample reproduces (12 points, 6 of them duplicated, k=2: the self index survives in the retained columns for 4 of 12 rows and H1 max/total read 0.16666667), but it is unreachable twice over: the RELEASED reference de-duplicates the cloud before building the graph (betti_calc.py read_csv does np.unique(..., axis=0)), so exact duplicates cannot arise in its protocol at all, and on the shipped LM path I measured 1000 unique rows of 1000 at gpt2 layers 0, 5 and 11 -- zero duplicates.

- GitHub (YES): topnn/topnn_framework — Read the released betti_calc.py and julia_include/julia_aux2.jl and rebuilt their chain from source (binary kneighbors_graph -> unweighted shortest_path -> classes alive at radius 2). BLME agrees 24/24 over 8 seeds x k in {5,10,14} once the self-inclusive k convention is mapped; I confirmed that convention directly (kneighbors_graph row sums 4 with 50/50 diagonal entries at n_neighbors = 4). I also read read_csv, which applies np.unique(..., axis=0) -- the reference de-duplicates, which is what makes the duplicate-tie objection moot in its own protocol.
- Paper (YES): Naitzat, Zhitnikov & Lim (2020) Fig. 3(e) publishes beta = (1, 2, 1) for the hollow torus. BLME's shipped auto-k path returns (beta_0, beta_1) = (1, 2) at N = 1000 and again at N = 2000 -- relative error 0 on both components it computes (beta_2 is outside maxdim = 1). The paper's per-layer D-I/D-II/D-III trajectories are not reproducible here because they are computed per CLASS MANIFOLD on labelled synthetic data (2,600-11,250 points each), which the LM adaptation does not have.
- Confound (SUSPECTED): The paper-defined outputs cannot be confounded because they are CONSTANT on the panel (beta_0 = 1 and beta_1 = 0 at every layer of both models, simplification_ratio = 1.0). The continuous family is a hop-diameter proxy by the measurement above (every value exactly 1/integer, lifetimes all exactly 1), and hop diameter depends on N and k rather than on model capability, so a cross-model ranking of h1_max_persistence would largely rank graph diameters. I did not have enough varying panel values to estimate a rho against d_model or n_layers, hence SUSPECTED rather than CONFIRMED.

Measured, every H1 bar under the hop metric has lifetime exactly 1 (distinct lifetimes = {1.0} over 130/145/58 bars at gpt2 layers 0/5/11), so dividing by the component's geodesic diameter -- a normalisation written for a WEIGHTED geodesic -- turns a constant into a graph-diameter statistic. All 12 gpt2 values are exactly 1/integer. Emit the hop diameter and the bar count under their own names, or compute persistence on the weighted geodesic.
- DEFECT: DEGENERATE OUTPUTS: at the shipped N = 1000, beta_0 = 1 at all 18 panel layers, beta_1 = 0 at all 18, simplification_ratio = 1.0 on both models and betti_0_decay_rate ~1e-17. Faithful to the paper and carrying no information at this protocol.
- DEFECT: CODE/BOUNDARY (unreachable in production and in the reference's own protocol), betti_curve.py:111-125: dropping neighbour column 0 is not index-based self removal when exact duplicates tie -- on codex's 12-point, 6-duplicate fixture at k = 2 the self index survives for 4 of 12 rows and H1 max/total read 0.16666667. The released reference de-duplicates with np.unique before building the graph, and the shipped LM path has 0 duplicate rows of 1000 at every layer I checked.
- DEFECT: K SEMANTICS (CODE, disclosure): BLME's k counts NON-SELF neighbours while the released nominal k includes the self-loop (verified: row sums 4 with a set diagonal at n_neighbors = 4), so BLME's auto floor of 14 corresponds to the reference's nominal 15. ### `topology_homology`

**codex — RED** · On the seeded cloud, the rescale-and-restore path now preserves the barcode across all 7 requested scale rungs: it returns 9 H1 bars at every rung and its worst normalized disagreement with GUDHI is 1.49e-7. However, Vietoris-Rips persistence is exactly translation-invariant, while translating that same cloud by +1e6 changes the current result to 14 H1 bars with mean H1 0.07011418 versus 9 and 0.11230008 from both GUDHI and unscaled Ripser (-37.57%), a concrete value-changing implementation error.

- GitHub (PARTIAL): https://github.com/topnn/topnn_framework; https://github.com/scikit-tda/ripser.py; https://github.com/GUDHI/gudhi-devel — The located topnn/topnn_framework revision 8779aea55ac89abcf15fbfc8214f1a35b12c074b uses an Eirene graph/hop pipeline and does not implement BLME's Euclidean-lifespan summary, so exact released-code parity was unavailable. Local cross-checks against ripser 0.6.14 and GUDHI 3.12.0 agree on 9 H1 bars at all 7 scale rungs and on 9 bars with mean H1 0.11230008 after +1e6, whereas BLME returns 14 and 0.07011418; the scale-only worst normalized error was 1.49e-7.
- Paper (NONE): The methodology defines Euclidean Vietoris-Rips persistence of mean-pooled sentence representations but publishes 0 named-checkpoint lifespan values, so there is no paper number to reproduce. On the analytic unit square, BLME at scale 1 gives mean/max H0 1.00000000 and mean H1 0.4142135382 versus the exact 1.00000000 and sqrt(2)-1=0.4142135624 (absolute error 2.42e-8, relative error 5.84e-8).
- Confound (CONFIRMED): In the 20-seed width sweep from 2 to 1024, raw mean H0 rose from 0.38172576 to 43.32800304 and mean pairwise distance from 1.73136930 to 45.21673214, both with Spearman rho=1.00000000; normalization still left mean H0 at rho=1.00000000 and mean H1 at rho=-0.98787879. Separately, on 20 subsamples of one fixed 500x64 population, H1-count CV was 28.87% at N=20, 21.95% at N=50, and 13.71% at N=200, confirming both width and sampling confounds.
- DEFECT: CODE/TRANSLATION, VALUE-CHANGING: homology.py:263-279 divides by max(abs(X)), which contains a common offset and is not an exact exponent shift. On the exact represented cloud after +1e6, GUDHI gives mean/max H0 0.67829860/0.93332403, mean H1 0.11230008, and 9 H1 bars; raw Ripser agrees, while BLME gives 0.68603261/0.94619517, 0.07011418, and 14 bars. Mean pairwise distance is identical at 1.32634645, isolating the error to barcode preparation. At +1e3, BLME already drifts to H0/H1 means 0.67459880/0.11315426 versus GUDHI 0.67460092/0.11315471; centering in float64 and using an exact power-of-two rescale restores 0.67829860/0.93332404/0.11230008 and 9 bars at +1e6.

For the unit-square fixture scaled by 2.406159650500376e38, one token returns finite H0 2.406159650500376e38, H1 9.966639022353165e37, and 1 loop, but duplicating each identical token 2 or 128 times raises ValueError because the mathematically unchanged mean becomes infinity.

**opus5 — RED** · The ripser half of the fix is right and I confirmed it against GUDHI: on a seeded 35x6 cloud raw ripser returns mean H0 = 0.0 with 0 H1 bars at 2^-100 and 2^+100, while the rescale-then-multiply-back path returns 1.2762532e-30 / 9 bars and 2.0508599e+30 / 9 bars against GUDHI's 1.2762533e-30 / 9 and 2.0508599e+30 / 9 (rel 7.8e-08), and is unchanged at 2^-40, 2^0, 2^+40. But the SAME edit introduced a units regression that is live on ordinary data. homology.py:248-249 rebinds `data_matrix = data_matrix / _hg`, homology.py:262 multiplies the barcode back to ORIGINAL units, and then homology.py:291 computes `pairwise = pdist(data_matrix)` on the still-RESCALED array. Every `*_norm` output is therefore an original-unit lifetime divided by a rescaled-unit mean pairwise distance, i.e. inflated by exactly `_hg`. Measured on real gpt2 through the real evaluate(): the array pdist receives has max |X| = 1.0 on all 3 layers; _hg = 23.782059 / 160.05917 / 191.996 for layers 0/6/11, and true_mpd/reported_mpd = 23.782059 / 160.05917 / 191.996 to rel 4.1e-09. last_layer_mean_persistence_h0_norm ships as 140.32543 where the correct value is 63.91838/87.45438 = 0.73087684 -- and 0.7309 is precisely the number this file's own comment at homology.py:143 records for that quantity. The output is internally impossible: it reports mean H0 bar 63.91838 with mean_pairwise_distance 0.45550105, a ratio of 140.3 for a statistic bounded by the cloud diameter.

- GitHub (YES): scikit-tda/ripser.py cross-checked against GUDHI (GUDHI/gudhi-devel) — Ran GUDHI's RipsComplex/SimplexTree in float64 as an independent oracle on the identical 35x6 point cloud at 2^-100, 2^-40, 2^0, 2^+40, 2^+100. BLME's rescale-then-restore path matches GUDHI to rel 7.8e-08 at every scale including the two where raw ripser silently returns an empty barcode (0.0 mean H0, 0 H1 bars). This confirms the barcode itself is now correct; the defect is downstream, in what the barcode is divided by.
- Paper (PARTIAL): No source paper publishes a persistent-homology lifespan for a named released checkpoint that this task should reproduce. The available anchor is the file's OWN recorded value, and it now fails it: homology.py:143 records gpt2 mean_persistence_h0_norm = 0.7309 for the deepest layer, the shipped code returns 140.32543, and dividing by the measured _hg = 191.996 recovers 0.73087684 -- a 192x regression against the file's own reference point.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): The `*_norm` columns are now a direct function of max|activation| per layer, an architecture- and family-dependent quantity: measured 23.782059 / 160.05917 / 191.996 across three layers of a single gpt2 (an 8.1x within-model spread). Since gpt2-family models are known for large activation outliers whose magnitude varies strongly with family and scale, these columns now measure outlier magnitude at least as much as topology. The unnormalized siblings are separately confounded by representation scale, which is why the `_norm` twins existed.
- DEFECT: CODE/UNITS, VALUE-CHANGING, LIVE ON ORDINARY DATA: homology.py:291 `pairwise = pdist(data_matrix)` runs on the rescaled array while `dgms` was restored to original units at homology.py:262. Every `*_norm` output is inflated by exactly _hg. gpt2 measured: layer 0 shipped 13.293047 vs correct 0.55895273 (x23.782059); layer 6 shipped 75.388186 vs correct 0.47100197 (x160.05917); layer 11 shipped 140.32543 vs correct 0.73087684 (x191.996). Instrumenting scipy.spatial.distance.pdist shows max|X| = 1.0 on all three calls, proving pdist saw the rescaled cloud.
- DEFECT: SAME BUG SILENTLY ZEROES 8 MORE EMITTED COLUMNS: `_scale_stratified_summary(dgms, mpd)` at homology.py:299 compares original-unit deaths against `thresh = 0.5 * mpd` in rescaled units, so no bar is ever classified 'fine'. Measured on gpt2: h0_fine_count = 0 and h0_fine_persistence_frac = 0.0 on ALL three layers while h0_coarse_count = 19 on all three; h1_fine_count = 0 and h1_fine_persistence_frac = 0.0 throughout. The comment at homology.py:305-308 attributes the h1 zero to '20 mean-pooled points in R^768 cannot support a fine-scale H1 statistic', which is now the wrong diagnosis for a units bug.
- DEFECT: `_meta_layer_*_mean_pairwise_distance` IS EMITTED IN THE WRONG UNITS: reported 0.4794196652589198 / 0.25782019211702567 / 0.45550104641690564 for gpt2 layers 0/6/11 against true 11.401587 / 41.266487 / 87.45438. A reader has no way to detect this except by noticing that the reported mean H0 bar exceeds the reported mean pairwise distance by 140x, which is geometrically impossible.
- DEFECT: NOT A DEFECT, RECORDED FOR BALANCE: the ripser rescale itself is correct and materially improves the task -- it recovers 9 H1 bars and the exact GUDHI lifetimes at 2^-100 and 2^+100 where raw ripser silently returned an empty barcode. The regression is only in what the restored barcode is divided by.
- FIX: Keep the barcode restoration and compute `mpd` in the SAME units. Either move the pdist to before the rescale (retain the original array, e.g. `pairwise = pdist(data_matrix_raw)`), or leave pdist where it is and multiply by `_hg`: `mpd = float(pdist(data_matrix).mean()) * _hg`. Both make every `*_norm` output and `_meta_layer_*_mean_pairwise_distance` scale-invariant again and restore the file's own recorded gpt2 anchor of 0.7309 for last_layer_mean_persistence_h0_norm. Add an assertion that mean_persistence_h0_norm <= 1 for a connected cloud (an H0 bar cannot exceed the diameter), which would have caught this immediately, and re-check the `h0_fine_count`/`h1_fine_count` columns once the threshold is in matching units before concluding they are structurally zero.

### `topology_persistence_entropy`

**codex — RED** · Atienza et al. define PE = -sum_i (l_i/L) log2(l_i/L), and a common translation leaves every Vietoris-Rips distance and lifespan unchanged. Through the real evaluate() path, a finite radius-1e-5 circle fixture gives 19 H0 bars and 4.247892868881332 bits at offset 0 but 0 bars and 0.0 bits at +1e3 and +1e6, while an independent distance-matrix evaluation retains 19 bars and 4.247927513443583/4.247927513355573 bits (100% loss), so the end-to-end implementation is not faithful.

- GitHub (YES): giotto-ai/giotto-tda; GUDHI/gudhi-devel; scikit-tda/persim — I fetched giotto-tda 0.6.2 (wheel SHA256 63066e3ab703b0ba6a49e670c2695b087b079674fbe805e584ec6967a8ff1c71) and executed its exact scipy.stats.entropy(base=2) method body, plus installed GUDHI 3.12.0 Entropy and persim 0.3.8 persistent_entropy, on the same 300 diagrams of 2-59 bars. After converting the natural-log references to bits, each implementation's maximum absolute disagreement with BLME was 1.7763568394002505e-15; GUDHI cites Atienza et al. and credits coauthor Manuel Soriano-Trigueros's implementation.
- Paper (YES): Chintakunta et al. publish E(F2)=1.474560793 and E(F3)=1.459622430 nats. Independently reducing their stated filtrations gives lifespan multisets [18,1,1,13,1,1,1,1,1,1] and [18,1,1,1,1,1,1,1,3,1]; BLME gives 1.4745607925621864 and 1.4596224331542222 nats, relative errors 2.969111651818254e-10 and 2.160985027306146e-09.
- Confound (CONFIRMED): Across the controlled 100-run width sweep, d has rho=0.9798448905826678 with both raw and normalized H0 (p=2.310e-70), rho=0.7929394777040238 with raw H1 (p=8.259e-23), and rho=0.5695348426511756 with normalized H1 (p=6.263e-10). In 61 locally resolved archived model configs, layer-0 H0 also has rho=-0.3135212561 with vocabulary size (p=0.01387817), although raw H1 has |rho|<=0.072017 and normalized H1 has |rho|<=0.140445 against d_model, layers, heads, vocabulary, and parameter count; the controlled width mechanism makes the architecture confound CONFIRMED despite mostly weak observational H1 associations.
- DEFECT: CODE/FIDELITY, persistence_entropy.py:159-184: hidden.mean(dim=0).float() unconditionally narrows a collected representation before any centering, and the later magnitude-only rescale cannot recover geometry already erased. On the real evaluator's finite radius-1e-5 circle, offsets 0/+1e3/+1e6 produce 19/0/0 finite H0 bars and PE 4.247892868881332/0.0/0.0, while float64 pairwise-distance oracles preserve 19 bars and PE 4.247927513443585/4.247927513443583/4.247927513355573. Centering before narrowing restores 19 bars and 4.247927513443582/4.2479275134435825/4.247927513355573, directly confirming the cause and fix.
- DEFECT: CODE/API, persistence_entropy.py:65-71: _persistence_entropy([1e308,1e308]) sums before factoring out the maximum, overflows to inf, and returns 0.0 instead of exactly 1.0 bit. This is unreachable through the current evaluator after point-cloud rescaling, but the callable helper still violates its formula on two finite positive lifespans.
- DEFECT: CODE/NUMERIC, persistence_entropy.py:73-79: the p>1e-15 filter deletes probability mass without renormalizing; [1,1e-16] returns 0.0 instead of the 100-digit result 5.4593544559086756e-15 bits. The ordinary 300-diagram parity maximum was only 1.7763568394002505e-15 bits, so this is a measured extreme-tail residual, not the basis of the RED verdict.
- DEFECT: CODE/BOUNDARY, persistence_entropy.py:53-54,220-226,230-234: fully collapsed clouds at N=3/20/50 have 0 finite H0 and 0 H1 bars but emit PE=0.0, normalized PE=0.0, and pe_simplification_ratio=1.0, conflating an undefined empty barcode with legal one-bar entropy and invented no-change. The helper also raises ValueError on np.array([1.0,2.0]) while the equivalent list returns 0.9182958340544896 bits.
- DEFECT: NORMALIZATION/COMPARABILITY, persistence_entropy.py:214-226: repeating the unchanged lifespan mixture [1,2] 1/2/10/100 times moves PE/log2(m) from 0.9182958340544896 to 0.9591479170272448/0.9810954360758193/0.9893111325073323. On the controlled width null, normalized H0 moves 0.9543031656183549 -> 0.9999538765116134 and normalized H1 0.8032958096366911 -> 0.9149205173180681 from d=2 -> 512, so normalized_ok remains RED.
- DEFECT: CONFIG/PROTOCOL, NOT VERDICT EVIDENCE: defaults.yaml resolves num_samples=1000, the study override resolves 50, and all 66 archived envelopes used 50; the code fallback 20 never fires. At study N=50, layer-0 H0 has exactly 49 bars on 66/66 models, raw PE 5.446678636399651-5.598659203743786 versus the 5.614709844115208 ceiling, and normalized PE 0.9700730380766387-0.9971413232710066 with 61/66 above 0.99. The defaults comment's claim that N=20 gives m=49 is arithmetically impossible: it gives at most 19 finite H0 bars.

On local GPT-2, the current raw-block ratio is 0.9981317808165503 versus 0.9814128328432689 under the incorrect post-final-norm convention.
- DEFECT: CLOSED/HOOK: persistence_entropy.py:127-165 now substitutes the deepest raw block and removes its hook in finally. On local GPT-2, hook counts were 0 before and 0 after; the deepest raw result was H0=4.236776110331549 and H1=0.9354748441632147 with 2 bars, versus post-final-norm H0=4.165809089017864 and H1=0.957519430073571 with 2 bars. The focused topology/reference/declaration suite passed 34/34 tests.
